Built `packages/deploy` — the edge-worker execution half of the C2/C3.2/C4.4 deploy targets, pure/deterministic string transforms (Architecture §1 Layer 4 "Edge Workers"): `html.ts` (`applySchemaDiff`/`applyMetaDiff`/`applyHtmlActions` — insert-or-replace JSON-LD, `<title>`, `<meta name="description">`, HTML-escaped), `robots.ts` (`applyRobotsActions` — latest deployed robots action wins, since `unblockCrawlers`'s diff is already a complete file), `verify.ts` (`verifyHtmlDeploy`/`verifyRobotsDeploy` — confirm a deployed diff actually landed before the Fix Queue moves to `verified`). - Scaffolded **`apps/workers`** — the actual Cloudflare Worker for the `edge-worker` `DeployTarget`: reverse-proxies a customer origin, reads every `deployed`/`verified` Action for the project live from Postgres on each request (`repositories/deployedActions.ts`, joined through `findings.evidence->>'url'` for page scoping), and rewrites the response via `@engine/deploy`. Deploy/rollback are pure DB status flips in `apps/api` — no separate push step to the worker.
- Wired the **Fix Queue lifecycle into `apps/api`**: `repositories/actions.ts` (create/get/update against the existing `actions` table), and `POST /projects/:id/actions/{approve,deploy,rollback,verify}` driving `@engine/actions`' guarded `transition()` state machine (illegal moves → 409). `/actions/generate` now persists its output instead of returning ephemeral objects. `/verify` takes caller-supplied post-deploy content (rendered HTML or robots.txt, same out-of-band-fetch pattern as `/audit`/`/pulse`) and checks it against the diff via `@engine/deploy` before allowing the transition.

All 20 turbo tasks (typecheck/build/test across 9 workspace packages, `@engine/deploy` and `@engine/workers` now included) green. [PR #13](https://github.com/thenameisab/engine/pull/13) — merged.
- **Toward M1.5 ("rollback proven").** Checked PR #13's merge status first (per the standing rule) and branched fresh off `main`. Built the **C1.7 "automatic rollback"** signal: `packages/deploy/src/health.ts` — pure, deterministic checks the edge-worker runs on every request before serving a transform: `checkHtmlDeployHealth` (origin 5xx first, then a page-shrunk-below-50%-of-original heuristic to catch a transform that mangled the page, then JSON-LD re-validation), `checkJsonLdValidity`, `checkRobotsDeployHealth` (non-empty, contains a `User-agent:` line). 9 new unit tests (21 total in `@engine/deploy`).
- Wired **fail-safe serving + auto-rollback into `apps/workers`**: the fetch handler now health-checks every HTML/robots.txt transform before returning it; on failure it serves the *untransformed origin response* to the visitor (never ships a broken fix) and `ctx.waitUntil`s a background `POST /projects/:id/actions/:actionId/rollback` per offending action with `actor: 'system:edge-worker-health-check'` and the failure reason in the audit detail — self-healing with no human in the loop, per C1.7's "staged rollout + automatic revert." Extended `apps/api`'s transition endpoints (`approve`/`deploy`/`rollback`) to accept an optional `detail` object so this reason lands in the immutable audit log (C1.8).
- **Staged the M1.5 rollback test** the same way M1.4 was verified — a scratch script (not committed) simulating a `deployed` schema action with deliberately corrupt JSON-LD: confirmed `checkHtmlDeployHealth` catches it (`reason: 'invalid-json-ld'`), the worker's fail-safe path serves the original page (broken schema never reaches a visitor), a *healthy* deploy is correctly left alone (not a false-positive always-fail), and the resulting `transition(..., 'rolled_back', ..., { reason })` call produces the expected audit entry. Same known gap as M1.4: no live Postgres/Wrangler in this sandbox, so the real HTTP round-trip between worker and API isn't integration-tested. - Built **`packages/billing`** — pure, dependency-light Stripe logic, portable to both Cloudflare Workers and Node (Web Crypto, no Stripe SDK): `signature.ts` (`verifyStripeSignature` — reimplements Stripe's documented HMAC-SHA256 webhook-signature scheme with replay-tolerance and constant-time comparison; unit-tested against signatures computed independently via `node:crypto` as a cross-check, not just self-consistency), `plans.ts` (`PLAN_LIMITS`/`isOverLimit` — G1/G5; only Starter's caps are blueprint-pinned, Growth/Agency/Enterprise are flagged as provisional placeholders pending real pricing sign-off), `stripeEvents.ts` (`mapStripeSubscriptionEvent` — pure mapper from a `customer.subscription.*` webhook event to our `Subscription` shape, keyed by an `accountId` the caller must stash in Stripe metadata at checkout). 15 new unit tests, all passing — this package is the one piece of M1.6/M1.7 that's genuinely fully tested, since signature verification is just crypto and needs no live Stripe account.
- Added `infra/migrations/postgres/0002_onboarding_billing.sql`: `onboarding_progress` (one row per project, mirrors `OnboardingProgress`) and `subscriptions` (one row per account, mirrors `Subscription`, Stripe as source of truth).

- **Wrote 28 tests against real Chromium + a real local `node:http` server** (`testServer.ts`, test-only, excluded from the build) — deliberately not `page.setContent`, since crawlPage needs real HTTP status/headers/redirects. This caught 4 real bugs before they shipped: (1) `@graph` children were failing validation because `@context` isn't repeated on graph members, only inherited from the container — fixed by propagating it down before validating; (2) a robots.txt `Sitemap:` directive given as a relative path wasn't resolved against origin, silently fetching nothing — fixed by resolving every declared sitemap URL through `new URL(d, origin)`; (3) the test server wasn't sending a `charset=utf-8` content-type header, so the crawled page's em-dash title came back mojibake'd (`â€”`) — a genuine reminder that missing charset headers break real crawls, not just a test artifact; (4) one of my own test's expected values for `normalizeUrl` contradicted the function's documented behavior — fixed the test, not the code.

- Built **`packages/auth`** — Web Crypto only, no SDK, identical on `workerd` and Node. `jwt.ts`: `verifyJwt` (signature + exp/nbf/iss/aud + skew leeway), `bearerToken`, `decodeJwtUnsafe` (named loudly so misuse is deliberate). Accepted algs are an **allowlist** — `alg` is attacker-controlled, so `alg:none` dies before a key lookup; a token with no `exp` is rejected rather than treated as eternal. `jwks.ts`: `createRemoteJwks` with TTL cache, rotation healing (unknown `kid` → refetch) that is **rate-limited + single-flighted** so junk `kid`s can't amplify outbound traffic. `serviceToken.ts`: constant-time compare. **25 tests against real generated Ed25519/RSA keypairs** (`testTokens.ts`, build-excluded per the crawler's `testServer.ts` precedent) — tampered payloads, `alg:none`, a foreign key lying about a trusted `kid`, expiry boundaries, rotation, refetch rate limit.

Added `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN` (shared service token, kept separate from the JWT path so the trust sources don't blur), and made the worker **log** a failed rollback instead of swallowing it (`fetch` doesn't throw on 4xx).

## 2026-07-16 (cont.)
- **Cleared the blocker the auth session surfaced: `infra/migrations/` had never been applied to Neon.** DB-backed routes weren't failing because of auth — the schema simply wasn't there (`relation "actions" does not exist`). Built **`packages/db`**, a migration runner, and applied both migrations to the live Neon DB (user-approved). All 8 tables + a `schema_migrations` ledger now exist; verified `actions` is queryable and 7 FKs landed.
- **Runner design — the rules are the point, not the file-walking.** Applied migrations are **checksummed and immutable** (editing one that ran = drift, aborts: the DB can no longer be re-derived from the tree). Filenames are strictly `NNNN_snake_case.sql` — a bad name is *rejected*, not skipped, because a migration that never runs is worse than one that fails loudly. New migrations must sort **above** every applied one, else the schema is one no fresh `migrate` could reproduce. A DB ahead of the checkout aborts rather than guessing. **All pending migrations run in one transaction** under a **transaction-scoped** advisory lock — Neon's pooled endpoint is a transaction-mode pooler where session-scoped locks aren't reliably held by the same backend; the tradeoff (no `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` through this runner) is documented, since a half-applied schema is the worse failure. Re-reads under the lock so a concurrent runner can't double-apply. `DATABASE_URL` falls back to `apps/api/.dev.vars` (same file `wrangler dev` reads); connection strings are never logged (host/db only). 15 unit tests on the pure planner (drift, out-of-order, duplicate, DB-ahead, filename rejection).
- **Found and fixed a real data-corruption bug the live run surfaced (`apps/api/src/repositories/actions.ts`).** `${JSON.stringify(v)}::jsonb` **double-encodes**: the cast makes Postgres infer the param as jsonb, so the driver re-encodes the already-encoded string and the column stores a jsonb **string**. It round-trips through its own repository looking correct — which is why no unit test with a fake driver would ever catch it — but `target->>'kind'` is `null`, `jsonb_array_length(audit_log)` **errors**, and every jsonb predicate silently matches nothing. This would have quietly broken any Fix Queue query filtering on `target`/`diff`/`status` history. Fixed via a documented `toJsonb()` helper in `apps/api/src/db.ts` (postgres.js' `JSONValue` demands index signatures our domain types deliberately don't carry). Proved it side-by-side on the live DB: pre-fix row `jsonb_typeof(target)=string, ->>'kind'=null`; post-fix `object, ->>'kind'=edge-worker`. `actions` was the only site of the idiom repo-wide.
- **Regression guard, honestly scoped:** a source-level test (`apps/api/src/repositories/jsonb.test.ts`) — behavioural coverage is impossible here since CI has no Postgres and the bug only misbehaves against a real one. Verified it *fails* on the reintroduced idiom (naming the offending file), not just passes. Added vitest to `apps/api`.
- **Verified on the real Workers runtime**, not just vitest: drove `wrangler dev` against migrated Neon — entity POST/GET round-trips through real Postgres, `actions/generate` persists (the exact route that threw last session), `approve` transitions and appends an audit entry under the *verified* identity. Unknown project returns `{"entities":[]}`, not a 500. Runner is idempotent (re-run → "Already up to date"). All green: 21 test tasks, 23 typecheck, 14 build.
- Documented the migration runbook + both rules (immutability, `toJsonb`) in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §2.3.1; added `packages/db` to the repo layout.

## 2026-07-16 (cont. 2)
- **Confirmed PR #22 merged**, synced `main`, no open PRs. Reviewed the roadmap and picked the item PR #22 unblocked: with the schema finally applied, the Fix Queue could stop being theatre.
- **Found the loop's last broken link.** The dashboard kanban rendered nine hardcoded `MOCK_ACTIONS` (`a1`…`a9`) while `transitionAction` POSTed to the *real* API — so "Approve" on `a1` hit Postgres for an id that never existed, and the failure was swallowed by a `catch` that reported "(sample — no live DB)". There was also no `GET /projects/:id/actions` at all: `actions/generate` persisted rows nothing could ever read back. Chain was generate → persist → **(gap)** → transition.
- **A live probe (read-only) found worse than the code review did:** the API has no findings repository and `/audit` never persisted, yet `actions.finding_id` is a uuid FK — so the one findings row in Neon had been hand-inserted last session, and `actions/generate` driven from a *real* audit would have died on an FK violation. Findings persistence was the actual missing link, not the list endpoint.
- Built **migration `0003_findings_fingerprint`** + `apps/api/src/repositories/findings.ts`. Diagnosis mints a deterministic `fnd_<fnv1a>` id per (entity, url, issue-type); the DB uses uuid PKs. Stored the diagnosis id as `fingerprint` (unique per entity) and made `/audit` **upsert** on it, so a re-crawl refreshes scores in place rather than duplicating findings and re-proposing fixes. `created_at` is untouched on conflict — it keeps meaning "first seen". The returned Finding carries its **database uuid**, which is what makes the FK to Actions hold.
- **Added a tenancy check to `/audit`:** `entityId` is caller-supplied, so pages are rejected (400) unless their entities belong to the project — otherwise one project could hang findings off another's entity, and the FK failure would surface as a 500 anyway.
- Added **`GET /projects/:id/actions`** — a two-hop `actions → findings → entities` join, deliberately *not* a denormalized `project_id` column (the entity-first model is the sequencing rule that can't be retrofitted). Projects `predictedImpact` from the finding, since a queue card is unrankable without it.
- **Wired the dashboard to it and deleted `MOCK_ACTIONS`.** The board now distinguishes an **empty queue** ("No actions yet") from an **unreachable API** ("Could not load the queue: …") — inventing cards for both is worse than saying which happened. A failed transition no longer moves the card: it reports the error and re-enables the button, because a card that moves on a rejected write is a lie the user finds on reload.
- **Driving it on the real runtime caught a bug vitest never could:** `predictedImpact` is `severityWeight × pageValue` — a **0–1** score — but the card renders `+N impact` on a 0–10 scale (the mocks used integers). The first live card read **"+0.855 impact"**. Added `impactPoints()` (rescale + clamp) in `format.ts` with tests, rather than bending the frozen Finding contract. Also moved the card mapping out of `api.ts` into `format.ts`, where the house pattern keeps pure presentation logic unit-tested.
- **Applied `0003` to live Neon (user-approved):** dry-run clean → applied → `3 applied, 0 pending` → re-run "Already up to date". Backfilled the one legacy row as `legacy_<id>`.
- **Deleted the corrupt leftover row (user-approved):** action `ed272799…` still had `jsonb_typeof(target)='string'` — the pre-fix specimen from last session's jsonb proof, which would have rendered as a broken card and silently mis-matched every jsonb predicate. Showed the row first, deleted only that id. `jsonb_array_length(audit_log)` now succeeds on every remaining row.
- **Verified end-to-end on `wrangler dev` against migrated Neon**, not just vitest: foreign entity → **400** (not an FK 500); real audit → 5 findings with real uuids; **re-run → identical uuids, no duplicates, `created_at` preserved**; generate → a persisted robots.txt diff (`Disallow: /` → `Allow: /`) from a genuine finding; `GET /actions` → the joined queue; unknown project → `{"actions":[]}` 200, not a 500.
- **Browser-verified the whole loop**: Fix Queue rendered the two real Postgres rows (ROBOTS "+9 impact" — the rescale landing correctly), clicking **Deploy** moved the card and wrote through to Neon (`status=deployed`, 3 audit entries), with the audit trail recording the **verified** identity (`dev@engine.local`) rather than the caller-supplied `dashboard:internal` — C1.8 holding. Empty-project and dead-API states both confirmed. Zero console errors. All 35 turbo tasks green (20 dashboard tests).
- Documented the three persistence rules (persist-before-generate, fingerprint identity swap, no `actions.project_id`) in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.1.

## 2026-07-17
- **Confirmed PR #23 merged**, synced `main` (`58add7b`), no open PRs, tree clean. Reviewed the roadmap and traced the loop for the next real gap.
- **Found that the crawl runner has been unable to reach its own API since PR #21.** `reportCrawlToApi` POSTs to `/projects/:id/audit`, PR #21 gated `/projects/*`, and the reporter sends no `Authorization` header — every crawl died on `401 missing bearer token`. Since the crawler is the *only* path that puts real findings into a project, a deployed Engine could not acquire a finding at all: crawl → **(401)** → audit → findings → Fix Queue. Same shape as the auto-rollback break PR #21 caught for `apps/workers`, but this caller was missed.
- **CI could never have caught it**: `report.test.ts` injects a mocked `fetchImpl`, so it tests the reporter's shape and is structurally blind to a gate. Second time a mocked-`fetch` test hid a bug only the real runtime shows (connectors' "Illegal invocation" was the first).
- **Reproduced on the real runtime before touching code** — `wrangler dev` with auth enabled → `POST /audit` → `401`. (Two red herrings first: a transient Neon `CONNECT_TIMEOUT` on cold start, and my own hand-written payloads — `proj_demo` isn't a uuid, and my `ActionContext` was missing `target`/using `robotsTxt` instead of `currentRobotsTxt`. Neither was a product bug; verified Neon was reachable from Node independently.)
- **Fixed**: `reportCrawlToApi` takes a `token` and sends it as a bearer credential; the CLI reads it from **`ENGINE_API_TOKEN` in the env, not a flag** (argv is `ps`-readable and lands in shell history), warns *before* spending browser time when it's absent, and a tokenless 401/403 now names its own cause instead of surfacing a bare status.
- **Renamed the service principal `service:edge-worker` → `service:internal`.** Adding the crawler as a second holder of the shared token is what made the old label a lie — a shared secret proves *a* trusted machine is calling, not which one, so naming one asserted something never verified (the same flaw C1.8 removed from the human path). Self-labelling via `actor` still works and is now documented as a self-report from inside the trust boundary rather than an identity. Nothing referenced the old string — it was entirely untested.
- **Verified on the real gated runtime**: drove the actual `engine-crawl` binary against `wrangler dev` + migrated Neon, using the crawler's own test server as the crawl target. Tokenless → 401; wrong token → 403 `malformed`; valid token → a real 2-page Playwright crawl persisted **3 findings with real uuids** (correctly catching the test site's `GPTBot` block). **Re-runs returned identical uuids with `created_at` preserved** — the PR #23 fingerprint upsert holding under a real crawl. Closed the loop: a crawl-produced finding generated a real robots diff (`GPTBot: Disallow: /` → `Allow: /`); audit trail recorded `service:internal` unlabelled and `service:crawl-runner` when self-labelled.
- **Proved the regression guard is real**: removed the `Authorization` header again and confirmed the new test *fails* naming the exact behaviour, then restored it. All 40 turbo tasks green (`@engine/crawler` 28 → 32 tests).
- Added `packages/crawler/README.md` (it had none — part of how the runner drifted out of sync with the gate it depends on), documented both machine callers in `docs/40-Integrations.md`, and gitignored `.wrangler/` (untracked *and* unignored; holds a local copy of the dev bindings).

## 2026-07-17 (cont.)
- **Fixed two 500s that should have been 400s** (`apps/api`): `POST /projects/:id/audit` and `POST /projects/:id/actions/generate` both did `await c.req.json<T>()` and trusted the result. The generic is a compile-time fiction about untrusted wire bytes — a `CrawledPage` missing `metaDescription` threw `TypeError: … reading 'trim'` from inside `checkMeta`, and an `ActionContext` missing `target` reached postgres.js as `UNDEFINED_VALUE`. Both surfaced as opaque 500s naming neither the field nor the caller's mistake.
- **Added `apps/api/src/validate.ts` — vanilla, no schema library.** Checked first: the repo carries none (deps are Hono + postgres.js). Two request bodies and a "name the first bad field" check don't earn a dependency in a deliberately lean repo. Validators return `{field, message}` or null, and each covers **exactly** the fields its route dereferences or persists — requiring the rest of the `Finding` contract (`severity`, `createdAt`, …) would reject bodies that work fine. Routes now answer `400 {error, field}` naming the JSON path (`pages[2].vitals.lcpMs`).
- **Made the untyped read the only read:** `readJson()` returns `unknown` (not a caller-asserted `T`), so the sole route to a typed body is through a validator. It also catches unparseable JSON, which was itself an uncaught 500.
- **Required all four `aiCrawlerAccess` verdicts** even though a missing key doesn't throw: it reads back `undefined`, compares unequal to `'blocked'`, and the crawler is **silently audited as allowed** — a false-negative B1.6 finding, which is worse than a 500 because nobody finds out.
- **The live run found what the reported bug didn't.** With shape validation in, `/actions/generate` still 500'd on a *shape-valid* `finding.id`: a non-uuid string → `invalid input syntax for type uuid`, and a well-formed-but-absent uuid → FK violation. Worse, the route had **no tenancy check at all** — one project could hang an Action off another project's finding, the same cross-tenant hole `/audit`'s entity check already closes. Added `findingBelongsToProject()` (the same `findings → entities → project_id` hop `listActionsByProject` reads through) and a 400. Compares `f.id::text` deliberately: an uncast uuid literal 500s on a malformed id *before* it can simply not match.
- **Verified on the real Workers runtime against live Neon, before *and* after.** Stashed the fix and reproduced both reported 500s on `wrangler dev` (logs confirm `TypeError: … 'trim'` and `UNDEFINED_VALUE`), then restored: same payloads → 400 naming `pages[0].metaDescription` / `context.target`. No regression on the valid path — a real crawl still ran the rule engine and persisted 4 findings with real uuids, and generate still wrote a genuine `Disallow: /` → `Allow: /` robots diff. Confirmed `title: ""` stays **valid** (an empty string is a real `meta-title-missing` signal; only an absent field is a contract violation) — the distinction strict validation could easily have broken.
- 57 tests in `apps/api` (was 1): validators against a fixture copied from `crawlPage`'s actual output, plus route-level tests through the real Hono stack with an unreachable `DATABASE_URL` — proving the 400 lands before `createDb`. All 40 turbo tasks green.
- **This bug had already cost a session.** The PR #24 entry above logs, as a dismissed red herring, "my `ActionContext` was missing `target`… Neither was a product bug." The missing `target` *was* the product bug — the 500 just refused to say so, so it read as the author's own payload error. Exactly the debugging tax an opaque 500 charges the next person.

## 2026-07-17 (cont.)
- **Confirmed PR #24 and PR #25 merged**, synced `main` (`917df9c`), no open PRs. PR #25 was the validation follow-up I flagged last session, run as an independent task; reviewed what it landed (`validate.ts`, `readJson`, `findingBelongsToProject`) so this work builds on it rather than around it.
- **Traced the loop for the next gap and found the Audit view was the last mock-only surface** — and the only view that never even attempted a live call. It rendered `MOCK_AUDIT`: health score 72 and five invented findings ("12-hop redirect chain on /pricing") for a site nobody had crawled.
- **The reason it couldn't be un-mocked was two real data-model gaps, not laziness:**
  1. **A persisted finding could not say what was wrong.** `packages/diagnosis` used the issue type for the severity weight, the action templates and the fingerprint — then *dropped* it. Absent from the `Finding` contract and the `findings` table. Unrecoverable after the fact: FNV-1a is one-way, and inferring the type from `actionTemplates` is lossy (`schema-missing`/`schema-invalid` share one template, as do the two `meta-*` types) — so inference would have shown distinct problems as the same one.
  2. **Findings were write-only** — `upsertFindings` persisted them and nothing could read them back. Exactly the gap PR #23 closed for actions, one layer down.
- **Added `Finding.issueType`** (`packages/core`), set by `runAudit`, persisted by migration `0004`. A `string`, not a union: each Pillar B source owns its vocabulary and core can't import from packages that depend on it; `source` says which vocabulary applies. Required rather than optional — optional would let the gap return silently. No schema `check` constraint either: pinning B1's list into SQL means a migration every time the rule engine learns a check.
- **Found the health score has no home.** §8 normalizes it by pages audited, so it's a property of a *run*, and crawled pages are never persisted — it cannot be recomputed from findings later. Added an **`audit_runs`** table (0004); `/audit` records one per crawl and `GET /audit` reports the newest. Returns **null** for a never-crawled project rather than a default that reads as a clean bill of health.
- Added **`GET /projects/:id/audit`** + `listFindingsByProject` (two-hop `findings → entities`, same reason as `listActionsByProject`: no denormalized `project_id`). Rewrote the Audit view against it, deleted `MOCK_AUDIT`, and made it distinguish empty / never-crawled / unreachable — the Fix Queue's rule from PR #23.
- **Caught my own bad assumption:** I claimed making `issueType` required would break the fixtures at compile time. It didn't — every package's tsconfig has `"exclude": ["src/**/*.test.ts"]`, so test files are never typechecked and fixtures silently built Findings without it. Fixed the three fixtures by hand and noted the exclusion as a standing gap.
- **Applied migration 0004 to live Neon (user-approved):** 4 applied, 0 pending; 13 legacy rows backfilled `unknown`, `audit_runs` created.
- **Proved the self-heal instead of asserting it.** The first re-crawl *didn't* heal the legacy rows — my test server had picked a new random port, and the URL is part of the fingerprint, so those were genuinely different findings (harness artifact, not a bug). Reset this session's crawled rows to `unknown` to reproduce the post-backfill state, re-crawled, and confirmed they returned to real types **in place**: 16 rows before, 16 after, no duplicates.
- **Verified on the real runtime**: `engine-crawl` → gated `wrangler dev` → migrated Neon. A real 2-page crawl recorded an `audit_runs` row (health 35, 3 findings) and persisted real issue types; `GET /audit` before any run → `healthScore: null`, not 100; unknown project → empty inventory, HTTP 200, not a 500.
- **Browser-verified all three states**, both themes, zero console errors: populated (health 35 across 2 pages, `ai-crawler-blocked` rows carrying the crawl's real URLs, impact rescaled to the card's 0–10), never-crawled ("No audit has run for this project yet"), and unreachable — where the 401 is reported as itself instead of the old fabricated 72. The legacy rows honestly render "Issue type not recorded (pre-0004 finding)".
- All 40 turbo tasks green (`@engine/diagnosis` +2, `@engine/dashboard` format +6). Documented both new persistence rules in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.1.

Both routes now check the entity belongs to the project **before** spending SERP/LLM credit, same reasoning as `/audit`'s and `/actions/generate`'s existing tenancy checks.
- **`assembleSurfaceScores`** (`pulseRollup.ts`) is the rollup `POST /pulse`'s doc comment always said was coming: reads each entity's latest position per keyword and its citation events, feeds them through the *existing, already-correct* pure scoring math (`organicSov`/`aiSov`/`citationBandFromSamples` in `@engine/scoring` — none of it needed to change). Local (B5) has no data source yet, so it's excluded from the channel mix at **weight 0** rather than scored as 0 — scoring it 0 would assert "no local visibility," a different claim than "not measured," and `unifiedVisibilityScore`'s `normalize()` already renormalizes correctly when a weight is 0.
- **Added `GET /projects/:id/pulse`**, DB-backed, alongside the existing caller-supplied `POST /pulse` (kept for pure-math testing). Returns `score: null` for a project with nothing polled — not a 0, which would read as "zero visibility" — plus `aiBand` (the AI surface's own confidence band) so the dashboard's AI tile can honor Architecture §3.2 ("a point estimate is never surfaced for AI visibility") independent of the blended score's decomposition, which only carries a point.

Cross-tenant `entityId` on both poll routes → 400 naming the field, before any credit was spent. A real Serper call with `entityId` persisted a `serp_positions` row (position 1, `crm software`); the same call without `entityId` did not persist — confirmed by querying Postgres directly. `/pulse` moved from `score: null` → a real band as soon as that row existed. No OpenAI/Gemini key is wired in this environment, so `insertCitationEvents` itself couldn't be exercised through the live route; proved the rollup's read side instead by inserting rows through the exact schema the function targets, confirming `aiSov`'s Wilson-interval math renders correctly, then deleted those synthetic rows (they didn't come from a real poll and would have misled a future session).

Dropped `deltaVsPrior`/`trend`/`wins`/`risks` rather than fake them — those need historical snapshots and derived signal events this session didn't build, and inventing three "risks" for a site nobody polled is exactly the failure this pass exists to remove. The empty-vs-unreachable distinction from the Fix Queue/Audit views now applies here too.

Zero console errors in all three.

## 2026-07-17 (cont. 4)
- **Branched `feat/a4-keyword-research` off `main`** (not off the still-unmerged data-spine branch) specifically to avoid a stacked PR — A4 doesn't depend on migration 0005's tables, so it could land and merge independently in either order.
- **Scoped A4 honestly against what's actually buildable today.** The full spec calls for self-hosted embeddings + HDBSCAN clustering, IndicBERT/MuRIL, an LLM-gateway-routed prompt expander, and a managed keyword-volume API (DataForSEO-class) — none of which this environment has. Built the slice that needs none of it: **`packages/keywords`** — `classifyIntent` (A4.3, rule-based: transactional/commercial/navigational/informational, checked most-specific-first so "buy running shoes" doesn't fall through to informational on its question-shaped neighbors), `generatePromptSeeds` (A4.8, template-based, intent-diverse), and `hasAiOverlap` (A4.9, flags a SERP feature set carrying `ai_overview`/`ai_mode` — mirrors `SerpFeature` in `@engine/connectors` by value rather than importing it, keeping the package a dependency-free leaf like `@engine/scoring`).
- **`transliterateToDevanagari` (A4.7)** is a real deterministic ITRANS-style transliterator, not a stub: greedy longest-match tokenizer over consonant digraphs/single consonants and vowel matras/independent forms, with halant insertion for mid-word consonant clusters. Verified against real words during development, not assumed: `ghar`→घर, `dost`→दोस्त (exercises the halant path), `kaise`→कैसे. Caught and fixed a real bug this way — `loan` was decomposing into लोअन (an extra vowel) because "oa" wasn't recognized as a single English-loanword vowel sound; added `oa`/`ow` digraphs and it now renders लोन. Documented the scheme's short/long-a convention explicitly (`a` = inherent/short, `aa`/`A` = long आ) rather than claiming dictionary-perfect Hindi — the spec's own risk register already names transliteration accuracy as a known, later-invested-in gap (IndicBERT/MuRIL).
- **Closed a second real gap while wiring A4 into the API: nothing ever created a `keyword_configs` row.** `apps/api/src/repositories/billing.ts` already counts them toward a plan's tracked-keyword limit (migration 0001), so that count was permanently zero. Added `createKeywordConfig`/`listKeywordConfigsByEntity` and `POST`/`GET /projects/:id/entities/:id/keywords` — the A4 spec's "one-click push into A1 tracking" (§4.8), and the missing other half of billing's usage counter.
- **Added `checkCreateKeywordConfigBody`** (`apps/api/src/validate.ts`): `device`/`engine`/`cadence` are `check` constraints in the table, and an unvalidated bad value would 500 as a Postgres constraint violation naming neither the field nor the allowed values — same class of bug PR #25/#26 fixed for `/audit` and `/actions/generate`. Both keyword-config routes carry the same pre-DB tenancy check as `/audit`: `entityId` is a path param, so it's checked against the project before any write.
- **`POST /keywords/research` is a stateless preview**, not a tracked write — results (intent, transliteration, prompt seeds) are returned for the caller to inspect before `POST .../keywords` commits one to tracking, deliberately separating "research" from "commit," which is what the spec's UX section calls for.
- **Verified on the real runtime**: `wrangler dev` against live Neon. `/keywords/research` on `["home loans", "ghar ka loan", "best crm software"]` returned real transliterations and correctly classified "best crm software" as commercial. Cross-tenant `entityId` on the create route → 400 before any write; a real create persisted a `keyword_configs` row, confirmed via `GET`; an invalid `device` → 400 naming the field, not a 500.

## 2026-07-17 (cont. 5)
- **Continuing Phase-1 closure** (data-spine/Pulse merged as PR #27; A4 shipped as PR #28, still open at the time this branch started). Branched `feat/cms-plugins` off `main` for C2's WordPress + Shopify plugins — `plugins/wordpress` and `plugins/shopify` were both empty directories despite being in the repo layout since the first scaffold commit.
- **The edge-worker deploy target's model doesn't transfer.** `apps/workers` re-derives its HTML transform from the live `deployed`/`verified` action set on every request — rollback is a status flip it picks up on the next request, no separate push. WordPress and Shopify have no equivalent of "serve the untransformed origin response," so a CMS plugin needs a genuinely different model: **pull `approved` actions once, apply them into the CMS's own storage, report the push, stop watching that action.** Rollback in this model leaves the write in place until the next audit re-detects the issue — a real, documented asymmetry, not an oversight.
- **Built the API side first, since it's what's actually testable/verifiable here:** `listPendingCmsPluginActions` (`apps/api/src/repositories/cmsPluginActions.ts`) mirrors `listLiveEdgeActions` in `apps/workers` (same `actions → findings → entities` join for the page URL) but filters `status = 'approved'` and `target->>'plugin'`/`target->>'siteId'`, not `deployed`/`verified`. New route: `GET /projects/:id/cms-plugin/actions?plugin=<wordpress|shopify>&siteId=<id>`. The existing `POST .../actions/:id/deploy` transition needed no changes — a plugin reports its push through the same endpoint every other deploy path already uses.
- **Verified the whole pull → deploy loop on `wrangler dev` against live Neon**, using the "Verification Project" sandbox: a real `/audit` call produced a genuine `schema-missing` finding, `/actions/generate` with `target: {kind:'cms-plugin', plugin:'wordpress', siteId:'test-site-1'}` created a real action, approving it made it appear in the queue scoped to that exact plugin+siteId (confirmed a wrong `siteId` and a wrong `plugin` both correctly returned empty), and calling `deploy` made it disappear from the pending queue — proving `approved`-only filtering holds without needing to fabricate a second scenario.
- **Then built the actual plugin artifacts as thin, honestly-scoped clients on top of that verified contract:**
  - **`plugins/wordpress/engine-seo.php`** — a real single-file WP plugin: WP-Cron pulls the queue hourly (+ a manual "Sync now" in Settings), resolves each action's page URL via `url_to_postid`, writes JSON-LD/meta into post meta, renders schema via `wp_head` and title via `pre_get_document_title` (composes with the theme's title pipeline instead of emitting a second `<title>`), then reports each push via `deploy`. **Cannot be executed here — no WordPress runtime is available in this environment.** Said so plainly in the README rather than claiming verification that didn't happen; what *is* verified is everything on the API side this plugin depends on.
  - **`plugins/shopify`** — a real, fully-testable TypeScript package instead (`@engine/shopify-plugin`, `pnpm --filter` wired into the workspace): a CLI (`engine-shopify-sync`, same env-var-not-flag pattern as `packages/crawler`'s `engine-crawl`) that resolves a product URL to its handle, looks it up via Shopify Admin GraphQL, and writes the diff into `engine_seo.*` product metafields — not a Script Tag (site-wide, not per-product) or a theme edit (needs merchant action) — documented tradeoff: the metafield is *available*, not automatically *rendered*, without a one-time theme change or a future Theme App Extension.
  - **21 tests** for the Shopify package (mocked `fetch`, routed by request shape rather than call order since the sync interleaves Engine and Shopify calls per action): applies schema and meta/title actions, skips an unresolvable product or a non-product URL without blocking the rest of the batch, skips (doesn't mark deployed) an action type this plugin has no handler for, and proves one failure doesn't block a second action's success. Same disclosed limitation as the crawler's report tests: mocked-`fetch` proves the client's request shape, not that Shopify's real API accepts it — no dev store or access token exists in this environment.
- **Added `checkCreateKeywordConfigBody`-style route-level tests** for the new `cms-plugin/actions` query-param validation (unrecognized `plugin` / missing `siteId` → 400 naming the field, before any DB call) — 2 new `@engine/api` route tests.
- Documented the pull/apply/report model and its rollback asymmetry in `docs/20-Architecture.md`. All 43 turbo tasks green (`@engine/shopify-plugin` new at 21 tests; `@engine/api` 59, was 57).

Then again after PR #28 (A4) merged and independently also claimed §3.5 for its own new section — bumped this one to **§3.6**, resolved a second `apps/api/src/index.ts` import conflict and a `routes.validation.test.ts` conflict (both branches added a `describe` block near the same spot; kept both), and folded this branch's log entry in after A4's now-canonical one rather than duplicating it.

## 2026-07-17 (cont. 6)
- **Last Phase-1 item: Stripe Checkout (M1.7 "Starter/Growth purchasable via Stripe").** Branched `feat/stripe-checkout` off `main`. `POST /billing/webhook` already existed and could turn a subscription event into our read model, but nothing could create the subscription a customer would actually pay for — `@engine/config`'s own integration registry had flagged this: `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` was already listed, `required: false`, with the description "only needed for outbound calls (creating checkout sessions / portal links)." Nothing had made that call yet.
- **`packages/billing/src/checkout.ts`**, following the file next to it (`signature.ts`)'s established convention: hit Stripe's REST API directly via `fetch`, not the Stripe SDK, since this runs in Cloudflare Workers and Checkout Sessions are a plain form-encoded POST. `resolvePriceId` inverts the existing `STRIPE_PRICE_TO_TIER` map (price id → tier, already required for the webhook) to go tier → price id, rather than adding a second map that could drift out of sync with the one the webhook trusts.
- **Found and got right on the first pass, not after a bug report, the one thing that would have silently broken checkout:** `mapStripeSubscriptionEvent` (the webhook mapper) reads `accountId` off the *subscription* object, not the session — so the checkout body must set `subscription_data[metadata][accountId]`, not top-level session `metadata`. The latter would have looked completely correct in a manual Stripe dashboard test (the session itself would show the metadata) while every subsequent webhook silently failed to find an `accountId` and dropped the update. Wrote a test asserting the *subscription_data* key specifically and that top-level `metadata[accountId]` is absent, so a regression here fails loudly instead of passing a superficial "checkout works" check.
- **`checkCreateCheckoutBody`** (`apps/api/src/validate.ts`) validates `tier`/`successUrl`/`cancelUrl`/`customerEmail`. Tightened the URL check beyond "does `new URL()` throw": the WHATWG parser accepts any scheme as syntactically valid (`javascript:`, `data:`, ...), and a caller-supplied redirect target reaching Stripe unchecked on a customer-facing checkout flow is an open-redirect-adjacent hole, not just a validation nicety — restricted to `http:`/`https:`.
- **`POST /accounts/:id/billing/checkout`**: 503 when `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is absent (degrades cleanly, same pattern as the SERP/LLM poll routes), 400 naming the field for a malformed body, 400 naming `tier` when the requested tier has no configured Stripe price (rather than a 500 from `createCheckoutSession` receiving an empty `priceId`).
- **Verified on `wrangler dev` against live Neon**: this environment's real `.dev.vars` has no `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` configured (confirmed by grep before testing, not assumed) — hit the route and got the real 503, matching what a customer would see today. Also incidentally re-confirmed `GET /accounts/:id/plan` (pre-existing, untouched by this change) 500s on a non-uuid `accountId` — same class of bug PR #25/#26 fixed elsewhere, but out of scope here; flagged separately (spawned as a follow-up task) rather than silently fixed inside an unrelated PR.
- **The actual live Stripe Checkout Session creation call was not verified against Stripe's real API** — no test-mode secret key exists in this environment. Disclosed plainly rather than claimed: `createCheckoutSession`'s request shape (form-encoded body, bearer auth, endpoint URL) is written to match Stripe's documented API and is unit-tested with a mocked `fetch`, same class of limitation as the Shopify plugin's tests in the prior slice.

- **Extended the `github-pr` target with a required `path`** (`packages/core/src/contract.ts`, additive): `repo`/`branch` say where, nothing said which file a diff should write to, and that can't be inferred — a site generator's file layout is opaque to Engine. Updated `apps/api/src/validate.ts`'s existing (already-present, never-exercised) target-shape check to require it.
- **Built `packages/deploy/src/githubPr.ts`** — calls GitHub's REST API directly via `fetch` (same reasoning as `packages/billing/src/checkout.ts` for Stripe: no SDK, `fetchImpl` injectable for testing). `exportActionAsPr` orchestrates: read the base branch's sha → create a branch named from the action id (`engine-fix/<id prefix>`, so a retried deploy reuses the same branch) → write the diff's `after` content to `path` → open a PR. This is the one deploy target in the package that's genuinely I/O, not a pure transform — 10 tests against mocked `fetch` responses routed by request shape, same discipline the Shopify plugin already established for a live account this sandbox doesn't have.
- **Wired it into the existing generic `deploy` transition**: a `github-pr` target has no live-request path to apply a diff through, so "deployed" means "the PR is open" and the export runs synchronously inside the transition before the status flips — a failed export (missing `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a real API error) leaves the action `approved`, never falsely `deployed`. The opened PR's URL/number land in the transition's audit-log `detail`. Registered `GITHUB_TOKEN` in `@engine/config`'s integration registry (category `deploy`, new).

## 2026-07-19
- **Merged PR #35**, synced `main`. Closed out M2.3's technical-fix half with the one remaining gap: C4.3 hreflang generation. B1.9's `hreflang-missing` finding has carried a `meta` template since diagnosis was built, but `generate.ts`'s `'meta'` dispatch only ever regenerated title/description — a hreflang finding silently produced nothing. Branched `feat/hreflang-c4.3` off `main`.
- **Built the generator honestly-scoped to what's derivable**: a page's i18n cluster (its sibling URLs in other languages) isn't observable from crawling that one page — B1.9's own `expectsHreflang` field already documents this as "caller's i18n config." Added `ActionContext.hreflangAlternates` for the caller to supply the cluster; `generateHreflangAction` (`packages/actions/src/hreflang.ts`) only generates the `<link rel="alternate" hreflang="…">` markup from what it's given, nothing inferred. `generate.ts`'s `'meta'` case now dispatches on `finding.issueType === 'hreflang-missing'` before falling through to title/description — the same "route by finding, not template type alone" pattern `'redirect'` already established for its two source findings.
- **Extended `Diff.field` with `'hreflang'`** (additive, alongside `'title'`/`'description'`) so `packages/deploy` knows where to write it. `applyMetaDiff` inserts the tag block before `</head>` (always appended — the finding only fires when a page declares *no* hreflang, so nothing existing to search-and-replace); `verifyHtmlDeploy` checks it landed. No `apps/workers` changes needed — it already applies any `'meta'`-typed action generically.
- **Verified live end-to-end through the actual edge worker** (the first time this session's technical-fix work drove a schema/meta-style deploy through `apps/workers`, not just the API): a page audited with `expectsHreflang: true` and no hreflang produced the real finding → generated a real action with two supplied alternates → approved → deployed → `wrangler dev` served the two `<link>` tags inside `<head>` of the actual proxied response. Caught my own harness mistake mid-verification: the first attempt used a finding whose `evidence.url` (a fake `acme.example` domain) didn't match the worker's `ORIGIN`-derived request URL, so the page-scoped action filter correctly found no match and served unchanged HTML — not a product bug, fixed by aligning the crawled URL with the actual local origin. 5 new `@engine/actions` tests (25→30), 2 new `@engine/deploy` tests (37→39). All 46 turbo tasks green. Documented in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.11.
- **Merged PR #36**, synced `main`. All four C4 technical-fix gaps named in M2.3's exit criterion (redirects, canonical, hreflang, GitHub PRs) now landed. Picked C3.2 (meta title/description "at scale") as the last M2.3 item, per the user. Branched `feat/meta-fixes-at-scale-c3.2` off `main`.
- **Found the real gap before building anything**: generating a fix has always meant one `/actions/generate` call per finding — and nobody in the product ever actually called it in a loop. Neither `apps/dashboard` nor `packages/crawler`'s `engine-crawl` CLI ever invokes `/actions/generate` at all; a crawl produces findings, and turning them into proposed fixes has only ever happened through manual API calls (mine, for verification). "At scale" isn't a performance problem, it's a missing feature.
- **Built the opt-in auto-propose path in `POST /projects/:id/audit`**: an optional `target: DeployTarget` in the request body (Engine has no per-project default deploy target stored anywhere, so it can't invent one — the caller must say where fixes go). When supplied, every `meta-title-missing`/`meta-description-missing` finding from the crawl without an existing Action gets one proposed automatically from that page's own crawled title/description. Omitted, `/audit` behaves exactly as before. Scoped to meta only — schema/robots/redirect still need explicit context this route has no basis to assume. Added `findingIdsWithActions` (`apps/api/src/repositories/actions.ts`) so a re-audit doesn't duplicate proposals.
- **The build itself surfaced a real, pre-existing bug**: `generate.ts`'s `'meta'` dispatch checked `ctx.currentTitle`/`ctx.currentMetaDescription` directly instead of the finding's own `issueType`, so a `meta-title-missing` finding and a `meta-description-missing` finding for the same page each independently emitted *both* a title and a description action — doubling every meta proposal the moment two related findings for one page were processed together, which nothing had done until this auto-propose loop did it naturally. Fixed by dispatching on `finding.issueType` explicitly (the same pattern `'hreflang-missing'` already needed), with a test that runs both findings through one shared context and asserts exactly one of each action, not two.
- **Verified live end-to-end**: a 2-page crawl with both fields missing, audited with a `target`, proposed exactly 4 actions (not 8, confirming the dispatcher fix). A re-audit of the same pages proposed zero (idempotency guard holding), and an audit without `target` proposed nothing (opt-in, backward compatible). 8 new `@engine/actions` tests (30→33 net, replacing/extending the old dual-emit test), 3 new `apps/api` validate tests (62→65). All 46 turbo tasks green. Documented in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.12.

Built that heuristic baseline — which is also literally what the roadmap's M2.1 exit criterion ("beats a heuristic baseline") presupposes exists.

B1 never needed it (technical audit only reads meta signals). Added `headings`/`bodyText` as new **optional** fields (unlike this repo's usual preference for required fields — `CrawledPage` is constructed in every crawler test, API fixture, and the dashboard, and making these required would ripple a B2-only signal through call sites with no reason to know about it). Wired real extraction into `packages/crawler`'s `crawlPage.ts`, preferring `<main>`/`<article>` over the whole `<body>` to keep nav/footer chrome out of the heuristics.
- **Built `packages/content`** (new package, mirrors `@engine/diagnosis`'s shape): `score.ts` — B2.2 answer-first (lead-paragraph length + no filler-opener), B2.3 self-containment (fraction of paragraphs not opening with a dangling reference pronoun), B2.4 E-E-A-T (byline/date/author-schema presence) — combined into one 0-100 score. B2.1 entity coverage explicitly **not** scored (needs the entity keyword set nothing wires in yet); reported as `entityCoverage: 'not-measured'` rather than a misleading 0. `rules.ts`/`actions.ts`/`audit.ts` mirror B1's shape exactly: findings carry `source: 'content'` and a `'content'` `ActionTemplate` (the C3.1 executor still doesn't exist, declined this session — the finding ships anyway, diagnosis and execution are separate concerns). 17 new tests.
- **Wired into `POST /projects/:id/audit`**: runs `runContentAudit` alongside B1's `runAudit`, merges both finding sets before persisting. B2's score is reported separately (`content: {pageScores, pagesWithoutContent}`), not folded into B1's technical health score — §8 defines that score as technical-issue-only.
- **Caught a real bug the instant it ran against real Chromium, not a mock**: `page.evaluate()` only serializes the function it's given, not the module-scope constants that function closes over — a `const BODY_TEXT_MAX_CHARS` declared outside `extractDomSignals` threw `ReferenceError: BODY_TEXT_MAX_CHARS is not defined` *inside the browser context*, invisible to `tsc` and to any test that doesn't launch a real browser. `tsc` and `vitest run` on packages/content were both green while this was silently broken in the one package that actually exercises it. Fixed by inlining the constant; caught by the crawler's existing real-Chromium test suite — the same discipline that caught the connectors' "Illegal invocation" bug and the crawler's sitemap-resolution bug in earlier sessions.
- **Verified live end-to-end against migrated Neon**: a page with a byline, date, and answer-first lead scored 90/100 with no findings; a thin page with the same structure but no authorship signals scored 57 and fired exactly one `weak-eeat` finding; a page with no captured content was excluded from scoring and findings, counted separately (not defaulted to a misleading score). All three round-tripped correctly through `GET /audit`, including the persisted `content`-type action template. All 49 turbo tasks green (`@engine/content` new, 17 tests). Documented in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.13.
- **Merged PR #38**, synced `main`. User picked wiring B2.1 entity coverage next — closing the one gap §3.13 explicitly disclosed as `'not-measured'`. Branched `feat/entity-coverage-b2.1` off `main`.
- **Wired `entityCoverageScore` into `packages/content`**: fraction of the entity's canonical name + known keywords (from the entity graph, M2.2) found on the page, case-insensitive substring match. `null` (not 0) when the entity has no keywords defined yet — a fresh entity hasn't failed a coverage check, it hasn't had one. When measured, the combined score reweights to 0.25/0.25/0.2/0.3 (answer-first/self-containment/E-E-A-T/coverage) instead of silently treating an unmeasured dimension as 0 in a fixed-weight average. New `weak-entity-coverage` finding + `'content'` action template, matching B2's own Finding→Action table ("Entity coverage gap → Add covering sections").

All 49 turbo tasks green. Documented in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.13.

## 2026-07-20
- **Traced the real gap before designing the "multi-client grid" UI**, via a research subagent then confirmed by reading the code directly: `accounts`/`projects` (migration 0001) always had the right one-account-to-many-projects shape, but **no route ever created either row** — every account/project in this environment was seeded straight into Postgres — and **no table linked a signed-in identity to an account at all**. Every existing `/projects/:projectId/*` route (~15 of them) trusts the URL's `projectId` with zero ownership check. This is a real authz gap, and it's also the actual prerequisite for a "list of your clients" view: you cannot render "yours" without a table saying so. Entered plan mode given the size and security angle; user approved.
- **Migration `0006_accounts_membership_branding.sql`**: `users` (mirrors `AuthUser.id`, the Neon Auth JWT `sub`, upserted lazily per-request — no Neon Auth webhook exists to sync it any other way), `account_members` (`account_id, user_id, role: 'owner'|'member'`), `accounts.branding jsonb`. Applied live against migrated Neon (`npm run db:migrate`).
- **`packages/core/src/account.ts`**: `Account`/`Project`/`AccountBranding` types, exported from the package index.
- **`apps/api/src/repositories/accounts.ts`**: `upsertUser`, `createAccount` (+ owner membership row), `isAccountMember`, `getProjectAccountId`, `listAccountsForUser` (the multi-client grid's entire data source — scoped by the caller's own identity, not a caller-supplied id, so there's no cross-tenant path to check there at all), `createProject`, `listProjectsByAccount`, `updateAccountBranding`.
- **New routes** (`apps/api/src/index.ts`), all behind the existing `requireAuth` gate: `POST /accounts`, `GET /accounts`, `POST /accounts/:id/projects` and `PATCH /accounts/:id/branding` (both membership-checked — the one place in this slice where an `accountId` *is* caller-supplied), `GET /accounts/:id/report`.
- **`apps/api/src/report.ts`**: the branded report — a self-contained `text/html` document (not a generated PDF: Workers have no headless-Chromium runtime, and a browser's own Print-to-PDF covers that need), reusing `assembleSurfaceScores`/`latestAuditRun` per project. Unmeasured scores render "not measured yet", never a misleading 0 — same honesty rule `/audit`'s null `healthScore` already established. 4 new tests (escaping, logo fallback, honest nulls).
- **Dashboard**: new "Clients" nav item (`views/accounts.ts`, the grid — real accounts/projects, "+ New client"/"+ Project" flows); branding folded into Settings (an account-level setting, not a new page) operating on the last-selected `accountId`; `views/report.ts` fetches the report as a Blob URL, renders it in an iframe with a standalone-tab link.
- **Migration applied and every new route exercised live against migrated Neon** via `wrangler dev`: created a real account+project, confirmed `GET /accounts` scopes correctly to the caller, confirmed a non-member gets a real `403` on another account's report (the actual authz property this slice exists to establish) and a non-uuid id gets `400`; ran a real 1-page audit and confirmed the report reflects the real health score (44) alongside an honest "not measured yet" for the unpolled unified score.
- **Caught a real bug live, not in vitest**: the dashboard's branding-save PATCH failed silently in the browser (`net::ERR_FAILED` after a *successful* 204 preflight) — Hono's `cors()` middleware's `allowMethods` only listed `['GET', 'POST', 'OPTIONS']`, so the browser blocked the real PATCH client-side even though the preflight itself said 204. No test exercises a live CORS preflight; this only showed up driving the actual dashboard against the actual Worker. Fixed by adding `'PATCH'`.
- **Browser-verified the full flow against the live API** (dev-session bypass, since the sandbox has no route to the real Neon Auth magic-link endpoint): Clients grid renders real data, branding save round-trips and the report picks it up live, both light/dark themes, zero console errors after the CORS fix.
- **Flagged the pre-existing routes gap as a separate follow-up** (`mcp__ccd_session__spawn_task`) rather than folding a ~15-route retrofit into this PR: every `/projects/:projectId/*` route still has no ownership check against the caller's identity — this slice's new membership model (`isAccountMember`/`getProjectAccountId`) is what makes fixing that tractable, but doing it here would have bloated the change.
- **Explicitly out of scope, disclosed**: scheduled report delivery/email dispatch — needs an email-provider account (SendGrid/Postmark/Resend) this sandbox doesn't have; M2.5's own DoD line asks for "branded reports shipping," not scheduled delivery (the broader Platform-scope D3 bullet). All 32 turbo tasks green. Documented in `docs/20-Architecture.md` §3.15.
- **Closed the follow-up flagged above**: retrofitted the ownership check onto every pre-existing `/projects/:projectId/*` route (~15: rank/poll, ai/poll, entities x2, cms-plugin/actions, keywords/research, entity keywords x2, copilot/summary, pulse x2, audit x2, actions/generate, actions/generate-content, actions list/get, the approve/deploy/rollback transition handler, verify, onboarding x2, gsc/connect-url). Discovered M2.5 itself (PR #41, `isAccountMember`/`getProjectAccountId`) hadn't actually landed on `main` yet when this branch started — it was uncommitted in the other worktree; confirmed with the user, they merged PR #41, then rebased this branch onto the updated `main`.
- **First pass used a blanket `app.use('/projects/:projectId/*', ...)` middleware** — reverted it: it ran before each route's own body/format validation, so it broke `routes.validation.test.ts`'s "malformed body 400/503-before-any-DB-call" tests. Switched to a shared `projectAccessError(db, projectId, user)` helper called inline in each handler, after any DB-free format checks (matching the file's existing convention — e.g. the rank/poll and ai/poll entity-ownership checks run inline, not via middleware). Skips the check for service callers (`user.isService`, the crawl runner / edge-worker auto-rollback) — they hold the shared `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN`, not a Neon Auth user id, so there's no `account_members` row to check against.
- **Verified live against the real migrated Neon DB via `wrangler dev`**, not just vitest: found a real second project (`Verification Project`, a different account, left over from M2.5's own live testing) that the local dev user isn't a member of. Confirmed every retrofitted route 403s on that foreign project (entities, audit POST — including that it never reached `upsertFindings`, pulse, onboarding, actions list, action transitions, cms-plugin/actions, keywords/research, entity keywords), a random uuid 404s, and the dev user's own project still works end-to-end. All 98 existing vitest tests still pass; full `turbo build` green.

CHANGELOG.md stays the internal engineering record; the `<!--internal-->` marker machinery added earlier is removed since nothing from it is published now.

Rewrote /docs/roadmap off internal phases onto Available today / In progress / Further out.
- NEW: build-time leak guard. BANNED regex list (repo layout, stack, infra, internal contracts, suppliers, competitive analysis) is checked against the visible text of every rendered page; on a hit the build prints each offending term with its page, deletes the output dir and exits 1. Verified both directions — a deliberately planted "packages/local … Postgres" line fails the build with exit=1, clean build exits 0. Independently re-grepped the output: zero hits.
- Only the pillar (A/B) is still read from docs/feature-specs/*.md, purely so a new feature can't silently go missing from the index. Everything a reader sees is authored in build-docs.mjs or content/changelog.md.

## 2026-07-26 (why /docs was blank on the deployed URL)
- Reported: engine-7vv.pages.dev/docs/{changelog,features,roadmap} render blank. Diagnosed — not a docs bug, a deployment one.
- engine-7vv.pages.dev IS apps/dashboard (verified: served index.html is byte-identical to apps/dashboard/index.html). It's a SPA, so its Pages project returns index.html for every path; /docs/* gets the app shell, the router matches nothing, #root stays empty → blank screen. The docs were never deployed there. Also checked engine.pages.dev — that's an unrelated third party's "Boilerbay" site, not ours.
- Second blocker found: apps/web now contains node_modules (marked devDep) with pnpm workspace symlinks, so pointing a Pages project at apps/web would fail with the same "build output directory contains links to files that can't be accessed" error apps/dashboard hit on 07-16.
- Fix (branch fix/web-pages-deploy-output, PR to main): apps/web/assemble-site.mjs copies index.html + docs/ into a clean symlink-free apps/web/site/, and hard-fails if a symlink survives or any expected route is missing. Wired into the web build script; site/ gitignored; turbo inputs/outputs updated (dropped the stale CHANGELOG.md input — no longer a source). New apps/web/README.md records the Pages settings and why /docs must NOT be served from the dashboard project (SPA catch-all + it's noindex, which would hide the docs from search).
- STILL REQUIRED (Cloudflare dashboard, not code): create a Pages project for the public site with build command `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm --filter @engine/web build` and output directory `apps/web/site`.
- Verified by serving the assembled output: /, /docs/, /docs/changelog, /docs/features, /docs/roadmap, /docs/docs.css all resolve.
- NOTE: another session is mid-work on an interactive /docs/architecture page (apps/web/build-docs.mjs, docs.css, generated docs/**, new docs/architecture/). Left entirely untouched — staged only my 5 files. Running the web build regenerates docs/** with their in-flight nav change, so their output is in the working tree, uncommitted.

## 2026-07-26 (built /docs/architecture — interactive product-pipeline diagram)
- Scope check first (AskUserQuestion): build-docs.mjs's leak guard is explicit that /docs never publishes internal architecture (stack/infra/repo layout). Confirmed with the user that "architecture" means a conceptual, customer-facing pipeline diagram — Measure → Score → Diagnose → Fix → Where it lands — not infrastructure. Direction: scroll-driven vertical pipeline (not a draggable node graph or canvas/particle flow) to match the site's calm editorial register.
- Added `buildArchitecture(specs)` to apps/web/build-docs.mjs generating /docs/architecture as a real build route (survives `rmSync(OUT)` on rebuild, passes the same BANNED-term leak guard as every other page — verified clean). Added "Architecture" to NAV between Overview and Features.
- Content: 5 stages pulled from existing feature-spec copy (no new claims invented) — Measure (A1,A2,A4,A5,A6) → Score (A3, with a decorative "range not a point" bar) → Diagnose (B1–B5) → Fix (a hand-authored 4-step chain: Fix Queue → Apply → Verify → Landed/Rolled back, reusing the "Fix Queue" term the BANNED comment explicitly allows) → Where it lands (Your site / Your CMS / Your Business Profile, pulled from the homepage lede).
- Interaction: each of the 11 feature nodes is a click-to-expand disclosure (CSS grid 0fr→1fr trick, `inert` toggled via JS, degrades to open-by-default with no JS). Stage/connector reveal on scroll via IntersectionObserver adding `.in-view` (falls back to instant-visible without JS or IO support). Connectors are a minimal collector-line motif (bar + stem + arrow + two looping flow dots) rather than literal per-node wiring — deliberately simplified from an earlier bezier-funnel idea for responsiveness and lower risk. Full `prefers-reduced-motion` coverage (dots hidden, stages forced visible, no transform).
- Bug found and fixed during verification: `html.js-arch .arch-detail.is-open` needed the `html.js-arch` prefix too — without it, the collapsed-state rule's higher specificity silently beat the open-state rule and nothing ever expanded. Caught via computed-style inspection, not visually.
- Verified via Browser pane: light + dark theme, desktop + mobile (390px) responsive stacking, and the click-to-expand interaction, using a resized-tall viewport to capture the full page in one screenshot after the in-session scroll action became unreliable (repeated timeouts / blank captures on `computer scroll` and JS `scrollTo` — a tool-level flake this session, worked around rather than debugged).
- Added `--ease-in-out` token to docs.css (was missing, used by the range-breathe and flow-dot keyframes) and reduced-motion/mobile overrides for the new `.arch-*` classes.

## 2026-07-27 (made /docs actually reachable — mounted into the live Pages project)
- User reported /docs still blank on engine-7vv.pages.dev. Diagnosed: that Pages project builds **apps/dashboard** (output `site/` = index.html + styles.css + dist/), byte-identical to what's served at the root. apps/web has never been deployed anywhere. Every /docs/* URL returned 200 — the dashboard SPA catch-all serving its shell — so it rendered an empty `<div id="root">` rather than 404ing.
- The 07-25 fix (PR #51) was sound but shipped to a Pages project nobody points at. Per user direction ("make this work using this pages project only"), reversed the earlier "docs must not be served from the dashboard project" decision: apps/web is now build *input* for the dashboard's deploy output, not a separate deploy.
- apps/dashboard/scripts/assembleSite.mjs now runs apps/web/build-docs.mjs and copies apps/web/docs into `site/docs`, then applies the same guarantees apps/web/assemble-site.mjs made — hard-fail on any surviving symlink or missing expected route (index.html, styles.css, docs/{index,docs.css,changelog,features,roadmap}).
- Added `@engine/web` as a workspace devDependency of @engine/dashboard so the install can't leave `marked` unresolved, and added apps/dashboard/turbo.json declaring `site/**` as a build output (without it a turbo cache hit would restore a dist/-only build with no docs).
- Verified on the assembled output, not just the build log: /, /docs/, /docs/features/, /docs/features/rank-tracking/, /docs/changelog/, /docs/roadmap/, /docs/architecture/, /docs/docs.css, /styles.css all 200 with correct titles; root still serves the dashboard; docs render fully styled (screenshot + docs.css 200 in the network log). Clean-from-scratch build and full `pnpm build` (23/23) both pass.
- Branch fix/docs-into-pages-output off main (previous branch's PR #51 already merged; did not stack). No Cloudflare dashboard change needed — the existing project's build now produces the docs.
- Correction from user on the earlier "do not serve /docs from the dashboard project" note: the real constraint is internal vs customer-facing *content*, not which Pages project serves it. Customer docs on the public site are fine; internal docs (repo layout, stack, infra, suppliers, competitive analysis, internal roadmap, docs/00-Master-PRD.md et al) must never reach a public URL regardless of project. That distinction is already enforced in code by build-docs.mjs's BANNED guard. Rewrote apps/web/README.md to record it correctly, replacing the stale "needs its own Pages project" / two-hostname sections with the single-project reality, and noting that `noindex` sits on the dashboard shell only, not the generated docs pages.
- Landing page made public too (user: "the landing page should be public too"). One Pages project now serves the whole surface: `/` landing (apps/web/index.html), `/docs` public docs, `/app` the product. Dashboard moved off the root into /app — safe because it is hash-routed (src/shell.ts uses location.hash, so in-app routes are /app/#/pulse, no server-side SPA fallback needed) and its asset refs are relative ("./styles.css", "./dist/app.js"), so they resolve under /app unrewritten.
- assembleSite.mjs restructured accordingly; required-routes assertion extended to app/index.html, app/styles.css, app/dist/app.js. Added a guard that fails the build if the root index.html carries `noindex` — i.e. if the dashboard shell ever lands at / instead of the landing page. That mix-up is invisible in a build log (both files are index.html, both valid HTML) and is exactly the class of bug this whole layout exists to fix.
- apps/web/index.html "Log in" now points at /app/ (same origin), replacing https://engine-app.pages.dev/ — a hostname that never existed.
- Verified in-browser: / renders the landing page fully (first screenshot was blank in the hero — a webfont-loading flake, not a defect; confirmed h1 present with opacity 1 via computed style, second capture rendered correctly), /app/ boots the dashboard with all dist/** modules 200 under the new prefix and zero console errors, auth screen renders, /docs/* unchanged. 72 files, no symlinks.
- FOLLOW-UP for user (Neon Auth console, not code): neonAuth.ts sends `callbackURL: location.href`, so the OAuth callback moved from the root to https://engine-7vv.pages.dev/app/ — must be allowlisted or Google sign-in fails on redirect back.

## 2026-07-27 (fix: /docs index rendered unstyled in production)
- PR #53 merged and deployed; landing page, /docs and /app all live. But https://engine-7vv.pages.dev/docs/ rendered as raw unstyled HTML.
- Cause: an off-by-one in build-docs.mjs. `shell({depth})` counts levels below /docs, and buildIndex passed `depth: 1` when /docs/index.html is depth 0. So the index asked for `../docs.css` → `/docs.css`, which does not exist. Pages answered the miss with the SPA shell (HTML, status 200), and the browser dropped the stylesheet on its MIME type. Only the /docs index was affected — every deeper page had the right depth, which is why the earlier browser check (done on /docs/features/) passed and this did not.
- Fix at the class level, not the instance: `depth` is no longer passed by hand. write(route, opts) derives it from the route and renders the shell itself, so the depth and the path the file lands at can no longer disagree. Removed all six hand-declared `depth:` values.
- Added a relative-link check to build-docs.mjs: resolves every non-external href/src on every generated page against its DEPLOYED url and fails the build if the target is not published. Deliberately resolves against the deploy layout rather than the filesystem — on disk `../docs.css` from /docs/ hits apps/web/docs.css, the SOURCE stylesheet, so a filesystem check would have called this exact bug fine. Verified by reintroducing the off-by-one: build exits 1 and names `"../docs.css" → /docs.css, which is not published`.
- Verified: /docs/ now loads docs.css (139 rules applied, Instrument Serif on the h1) and renders styled; /docs/features/, /docs/architecture/, /app/ and / all still 200.
- Lesson worth keeping: on this Pages project a missing asset is never a 404 — the SPA fallback turns every miss into 200 text/html. Status-code checks cannot detect it; only resolving links against the deploy layout can.
