## 2026-08-24 (Google integrations: OAuth foundation, part 1)
- Scoped GSC + GA4 + GBP self-serve integrations. Survey of existing state: GSC consent-URL and code-exchange routes exist (apps/api/src/index.ts:1335, :1363) but discard the tokens; no Search Console *read* path exists at all; GA4 is absent entirely; GBP writes are complete and tested (packages/deploy/src/gbp.ts) but read one global `GBP_REFRESH_TOKEN` Worker secret; the dashboard Integrations view is read-only env-var readiness with no connect action; no encryption helper and no cron trigger exist anywhere.
- Named the structural gap: every integration to date is one credential per deployment, held as a Worker secret. Per-account OAuth inverts that — the credential is the customer's, arrives at runtime, and must live in Postgres. That inversion is the work; the Google API calls are the smaller half.
- Found a latent vulnerability to fix on the way: both existing OAuth routes use `state = projectId` and the callback is (necessarily) ungated. Harmless only because nothing is stored — once a callback persists a credential, a hand-edited `state` binds an attacker's Google account to a victim's account.
- Decisions taken with the user: connections scoped per **account** then assigned to projects/entities; refresh tokens **AES-256-GCM sealed in Postgres** under an `ENCRYPTION_KEY` Worker secret; **both** on-demand and scheduled sync; sequencing is **shared foundation branch first**, then one branch per provider (their CLAUDE.md forbids parallel PRs over shared files, and all three providers touch the config registry, apps/api/src/index.ts, the dashboard nav and migrations).
- Correction worth recording: ClickHouse is schema-only. `infra/migrations/clickhouse/0001_init.sql` exists but nothing in any `src/` connects to it, and migration 0005 already puts the equivalent A1/A2 timeseries in Postgres. Scheduled GSC/GA4 metrics follow that precedent — no ClickHouse provisioning needed.
- Built on branch `feat/google-oauth-foundation`: `packages/auth/src/secretBox.ts` (AES-256-GCM seal/open, random IV per call, account+provider bound in as AAD so a blob moved between rows fails to open) and `packages/auth/src/oauthState.ts` (HMAC-SHA-256 signed, TTL'd state carrying accountId/userId/provider/nonce, returning a typed reason rather than throwing so the callback can distinguish an expired tab from an attack). Both dependency-free Web Crypto, matching the SDK-free constraint in jwt.ts. 23 new tests, 48 pass in @engine/auth.
- Migration `0014_integration_connections.sql`: `integration_connections` (one consented Google account per account+provider, sealed refresh token, granted_scopes, connected/needs_reauth/revoked) and `integration_assignments` (provider resource -> project, or -> entity for GBP, since a location is an entity per 0013). Composite FK on (connection_id, provider) makes the denormalized provider non-divergent; a partial unique index enforces one GSC/GA4 property per project while leaving GBP many-per-project.
- Verified the migration against the live Neon database inside a transaction with savepoints, then rolled back: DDL valid, and all 9 constraint behaviours confirmed (duplicate connection, bad provider, entity_id shape both ways, provider/connection mismatch, second GSC property, two GBP locations accepted, duplicate resource). `status` re-checked afterwards — still 13 applied / 1 pending, live schema unchanged. Did not apply to Neon; that is the user's call.
- Connectors built (`packages/connectors/src/google/`): `providers.ts` (one registry driving the flow for all three providers — scopes, required Cloud APIs, whether an access request gates it, whether it writes), `oauth.ts` (consent URL, code exchange, refresh, revoke, permanent-vs-transient grant failure), `api.ts` (shared request plumbing + `GoogleApiError` classifying the three different causes Google returns as HTTP 403), `gsc.ts` (list properties, search analytics, startRow paging), `ga4.ts` (Admin API accountSummaries for the picker, Data API runReport, string metrics parsed to numbers), `gbpRead.ts` (accounts, locations with the mandatory readMask, reviews from v4).
- Notes worth keeping: GA4 needs the **Admin** API enabled as well as the Data API, or the connect flow has no properties to offer. The Business Information API rejects a locations list with no `readMask`, and a field missing from the mask reads as absent — which the B5 audit would report as a real finding against the customer. GBP reviews exist only on the v4 host. `business.manage` has no read-only variant, so reading reviews necessarily grants write access; the Fix Queue's approve-before-deploy gate is what keeps that in check.
- 83 new connector tests; 110 pass in @engine/connectors. Every request shape asserted against an injected fetch — no live OAuth client needed.
- Repository + routes. `apps/api/src/repositories/integrations.ts` is the only place a refresh token is sealed or opened — nothing it exports returns the ciphertext, and `getAccessToken` returns a short-lived access token while keeping the long-lived one inside. Added `getAccountRole` to the accounts repository: `isAccountMember` answered "may they read this?", which was all any route needed before, but connect/disconnect is an owner decision since the credential is shared by the whole account.
- `apps/api/src/routes/integrations.ts` (new module — index.ts was already past 1,600 lines): provider catalogue, connect-url, `/oauth/google/callback`, resource picker, disconnect, and project assignment. The callback returns HTML (a human's browser lands there), self-closes when opened as a popup, and escapes anything it echoes back.
- Removed the two superseded routes `/projects/:id/onboarding/gsc/connect-url` and `/oauth/gsc/callback`, plus the `GSC_CLIENT_*` env vars. Nothing called them but the docs. Moved E1's `markGscConnected` milestone onto the GSC *assignment* — it used to fire on token exchange, before any property was chosen and while the token was discarded, which made the time-to-first-insight KPI measure the wrong moment.
- Fixed a real CORS bug on the way: `allowMethods` listed only GET/POST/PATCH/OPTIONS, so PUT and DELETE would pass preflight and then fail — the same trap the branding route hit once with PATCH. Added both, with a test asserting the preflight names them.
- Config registry: replaced the `gsc-oauth` entry with `google-integrations` covering all three providers plus `ENCRYPTION_KEY` and `OAUTH_STATE_SECRET`, regenerated `.dev.vars.example`. Added a readiness test for the case that matters: a valid OAuth client with no `ENCRYPTION_KEY` is 'partial', not 'configured', because that deployment can walk a user through consent and then fail to store the result.
- 24 new route tests (125 pass in @engine/api), 2 new readiness tests (9 in @engine/config). Every route assertion is about a check that happens before `createDb`, proved by a DATABASE_URL that points nowhere.
- Sync layer. Migration `0015_google_metrics.sql`: `gsc_query_daily`, `gsc_page_daily`, `ga4_channel_daily`, plus `last_synced_at` / `last_sync_error` / `last_sync_rows` on assignments. Two GSC tables rather than one date×query×page table — that cross product is enormous and answers a question nobody asks; the product asks about keywords and about pages separately. Country and device deliberately not stored: no surface reads them, and unused dimensions are cardinality for nothing.
- Every metric write is an upsert on the natural grain. This is the one that would have bitten silently: a cron firing twice would otherwise double every click count and nothing about the number would look wrong. Verified against live Neon in a rolled-back transaction — same day inserted twice yields one row with the later value, not the sum.
- `apps/api/src/repositories/googleSync.ts` — one module serving both trigger paths, because a scheduled sync taking a different code path from the button the user pressed is a second implementation to keep correct, and the unwatched one drifts. GSC window ends 3 days back (Search Console lags ~2 days and revises for several more); the whole window is re-fetched each run so revisions land via the upsert.
- Caught a real off-by-one while writing the window tests: `windowDays: 28` produced a 29-day span. Both Google bounds are inclusive, so `from` is `windowDays - 1` steps back, not `windowDays`. Fixed, and pinned with a test that computes the actual day count for every provider and span.
- `syncGbp` writes the real `LocalProfileFacts` shape (I had guessed wrong first and checked `packages/local/src/types.ts`). Two fields the API cannot supply here are *scored* by the B5 audit — `photoCount` (media is a separate v4 endpoint) and `directoryListings` (A6's job, not GBP's) — so they are carried forward from any existing profile rather than overwritten with a false empty, which would manufacture a finding against the customer. Added `attributes` to the GBP read mask for the same reason: omitting it would make every synced location look like it has none.
- First `scheduled` handler in this Worker (`[triggers] crons = ["15 3 * * *"]`). `runScheduledSync` collects failures instead of throwing — one customer's revoked token must not abort every other customer's nightly sync. Default export is now `{ fetch, scheduled }` with `app` exported by name; the two route test files import the named export.
- 17 new tests (142 pass in @engine/api). Migration 0015 verified against live Neon and rolled back; status still 13 applied, 2 pending.
- Dashboard UI (`apps/dashboard/src/views/googleIntegrations.ts`), one card per provider, embedded in Settings above the existing readiness block. Kept them separate and relabelled the old one "Platform wiring": `/health/integrations` answers "have *we* wired a vendor key", which is an operator question; this answers "is *your* Google account connected". Four distinct states — no OAuth client on the deployment, nothing connected, connected, revoked/insufficient-scope — because collapsing any pair says "not connected" when the real answer is "reconnect" or "ask your administrator".
- Consent opens in a popup so the user keeps their page; the callback page posts a message and closes itself, with a `popup.closed` poll so a hand-closed window does not leave the promise pending. Popup-blocked falls back to a same-tab redirect.
- Fixed a UX flaw found in the browser: `request` throws "<status> <raw body>", so an API error rendered the JSON straight into the page. Added `readableError` — pulls out the `error` field and gives 403 an actionable sentence ("Pick the right client from the Clients grid") instead of a status code.
- Also caught that the assignment mapper predated 0015 and never surfaced the sync-health columns the UI reads. `lastSyncRows` maps `?? undefined` not `?? 0`: zero rows synced and never synced are different facts and the UI says "0 rows" vs "never".
- Verified in the browser against the live local API. `/integrations/providers` returns all three providers with the right warnings; CORS preflight on the new routes returns 204 including DELETE. With the three account/project calls stubbed, all provider states render correctly: connected + "synced 60m ago · 1420 rows", connected-but-insufficient-scope shows the amber warning, needs_reauth shows the revoked warning and withholds "Choose location" (listing would fail). The GSC picker disables an unverified property as "(no access)"; the GBP picker adds the location-entity field and hint. Computed styles confirm the CSS applies (connected vs needs_reauth borders differ, empty picker hidden by `:empty`), no horizontal overflow at 375px, no JS console errors. Screenshots unavailable — the Browser pane is not displayed in this session — so verification was DOM- and computed-style-based.
- Rewrote `docs/40-Integrations.md` §3 for the per-account model: how it differs from every other integration on the page, the handshake diagram, the one-connection-many-projects mapping, full provisioning steps, the sensitive-scope verification gate, and the ENCRYPTION_KEY rotation warning (rotating invalidates every stored connection; there is no re-wrap path).
- Full monorepo green: 699 tests across 20 packages, build clean.
- Migrated the C5 GBP deploy off the single-tenant secret. `POST /projects/:id/actions/:id/deploy` now takes its token from the project's own account connection; `GBP_REFRESH_TOKEN`/`GBP_CLIENT_ID`/`GBP_CLIENT_SECRET` remain only as a fallback so an existing single-tenant deployment keeps working. Worth stating plainly why: that secret held one owner's consent for every customer, so with two customers the second one's approved fix would have been written to the first one's listing.
- Full monorepo green after the change: 699 tests, build clean. PR #55 updated (5 commits).
- Revisited the column-list question the user raised. Tested both postgres.js dynamic-SQL forms against the live DB: `sql.unsafe()` is a raw splice with no identifier quoting; `sql(array)` is the safe helper, composes nested in a template, and blocked an injection attempt by quoting it into a non-existent column name. So `db.unsafe()` was the wrong tool regardless and removing it was right.
- Neither form fixes the actual hazard, though. The column list existed to *not* mention `refresh_token_sealed`, which is a convention someone has to remember — a `select *` written later would put a live customer refresh token into an API response, with no type error, no failing test, and nothing to notice. Generating the list from an array does not prevent that.
- Chose the structural fix instead: moved the token to its own 1:1 `integration_credentials` table in migration 0014 (free to change since 0014 is still unapplied; a data migration once it is). Every connection read is now a plain `select *`, and there is no column list anywhere in the repository. `upsertConnection` and `disconnect` became transactions, since connection and credential are one fact — a connection stored without its credential shows as healthy and fails on first use; a disconnect that removed one but not the other would leave the token behind after the user asked us to sever access. Disconnect now deletes the credential row outright rather than blanking a column, and `getAccessToken` left-joins so "no connection" stays distinguishable from "connection with no credential".
- Verified against live Neon in a rolled-back transaction, six properties: `select *` on connections returns no credential column (12 columns), one credential per connection enforced, the access-token join reaches it, disconnect removes the credential and keeps the audit row, re-consent preserves the connection id so assignments survive, deleting a connection cascades the credential away.
- Added `packages/db/src/schemaInvariants.test.ts` to pin it — plus duplicate-version and gapless-ordering checks on the migration set, which is the collision the user's CLAUDE.md warns about with branches in flight. Confirmed the invariant test actually fails by reintroducing the column: 1 failed, then restored to 19 passing. A guard that cannot fail proves nothing.
- Full monorepo green: 703 tests across 20 packages, build clean.

## 2026-08-25 (login hang, integrations discoverability, demo removal)
- **Login hang, root cause**: `mountShell` used `root.append(...)` while `mountAuthScreen` uses `root.replaceChildren()`. Signing in re-boots the app with the auth screen still in the root, so the shell mounted *underneath* a full-viewport `.auth-screen`. A reload fixed it because the session already exists by then and the auth screen is never mounted. Changed to `replaceChildren`, symmetric with the auth screen. Also de-duplicated the `hashchange` listener, which is registered on `window` inside `mountShell` and would stack one per sign-out/sign-in cycle, rendering every route twice.
- Verified in-browser rather than asserted: signed-out state shows only `.auth-screen`; dispatching the real session write + AUTH_EVENT leaves `.app` present and `.auth-screen` gone with no reload; five consecutive boots leave exactly one `.app` and one `.copilot-overlay`.
- **Integrations were invisible** because they were a section at the bottom of Settings, below API connection, deploy target and branding. Promoted to their own nav route (`integrations`, `plug` icon) between Local SEO and Clients. Settings keeps a one-line pointer. Also worth noting for the user: merging PR #55 deployed nothing — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs typecheck/build/test only, there is no Pages deploy job, so the live site still serves the pre-merge build.
- **Demo removal (production readiness)**:
  - Deleted the dev-session auth bypass in `neonAuth.ts`. `signInWithGoogle`/`sendEmailLink` used to call `setSession({provider:'dev'})` on *any* failure, so an unreachable or misconfigured Neon Auth silently admitted anyone as `you@engine.dev` — and since that grants no API JWT, every view then failed and the product looked broken rather than saying "sign-in is down". Both now throw; the auth card shows the message. Dropped `'dev'` from the `SessionUser` provider union.
  - Deleted `mock.ts` / `MOCK_READINESS`. It fabricated readiness when the API was unreachable, and had gone stale — it still listed a `gsc-oauth` integration that no longer exists. Unreachable is now reported as unreachable.
  - `AUTH_MODE=disabled` is now honoured only for a loopback request URL. The docs always said "never on a deployed Worker" but nothing enforced it; one stray `wrangler secret put` opened every project route, the database URL and live SERP/LLM credit with no signal. Reads the request URL, not `Host`/`X-Forwarded-Host`, which the caller controls — there is a test for the forged-header case.
  - `getProjectId()` no longer defaults to the literal `'demo'`, which is not a uuid and made every project route answer 400 about a project the user never chose. Added `requireProjectId()` for the 28 URL-building call sites, which throws a message views already surface.
- 4 new tests; 703 across 20 packages, build clean.
- Removed the email sign-in control entirely, not just its auto-login. Probed the live Neon Auth instance: `POST /sign-in/magic-link` returns **404** — the Better Auth magic-link plugin is not enabled — so the field could never sign anyone in. Its only working behaviour was the dev-session fallback that admitted whatever address was typed. Deleted the field, the "or" divider, the mail icon and `sendEmailLink`; restoring it means enabling the plugin server-side and reverting this commit's UI hunk. Card is now Google-only.
- Verified in-browser that a failed sign-in creates **no** session: stubbed `/sign-in/social` to 500, clicked, and confirmed `localStorage['engine.session']` stayed null, the auth screen stayed up, the note rendered red with "Sign-in is unavailable (500)…", and the button re-enabled for a retry. The old code would have signed the user in at this point.
- **Blocker found while probing, for the user (Neon Auth console, not code):** `https://engine-7vv.pages.dev` is **not** in Neon Auth's trusted-origins list. `POST /sign-in/social` with that Origin returns `403 INVALID_CALLBACKURL` for both `/app/` and `/`, while `http://localhost:4321` returns 200 with a Google redirect URL. Google sign-in therefore cannot work on the deployed site at all, for anyone, regardless of this branch.
- **"Create 3 users" — cannot be done as stated, and why.** `account_members.user_id` FKs to `users.id`, which is the Neon Auth JWT `sub` claim, and `users` rows are created lazily by `upsertUser` on first authenticated request. There is no email → `sub` mapping available before a person signs in, so pre-creating memberships from three email addresses is not possible. What *was* missing is the gate: the sign-in card says "Invite-only · pre-alpha access" and nothing enforced it — `POST /accounts` lets any authenticated user create an account and become its owner.
- So implemented the invite list: `ALLOWED_EMAILS`, comma-separated, checked in `requireAuth` **after** JWT verification (deciding on an unverified `email` claim would let anyone mint their own pass). Case- and whitespace-insensitive since a human types it. A *set* list rejects a token carrying no email rather than treating "no email" as "not excluded". Unset means no allowlist — deliberate, because deny-all-when-unset is indistinguishable from auth being broken, which is the failure class this branch keeps removing; `/health/integrations` reports whether it is configured. The three addresses are deliberately **not** committed — they are personal data and belong in the Worker var.
- 10 new tests; 709 across 20 packages, build clean.
- Local setup note: `wrangler` is not on PATH (same as `pnpm`/`corepack`). It is installed as an `apps/api` devDependency, so the working invocation is `npx --yes pnpm@10 --filter @engine/api exec wrangler <cmd>` — verified, reports 3.114.17. `wrangler whoami` reports **not authenticated**, so any `secret put` needs `wrangler login` first (a browser OAuth flow the user must run themselves).
- Set `ALLOWED_EMAILS` in the gitignored `apps/api/.dev.vars` with the three addresses, for local testing. Note the local file also has `AUTH_MODE=disabled`, which short-circuits before the JWT path — so the invite gate is *not* exercised locally while that is set; `isInvited` unit tests are the real coverage.
- Correction worth recording: an earlier `cd apps/api` persisted in the shell (the Bash tool keeps cwd between calls), which made `find` report `.dev.vars` at what looked like the repo root. I wrongly concluded the file had been moved out of `apps/api`. It had not — it was always at `apps/api/.dev.vars`, untouched. The `mv` and the first `printf` both failed harmlessly against a non-existent path and nothing stray was created; verified from an absolute path afterwards. Using absolute paths for config edits from here.

## 2026-08-25 (triage: cannot create a new client)
- Reproduced nothing: `POST /accounts` and `GET /accounts` both succeed against the real Neon database via `wrangler dev` (201 / 200). Route handler, validation, `upsertUser` and `createAccount` are all correct. Test row created and deleted again.

- Checked and cleared three hypotheses: Hono's `/accounts/*` middleware pattern *does* match bare `/accounts` (probed against the installed Hono, so the routes are authenticated); `users.email` has no unique index, so `upsertUser` cannot conflict; migrations are 15 applied / 0 pending.
- Static sweep for the same class of fault: extracted all 30 dashboard `request()` paths and all 50 registered API routes and diffed them. No mismatches. Also confirmed all 40 `/projects/:projectId/*` routes now call `projectAccessError` (3 reach it via `actionTransitionHandler`), so the ownership gap noted in the old code comment is closed.
- Monorepo green: typecheck + 59 tasks, all tests pass.
- The decisive evidence is in the data: `users` holds exactly one row, `dev`. No real Neon Auth identity has ever reached this database. Any authenticated `POST /accounts` that ran the handler would have upserted one. So the failure is in the deployed environment, before the handler — not in the code on main.
- Defect found on the way: `createAccount` does two inserts (`accounts`, then `account_members`) with no transaction. A failure between them leaves an account with no members, invisible to every user and never cleaned up. `integrations.ts` is the only repository that uses `db.begin`. Live DB currently has 0 orphans.

## 2026-09-06 (product state review + credential sign-in, motion-free neon login)
- Answered the state-of-the-product questions from the code rather than the docs. Confirmed the login hang the user still sees is a **stale build**, not a regression: the `replaceChildren` fix merged in #56 exists in source, but `apps/dashboard/site/` is gitignored build output last generated 2026-07-27 and `site/app/dist/shell.js:135` still read `root.append(appEl, toastHost)`. CI runs typecheck/build/test only — there is no Pages deploy job — so the live site has served a pre-merge build throughout.
- Also re-confirmed the deployed blocker: `https://engine-7vv.pages.dev` is not on Neon Auth's trusted-origins list, so `POST /sign-in/social` is 403 for everyone. That, not any handler bug, is why creating a client failed in the deployed environment (the 2026-08-25 triage's finding that `users` held only the `dev` row).
- Built credential sign-in for the three pre-alpha users. `packages/auth/src/localAuth.ts` (roster parsing, timing-safe credential check, HMAC-SHA-256 session tokens) + `POST /auth/login` on apps/api + a third branch in `requireAuth`, tried before the JWT path and skipped entirely when `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET` is unset.
- Roster is `LOCAL_AUTH_USERS` (`email:password[:Name]`), not a literal in source. "Hardcoded" is the *set* being fixed — no signup, no reset, no recovery, no path that grows it — rather than the identities being committed; passwords in git are passwords in every clone and CI log, and the addresses are personal data. Same reasoning that kept `ALLOWED_EMAILS` out of the repo in August.
- Two decisions worth keeping. **Principals are `local:<email>`**, namespaced away from Neon Auth `sub` values, so moving someone to Google later creates a new principal instead of silently inheriting account memberships. **The `users` row is written at sign-in**, not lazily on the first authenticated request — `account_members.user_id` FKs to it, so the lazy version means the first thing a new user does (create a client) fails on a foreign key.
- Security specifics: every login rejection is byte-identical (asserted by comparing the two response bodies — an unknown address and a wrong password must not be distinguishable, or the form enumerates three people's addresses); comparison is timing-safe with a same-shape dummy for unknown addresses; session tokens carry a checked `typ` claim because a signed OAuth state shares this wire format and carries an influenceable `userId`. `ALLOWED_EMAILS` deliberately does not compose with the roster — a second list adds no gate, only a way to lock all three out.
- Login screen rewritten: email + password, dark neon (static radial gradients and box-shadows), **no motion at all**. Deleted `auth/background.ts` — a requestAnimationFrame canvas running for as long as the first screen of the product was up — plus the card entry animation, the shake-on-error and every transition in the block. The CSS block carries a comment saying adding a transition back is a decision, not a tidy-up. Google button removed (it can only 403 today); `neonAuth.ts` kept intact so restoring it is one hunk plus the console setting.
- Session token is persisted in localStorage, unlike the Neon Auth bearer token which is memory-only. Different situation: the Google token is re-mintable from a cookie at any time, and there is no cookie behind a credential sign-in — not persisting it would re-prompt for a password on every reload, which trains people to type it into anything that asks.
- Verified against **live Neon via `wrangler dev`**, not just vitest: identical 401s for wrong-password and unknown-address, real login returns a token, token opens `/accounts`, tampered token does not (403), `POST /accounts` creates a real client, and `users` gained its first non-`dev` row (`local:abgaur1996@gmail.com`). Test rows deleted afterwards; 0 orphan accounts.
- Verified in the browser: sign-in swaps the auth screen for the shell with **no reload** (`.auth-screen` 0, `.app` 1); four sign-in/sign-out cycles leave exactly one `.app`, one `.copilot-overlay`, one `.toast-host`; a client created through the Clients grid reached the database; a failed sign-in writes no session and renders the error on the card. Zero animated/transitioning elements and zero canvases by computed style; no horizontal overflow at 375px.
- One thing I could not verify honestly: Enter-to-submit. It is wired through a real `<form>` (which is also what lets a password manager recognise the field pair) and the submit path is verified via the form's own submit event, but the automation harness sends a keydown with an empty `key`, so a synthetic Enter cannot trigger implicit submission. Worth a manual check.
- 749 tests across 20 packages, build clean. `.dev.vars` seeded locally with three roster entries (one real address, two placeholders) and `AUTH_MODE=disabled` commented out so the gate is actually exercised locally.


## 2026-09-06 (createAccount transaction — the orphan-account defect from the 2026-08-25 triage)
- Fixed the defect logged on 2026-08-25: `createAccount` in `apps/api/src/repositories/accounts.ts` did two sequential inserts (`accounts`, then `account_members`) on the pool. Both now run inside one `db.begin`, following `integrations.ts` — the same shape, including the `as Promise<Account>` cast that postgres.js' `begin` return type needs.
- Why it mattered rather than being tidy: `listAccountsForUser` reaches accounts *only* by joining `account_members`, so an account whose member insert failed is invisible to every user — including the person who just created it, who sees an error and retries. Nothing lists it, nothing sweeps it, no query complains.
- Neon's pooled endpoint runs in transaction mode, so this deliberately uses nothing session-scoped: a plain multi-statement transaction, no advisory locks, no `${JSON.stringify(v)}::jsonb`.
- New `apps/api/src/repositories/accounts.test.ts`. The failure is *between* the two statements, which no fake reproduces by calling the repository normally, so it is injected: a fake driver that fails a named statement and only "commits" what a transaction callback returned without throwing. Three behavioural tests plus a source-level guard that no `insert into accounts` is tagged with the pool.
- Confirmed the test earns its keep by running it against `origin/main`'s `createAccount`: 2 of 3 fail there, pass after the fix.
- Added two invariants to `packages/db/src/schemaInvariants.test.ts`: `account_members` is the single cascading path from a user to an account (both FKs cascade, composite PK), and no migration adds a second `owner_user_id`-style path on `accounts` that could drift from it. The comment there records *why* the real guard is the transaction — the invariant is not expressible as DDL, since a check constraint cannot see another table and a FK from `accounts` to `account_members` would be circular.
- Verified against **live Neon**, not just vitest: the real `createAccount` writes an account that `listAccountsForUser` returns; a transaction whose member insert fails on a bad user FK leaves zero rows in `accounts`. Probe rows deleted. Orphan count 0 before and after, database back to 2 accounts / 2 members / 0 probe users.
- Branched off `main` (which already contains #57), not off `feat/hardcoded-login-neon-screen`.
- Pre-existing and untouched: 23 `tsc` errors and 6 test files failing to import in `apps/api` exist identically on a clean `main`. Test count went 617 → 623, all passing.
- Followed up on those 23 errors: **not a repo defect, a stale install.** `apps/api/node_modules/@engine/` was missing symlinks for exactly the five packages reporting TS2307 (`backlink`, `competitor`, `copilot`, `entity-audit`, `local`) — deps that are in `apps/api/package.json` and in the lockfile, but were never linked locally. The 18 TS2339 errors (`findings` not on `EntityAuditRunResult` and friends) were all downstream of those unresolved modules, not separate faults.
- `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` cleared every one, and *frozen* is the point: the lockfile was already right, so nothing in the repo needed changing and CI was never broken by this. Same class as the stale `dist/` on 2026-08-25 and the stale `apps/dashboard/site/` build earlier today — the third time a local artifact has presented itself as a code bug in this project, which is worth remembering before triaging the next one.
- Monorepo now fully green from this worktree: 39 typecheck tasks, 60 test files, 755 tests. The jump from 623 is the 6 previously-unimportable files (132 tests) now running.

## 2026-09-06 (follow-up: the deployed app could not be signed into at all)
- Follow-up the same day, from the user asking whether the three users could actually log in on the deployed web app. They could not, and not for a deployment reason: sign-in now goes *through* the API, but the API base URL lived only in Settings, which is behind the sign-in screen. A first-time visitor got "No API URL is configured. Set it in Settings" — advice they cannot follow. The Google flow never hit this because it talked to Neon Auth at a hardcoded default. Reproduced in the browser from a cleared localStorage before touching anything.
- Fixed both ends: `getApiBaseUrl()` falls back to `window.ENGINE_API_BASE` (bakeable into a deployed page, same pattern as `ENGINE_AUTH_BASE`), and when nothing has supplied one the sign-in card shows an "API URL" field itself, saves it before the request so a wrong password does not also cost the typed URL, and hides the field on the next visit. Verified: cleared storage → three fields → sign-in reaches the shell → sign out → two fields, URL kept.
- Documented roster editing in docs/40-Integrations.md §2b, including the three things that surprise: changing a password does not sign that person out (rotate `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET` for that), changing an *address* creates a new principal with no memberships because ids are `local:<email>`, and a malformed entry silently drops one person rather than breaking the API.

## 2026-09-07 (the API URL leaves the login screen and becomes a build input)
- User: "the login asks for an API URL as well, what's that?" then "remove it, we only keep email & password, find a fix." Correct call — yesterday's field broke the deadlock but solved it in the wrong place. The API origin is identical for all three users and fixed at deploy time, so it is a build input, not something a person types into a login form.
- `scripts/assembleSite.mjs` now reads `ENGINE_API_BASE` from the environment and writes `window.ENGINE_API_BASE` into `app/index.html`. Escaped with `JSON.stringify` plus `<` → `<` so a value cannot close the script tag early. Unset warns loudly and still succeeds — right for a local build, and on a deployed build the build log is the only place that fact can ever appear.
- `getApiBaseUrl()` resolves from three sources, most specific first: Settings (escape hatch, unchanged), the baked global (deployed), then `http://localhost:8787` when the page is on a loopback host. That last one is the reason local development now needs zero configuration: `wrangler dev` always serves apps/api there, so `pnpm build` + `wrangler dev` just works.
- Verified from a cleared localStorage on a build with nothing configured: exactly two fields render, and sign-in reaches the shell **without ever writing `engine.apiBaseUrl`** — proof the fallback did it rather than a leftover value. Precedence checked live in the page: loopback default, baked global overrides, Settings overrides that, removal returns to default. Injection checked both ways.
- Branch discipline note: #57 and #60 were both merged while I was mid-task, so this is a fresh branch off the new `main` rather than a fourth commit on a merged branch. Checked with `gh pr view` before branching, not assumed.

## 2026-09-08 (Cloudflare Pages deploy in CI — and the reason it would have shipped a dead site)
- Synced `main` (#61 merged), then added a `deploy` job to `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `needs: build` plus a `push` + `refs/heads/main` guard, so a red build is never published and a pull request never publishes at all. `cloudflare/wrangler-action@v3` (wrangler pinned to 3.114.17, matching apps/api's devDependency) does a Direct Upload of `apps/dashboard/site` to the one Pages project, `engine-7vv`, with `--branch=main` so it lands as production rather than a preview. `concurrency: pages-deploy, cancel-in-progress` so two quick merges do not race over which build ends up live.
- **Found and fixed a silent defect in #61 while verifying, not by reading it.** Ran the real build both ways: `ENGINE_API_BASE=… pnpm --filter @engine/dashboard build` bakes the origin in, but `pnpm turbo run build` — which is what CI runs — does **not**. Turbo 2 executes tasks in strict env mode, so a variable not declared in `turbo.json` is stripped before the task sees it. `assembleSite.mjs` then took the unset path, printed its "fine for a local build" warning, and **succeeded**. So the deploy job as first written would have published a site with no API origin, and the only signal would have been a warning that reads exactly like a normal local build.
- Fix in `turbo.json`: `build.env: ["ENGINE_API_BASE"]`. Declaring it also makes it part of the cache key, which closes a second hazard — a build with the var set could otherwise restore a cache entry created without it.
- Second turbo fix in the same task: `outputs` was `["dist/**"]`, but `assembleSite.mjs` writes `site/**`. On a cache hit turbo restored `dist/` and not the assembled site, so the deploy step would have published whatever happened to be on disk. Now `["dist/**", "site/**"]`.
- Proved all three behaviours rather than reasoning about them: same value twice → `FULL TURBO` and `site/` restored intact after being deleted, with the right origin in it; a changed value → cache miss and the new origin baked; through-turbo build before the fix → origin absent (`grep` count 0).
- The job carries two guards. Unset `ENGINE_API_BASE` is a **hard failure**, deliberately asymmetric with `assembleSite.mjs`'s warning: unset is correct locally (the app falls back to `http://localhost:8787`), and fatal deployed, since sign-in goes through the API. Then it re-reads the assembled `app/index.html` and refuses to publish unless `window.ENGINE_API_BASE="<origin>"` is really there — asserting the artifact, not the intent, because the failure class above is invisible to any check on the input. Simulated all three cases (exact, trailing slash, wrong value) against a real built site.
- `ENGINE_API_BASE` is a repository **variable**, not a secret: it is written into a public page so it is not confidential, and a secret would be masked in the log — hiding the one place its value can be verified. `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (needs *Cloudflare Pages: Edit*) and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` are secrets. **All three must be created by the user; nothing here can create them, and the first deploy fails loudly until they exist.**
- The deploy job skips `playwright install`, which the build job needs and it does not — the dashboard build touches no browser.
- Documented in `apps/web/README.md` under Deploy, including that `apps/api` is still deployed by hand and that `ENGINE_API_BASE` must point at wherever that Worker lives.
- Green: 45 typecheck+build tasks, 35 of 37 test tasks. The 2 failures are `@engine/crawler`'s browser-launch suites — this sandbox has no Playwright headless shell binary; CI installs chromium in the build job. Environmental, unrelated to this change, and the third time (per 2026-09-06) a local artifact gap has presented itself as a failure.
- Follow-up the same day: user set the Actions secrets/variable, and reported no Worker exists in Cloudflare. **Confirmed against the live account** (Cloudflare MCP `workers_list`): one Worker, `resourcesbyaditya`, created 2021-12-06, unrelated. `engine-api` has never been deployed — so the Pages job would have published a page pointing at nothing.
- **Flagged the URL they configured as malformed.** `https://engine-apiabgaur19965027.workers.dev` has two labels; a workers.dev origin is `<worker-name>.<account-subdomain>.workers.dev`, and the Worker is named `engine-api`, so the real one is almost certainly `https://engine-api.abgaur19965027.workers.dev` with a dropped dot. Not verifiable from here — no tool exposes the account subdomain, and with no deployed Worker there was no live URL to copy from. `wrangler deploy`'s own output is the authoritative source, and the value must match byte-for-byte in three places (the Actions variable, `GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI`, Neon Auth trusted origins).
- Added a `deploy-api` job (user chose it over deploying by hand): `wrangler deploy` on `apps/api` via `cloudflare/wrangler-action@v3`, same wrangler pin, same push/`main` guard. The Pages job now `needs: [build, deploy-api]` so the API is live before the page pointing at it is — publishing a dashboard whose backend failed to deploy is precisely the failure this workflow exists to stop.
- Verified the bundle before writing the job rather than after: `wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir=…` needs no credentials and succeeded, 455.85 KiB / 108.77 KiB gzipped, with `AUTH_JWKS_URL` bound. That also proves the ordering requirement encoded in the job — wrangler bundles `src/index.ts` directly, but its `@engine/*` imports resolve to those packages' `dist/`, so `turbo run build --filter=@engine/api` has to run first.
- **Code only, deliberately.** Worker secrets are set once against the Worker and survive later deploys; routing them through CI would put the database URL and three people's passwords in a second system to leak from. Minimum for sign-in: `DATABASE_URL`, `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET`, `LOCAL_AUTH_USERS` — without the last two `POST /auth/login` returns a clear "not configured on this deployment", not a crash. Also told the user the API token now needs **Workers Scripts: Edit** on top of Pages: Edit.
- **The PR's own checks overturned a premise this work rested on.** #62 carries a "Cloudflare Pages" check pointing at a Pages project named `engineai` — and so did #61 and #59, each with its own deployment UUID. So a git-connected Pages project has been building this repo since at least 2026-09-06; "merging deploys nothing" was only half true, and the docs' claim of "**one** Pages project (`engine-7vv`)" was stale. Did not merge on discovering this: the job as written targeted `engine-7vv`, which would have meant a green CI and an unchanged live site — the exact failure class this whole change exists to remove.
- What was establishable from here: `engine-7vv.pages.dev` resolves, `engineai.pages.dev` does not (ENOTFOUND), only `engineai` posts GitHub checks, and the egress proxy blocks `pages.dev` so neither page could actually be loaded. Asked the user rather than guessing; they confirmed **`engineai` is the live project now**. Retargeted `--project-name` and corrected `apps/web/README.md`, which still described `engine-7vv` as the only project.
- Corrected myself on the mechanism: I had told the user Direct Upload is rejected against a git-connected project. Cloudflare's docs say otherwise — `wrangler pages deploy` works there and infers the branch from the git integration — so no rework was needed, only the project name.
- The real consequence is a race, not a rejection: Cloudflare's own git build and this workflow can both publish to `engineai`, and only one of them bakes `ENGINE_API_BASE` in or refuses to publish without it. Automatic deployments must stay disabled on the project so CI is the sole publisher. Recorded in the workflow comment and the README.
- **#62 merged at the commit before the retarget.** The user merged head `207f520`; `7ea00e0` (the `engineai` fix) was pushed seconds later and did not make it in. So `main` briefly carried `--project-name=engine-7vv`, and its CI run published the Pages half to the legacy project. Let that run finish rather than cancelling it — `deploy-api` creating the Worker is the thing actually wanted, and publishing to a legacy project is wrong but harmless. Fixed forward in a follow-up PR: merged `main` back into the branch rather than rebasing, since a force-push is neither necessary here nor permitted in this sandbox.
- **First real deploy ran on `main` and settled three open questions.** `deploy-api` succeeded: the `engine-api` Worker now exists (created 11:01:19Z, 455.85 KiB, cron `15 3 * * *` bound) at **`https://engine-api.abgaur19965027.workers.dev`** — the missing dot was real, and the user had already corrected the Actions variable, since the deploy log's own `Baked API origin:` line matches the Worker URL byte for byte.
- **`engineai` and `engine-7vv` are one project, not two.** Cloudflare's PR bot comment on #63 says "Deploying **engineai**" above a preview URL of `https://84cef740.engine-7vv.pages.dev`: `engineai` is the project *name*, `engine-7vv.pages.dev` the hostname it has always served. The project was renamed and the `pages.dev` subdomain, fixed at creation, was not. So `--project-name` needs `engineai` while every `engine-7vv.pages.dev` URL in the docs stays correct — corrected the README paragraph I had just written claiming one was legacy.
- **The Pages job failed for a reason I had assumed wrong.** Not the project name: `wrangler-action` probes for wrangler with `pnpm exec` and installs it when missing, and at the repo root that install is `pnpm add wrangler`, which pnpm refuses in a workspace root (`ERR_PNPM_ADDING_TO_ROOT`). `deploy-api` was immune only because its `workingDirectory: apps/api` is where wrangler is already a devDependency. Fixed by giving `apps/dashboard` the same devDependency (`wrangler ^3.99.0`, matching apps/api, resolving to the pinned 3.114.17) and pointing the step at `workingDirectory: apps/dashboard`. Verified locally: `pnpm exec wrangler --version` → 3.114.17 from that directory, and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` still passes with the updated lockfile, which is what CI runs.
- Worth noting the guard behaved exactly as designed and no better: `Require ENGINE_API_BASE` and `Verify the API origin was baked in` both passed, because they check the built artifact against the configured value. Neither can tell whether that value names a host that exists — that is what the first `deploy-api` log line is for.

## 2026-09-08 (deploy pipeline green; credentials moved from a Worker secret into Neon)
- **The deploy pipeline is complete and proven.** After #63, run 34219993713 on `main` went `build` → `deploy-api` → `deploy` all green, including *Publish to Cloudflare Pages*. Merging now deploys both halves of the product for the first time; the July-vintage hand-uploaded build era is over.
- User then asked to move the credential roster out of `LOCAL_AUTH_USERS` and into Neon, and approved seeding three named users. The Neon connector was added mid-task, which changed this from "build it and hand over a command" to "build it and apply it".
- **Migration 0016 `user_credentials`**, applied to live Neon (project `wispy-butterfly-72568636`, now 16 applied). Keyed by `user_id`, not email, and that is the load-bearing choice: the principal id is `local:<lowercased email>`, *derived*, so a lookup needs no unique index on `users.email` — an index that does not exist and cannot be added safely, since Neon Auth rows may legitimately repeat an address across providers. Applied through the repo's own bookkeeping (version/name/sha256 checksum computed the same way `plan.ts` does), not an ad-hoc insert, so the runner does not see drift on the next `migrate`.
- **`packages/auth/src/passwordHash.ts`**: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 via WebCrypto. bcrypt/scrypt/Argon2 are better primitives and none is available on Workers without shipping WASM into a bundle already at 456 KiB. Stored form is self-describing — `pbkdf2-sha256$<iterations>$<salt>$<hash>` — so the work factor is raisable without a migration and a future `argon2id$…` is a new branch in `verifyPassword`, not a rewrite.
- Iteration count is **100,000**, not OWASP's 600,000, and the reason is a real constraint rather than laziness: this derivation runs inside a Workers request with a CPU ceiling, and exceeding it does not slow sign-in down, it fails the request — a worse security outcome, because the fix people reach for is turning the hashing off. `needsRehash` upgrades each password on its owner's next successful sign-in, which is the only moment the plaintext exists to re-derive from. **Unverified on the real Workers runtime** — no way to measure it from this sandbox; if sign-in starts timing out, that number is the first suspect.
- **`POST /auth/login` reads the database first, the roster second, and the database wins.** A stored credential that fails to verify is a failed sign-in, full stop — it does not fall through to `LOCAL_AUTH_USERS`. Without that rule, changing a password in Neon would not actually change it: a stale roster entry in the Worker secret would keep admitting the old one and nothing would report the conflict. The roster survives only as a fallback for addresses with no stored credential, so local dev works with no database and the cutover did not have to be atomic.
- Timing was the fiddly part. PBKDF2 is deliberately slow, so an unknown address that returns early is measurably faster than a wrong password — tens of milliseconds, observable over the network, and the thing it leaks is which of three people's addresses are real. Hence `dummyHash()`: a syntactically valid hash nothing verifies against, derived against on every miss. Its test asserts it is *parseable*, because an unparseable one would make `verifyPassword` bail before deriving and quietly restore the gap.
- **Seeded the three users into live Neon**: `local:abgaur1996@gmail.com` (Aditya), `local:devansh.c@sorditcon.com` (Devansh), `local:ag.ravikumar18@gmail.com` (Ravi). All three were issued the same password, which is exactly the case a per-user salt exists for — verified the three stored hashes are mutually distinct, and that each accepts its password and rejects a wrong one, *before* inserting. The `dev` row remains with no credential, so it cannot sign in.
- Caught and removed a real password used as a test fixture before committing. A real credential in a test file is a real credential in every clone and every CI log — the same reasoning that kept `ALLOWED_EMAILS` and the roster out of the repo in August. Nothing in the working tree contains it now.
- 16 new tests, 85 in `@engine/auth`, monorepo otherwise green. `@engine/crawler`'s two browser suites still fail here for the known reason (no Playwright headless shell in this sandbox; CI installs chromium in the build job) — confirmed by cause, not assumed.
- **Not yet true: nobody can sign in.** `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET` and `DATABASE_URL` are still unset on the deployed Worker, so the rows are unreachable and no session token can be signed. Also outstanding: no supported path yet to add a user or rotate a password now that the database is authoritative — the next thing this needs.
- **CI caught a bug my own local run had reported green.** `routes.auth.test.ts` expects 503 when credential sign-in is not configured; my new gate turned that into a 401. The earlier `turbo run typecheck build test` said `@engine/api` passed — a stale cache hit, since the same suite fails deterministically on a direct run and with `--force`. Lesson worth keeping: for a package I just edited, run its own suite directly; turbo's summary is not evidence on its own.
- The test was right and the gate was wrong, but 401 was not the only fault. With a database configured and the roster empty, an *unreachable* database was answering "Those credentials are not valid" — a lie with a cost, since the person retypes a correct password, doubts it, and the outage reads as their mistake. Exactly the failure class the August work removed when it deleted the dev-session bypass so that unreachable is reported as unreachable.
- So `authenticate` now returns a three-way outcome — `ok` / `rejected` / `unavailable` — instead of `user | null`. Unreachable store *and* no roster configured → 503; unreachable store *with* a roster → fall back to it and keep working; a genuine miss → dummy derivation, then the roster, then 401. The dummy derivation now runs only on a real miss, not on an outage, which is correct: there is no timing signal to hide when nothing was looked up.
- Verified `--force` (0 cached): 61 tasks green, 171 tests in `@engine/api`. `@engine/crawler` still fails here for the sandbox's missing Playwright browser — and its five `crawlPage` tests are visibly green in the same CI log that failed on the auth assertion, which is the cleanest confirmation yet that the local failure is environmental.

## 2026-09-08 (why the Google cards cannot be clicked; vendor logos via logo.dev)
- **The connect buttons are disabled, and the cause is server-side, not UI.** `providerCard` renders `disabled` whenever `oauthConfigured` is false, and the API computes that in one place — `oauthClient()` returns null unless `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` *and* `GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` are all set. None of the three is in `apps/api/wrangler.toml`'s `[vars]` and CI sets none of them, so the deployed Worker has no OAuth client and every card greys out.
- Ruled out the two rival explanations rather than assuming: the deployed dashboard *does* ship the UI (fetched `engine-7vv.pages.dev/app/dist/views/googleIntegrations.js`, 16.6 KB, contains `oauthConfigured` and the "no Google OAuth client configured" string), and the view *is* routed — `shell.ts` registers `views/integrations.ts`, which renders `googleIntegrationsSection`. The screen is reachable and current.
- Second possible cause worth keeping in mind, since it produces a different screen with no buttons at all: `getAccountId()` empty renders "Pick a client from the Clients grid first". Which of the two a user is looking at is decidable from the text on screen.
- Also corrected the previous entry from outside: `POST /auth/login` on the deployed Worker answers **401, not 503**, for an unknown address. By `authenticate`'s own three-way logic an unconfigured credential store returns 503, so sign-in *is* configured on the Worker now — the "nobody can sign in" note is stale.
- **logo.dev added as the vendor mark for both integration surfaces.** `logoDomain` is a field on the two registries (`packages/config` `IntegrationDef`, `packages/connectors` `GoogleProviderDef`) rather than a lookup table in the dashboard, so a provider added later brings its own logo with no UI change — the same "edit the registry, not the copies" rule docs/40-Integrations.md already states for env vars and docs.
- The publishable key is committed as a default in `apps/dashboard/src/logo.ts`, overridable at build time via `ENGINE_LOGO_TOKEN`. It is publishable by design — readable in devtools on any site using the service, and it grants nothing but logo lookups — and a working default means a fresh checkout renders correctly with no configuration.
- **Every `*.google.com` subdomain returns the identical Google mark** (verified: `google.com`, `search.google.com`, `analytics.google.com`, `business.google.com`, `gemini.google.com` are byte-identical, md5 e17eee1f). So GSC, GA4 and GBP show the same logo and the product name is what tells them apart. Not worth faking per-product icons; the payoff is the platform grid, where six vendors are genuinely distinct, and every future non-Google provider.
- Tile keeps a white face in both themes, because most marks are dark-on-transparent and the dark theme's `--surface` is near-black. Verified in a harness against the real `styles.css` in light and dark, including a simulated Bing/HubSpot/Semrush row to check the card holds up once providers are not all Google.
- **`serper.dev`'s logo reads faint** — a light-blue mark (138,202,243) on white, 60% of its pixels pure white. logo.dev returns the same asset for `theme=light`, `dark` and `auto`, so there is no darker variant to request. Left as-is: it is decorative, `aria-hidden`, and the name sits beside it.
- `logoTile` falls back to a monogram when the domain is empty (Credential sign-in has no vendor) or when the image fails to load, so an unreachable logo.dev leaves a deliberate-looking tile rather than a broken-image glyph.
- Green after the change: typecheck 39/39, build 23/23, test 37/37 (751 tests).

## 2026-09-08 (a vendor-neutral integrations library, and the catalogue behind it)
- Built **`packages/integrations`** (`@engine/integrations`), 9 source files, 94 tests. Pure — no database, no Hono — so every rule is testable without a network or a live vendor account. The library is **additive so far**: it compiles, is fully tested, and is not yet wired into the API, which still runs the Google-specific path in `packages/connectors/src/google`.
- The organising rule, stated in the registry and enforced by the tests: **adding a provider is adding a row.** Auth is a discriminated union (`oauth2` | `api_key`) rather than an assumption, and every vendor quirk — endpoints, `prompt=consent`, client-auth style, refresh-token rotation — is data on the provider instead of a branch in shared code.
- **Two bugs found by their own tests, both real, both mine.** (1) `redact` ran the key/value rule before the scheme rule, so `Authorization: Bearer <token>` redacted the word "Bearer" and left the credential in the line. Ordering is now load-bearing and commented as such. (2) `vendorFetch` redacted every response body — including successful token responses, which *are* the credential, so every OAuth flow would have parsed `access_token: "[redacted]"`. Redaction moved to `IntegrationError`'s constructor, which is the only place a string escapes into a log, a column or a response.
- A third failure was a test artefact worth recording so it is not re-diagnosed: a stub returning one `Response` object across retries fails with "ReadableStream is locked", which looks exactly like a retry-path bug. Responses are now supplied as factories.
- **PKCE (S256) on every OAuth flow**, including confidential ones where RFC 7636 does not require it. The signed `state` already proves the callback belongs to a flow we started; it does nothing about a code leaked from browser history, a Referer header or a proxy log. Verified against the RFC 7636 appendix B vector, so a re-encoding cannot pass by being self-consistently wrong.
- **Encryption keys can now rotate.** `ENCRYPTION_KEY` was a single secret whose honest consequence was that rotating it invalidated every stored connection — which means it would never have been rotated. The keyring seals under a primary, opens under whichever key sealed the blob, and reports `staleKey` so the caller re-seals. A bare unversioned key still loads, as `v1`, so the existing deployment is unaffected.
- AAD now binds `account:provider:kind`, not `account:provider`. Without `kind`, an API-key blob moved into the OAuth column would open cleanly and then be posted to a token endpoint. There is a test for exactly that.
- `vendorFetch` closes four failure modes in one place: per-attempt timeout (a vendor that never answers otherwise holds a Worker isolate), a 256 KB body cap, retries on 429/5xx only with `Retry-After` honoured, and `redirect: 'manual'` so a credential-carrying request is never replayed to another host.
- **Migration 0017** generalises the 0014 schema. Its three-table split was right and survives untouched; what did not survive a second vendor was the `check (provider in ('gsc','ga4','gbp'))`, the `google_*` column names, `refresh_token_sealed`, and two constraints that hardcoded the same three ids. Shape rules are now expressed against `resource_scope` rather than a list of provider ids. Adds `integration_events`, append-only, with no FK to the connection — the trail must outlive the disconnect it records.
- Deliberate trade in 0017 worth flagging: moving the provider list out of a check constraint means the database no longer rejects a typo'd provider id. The application gate (`assertConnectable`) is the single replacement, and every route touching a credential goes through it.
- Corrected `schemaInvariants.test.ts`, which asserted `refresh_token_sealed` against the historical `create table` body. That assertion would have passed forever while saying nothing about the live schema after the rename; it now asserts the rename too.
- **`docs/41-Integration-Catalogue.md`** — eight providers to bring in, ordered by what the product can already use, each with what it unblocks and what it is blocked on. Bing Webmaster, WordPress, Cloudflare and Ahrefs are blocked on nothing external; Google Ads, Shopify and HubSpot need a vendor application started early.
- Green: typecheck 40/40, build 24/24, test 38/38 (845 tests, 94 new).
- **Not done, and needed before any of this is user-visible:** the API repository and routes are not ported to the library; migration 0017 is not applied to Neon; the dashboard has no API-key connect form; and no resource lister exists for any of the eight new providers.

## 2026-09-08 (the Pages/CI publish race, then the integrations port: steps 1–5)
- **"no API base URL configured" was the race this log predicted on 2026-09-07.** The live dashboard had lost its baked `ENGINE_API_BASE` — verified by fetching `engine-7vv.pages.dev/app/` and finding the tag absent, where it had been present earlier the same day. Making the repo public re-enabled Cloudflare's own git build, which publishes to `engineai` without the variable and overwrote the CI build. Both screens the user reported were one cause: no API base means no account can be created, and no account means the Google panel renders "Pick a client from the Clients grid first".
- CI's `deploy` job re-baked it and the site recovered. **The durable fix is a Cloudflare setting, not code**: automatic deployments must be off on the `engineai` Pages project so CI is the sole publisher. Flagged; not mine to change.
- **Step 1 — repository ported.** `apps/api/src/repositories/integrations.ts` now runs on `@engine/integrations`: provider as a registry-validated string, credential kinds, keyring instead of a single key, `missingScopes` instead of `hasRequiredScopes`, `IntegrationError.needsReauth` instead of `isPermanentGrantFailure`. Added `recordEvent`/`listEvents` against `integration_events`, and `getApiKeyCredential` as the one narrow path that yields a long-lived secret — documented as such, because an API key has no short-lived equivalent to hand back.
- Key rotation drains **on read**: a credential opened under a superseded key is re-sealed under the primary, and the re-seal failure is swallowed because the caller already has a working credential. A rotating vendor's new refresh token is written back on every exchange; a non-rotating one's absence is not, which is why that is a provider flag and not a null check.
- **Step 2 — routes ported**, plus two things the old flow did not have. PKCE could not ride in the signed state (it travels in a URL, so a verifier beside the code it protects protects nothing), so **migration 0018** adds `oauth_flows`: the verifier sealed with the same keyring, keyed by the state's nonce. Claiming it is a conditional UPDATE, which also makes a `state` **single-use** — a captured callback URL was replayable for ten minutes until now.
- **A test caught a real hole I had left**: the callback resolved the provider with `getProvider`, not `assertConnectable`, so a `planned` OAuth provider could have been connected through it. Fixed. That is exactly the failure a single gate exists to prevent, and it survived until a test named it.
- Two other test failures were signals, not noise. The repo's `jsonb` convention test caught me writing `${JSON.stringify(v)}::jsonb` instead of `toJsonb(db, v)`. And an assertion caught my "no encryption key is configured" message dropping the variable name — true and useless; it now names `ENCRYPTION_KEY`.
- **Step 4 — API-key connect form** in the dashboard, rendered from the registry's field descriptors so a new API-key provider needs no code there. Secret fields are `type=password` with `autocomplete=off`, so a browser does not offer to save a customer's vendor credential under *our* domain; values are read at submit, cleared from the DOM on success, and never stored client-side.
- Disconnect now reports three outcomes rather than two. A vendor with no revocation endpoint has not "refused" — there was nothing to call — and claiming access was revoked there would be a false promise. Both `revokedAtVendor` and the old `revokedAtGoogle` are returned, and the client reads either, because a Pages build and a Worker deploy never land in the same instant.
- **Step 5 — a lister seam** (`packages/integrations/src/resources.ts`) rather than a switch in the route. Listers register against a provider id; the route asks the registry. Implemented for **Bing Webmaster** and **Cloudflare** (both API-key, both unblocked externally), and the three Google listers moved to `apps/api/src/repositories/googleListers.ts` and registered the same way — so the resources route no longer branches on a provider id at all.
- A provider with no lister raises `not_configured` rather than returning an empty list: an empty picker claims "this account has nothing", which is a different and misleading statement from "we have not built this yet".
- Green: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 — **888 tests**, 182 in `@engine/api` and 106 in `@engine/integrations`.
- **Step 3 is the one I could not do.** No `DATABASE_URL` in this environment and no Neon connector in this session, so migrations 0017 and 0018 are written and ordered but **not applied**. CI does not run migrations either — nothing in `ci.yml` calls `db:migrate` — so this is a manual step and the deploy will not do it. Until it runs, the ported code is against a schema that does not exist yet: 0017 renames live columns, so the API and the migration must land together.
- Also still unbuilt: the six remaining planned providers have no lister, and none of the eight has a sync — the sync route answers 501 for anything outside gsc/ga4/gbp rather than falling through to the wrong branch.

## 2026-09-08 (the dead click, and cutting copy in favour of hover cards)
- **The dead click was a missing CSS rule, not a broken handler.** `.btn` had no `[disabled]` styling, so a disabled connect button rendered in full primary blue with `cursor: pointer` — it invited a click the browser then silently swallowed. `.card-act[disabled]` and `.auth-btn[disabled]` already existed; `.btn` was simply missed. The buttons were correctly disabled all along (no Google OAuth client), but the screen looked broken rather than unconfigured.
- **`src/hovercard.ts`** — explanation on demand. Keyboard reachable (the trigger is a real `<button>`, so Tab and Enter work), touch works (no hover on a phone, so click opens), Escape/outside-click/scroll dismiss. Positioned `fixed`, not `absolute`: the trigger sits inside panels with their own overflow, and an absolutely positioned card is clipped by the first scrolling ancestor.
- **A real interaction bug found by driving it, not by reading it.** Hover opened the card and the click a moment later toggled it straight back off — a mouse user would watch it vanish at the exact instant they clicked to keep it. Fixed with explicit pinning: hover opens unpinned, click pins, a second click closes. Verified the whole state machine in the browser: `afterHover 1 → afterClickPins 1 → afterSecondClickCloses 0 → reopened 1 → afterEscape 0`.
- Worth recording so it is not re-diagnosed: the browser harness's synthetic pointer did not land where `getBoundingClientRect` said the trigger was, which made a working component look broken. Confirmed by logging events — only `mouseleave`/`blur` arrived, never `mouseenter`/`click`. Dispatched events and screenshots are the reliable check here.
- **Copy rule adopted, applied to Integrations first**: a sentence stays on screen only if it is needed to make the next decision. Four stacked paragraphs per provider became four short chips — "Not configured", "Needs vendor approval", "Grants write access", "Setup required" — each with its full sentence one hover away. Nothing was deleted; it moved.
- Also removed: the page subtitle (it restated what the cards below show) and the "Platform wiring" blurb (the section heading and the `from /health/integrations` label already said both halves of it). The platform blurb became a hover card on the panel header.
- `healthProblem` now returns a short line plus detail rather than one long sentence, because the two have different jobs — the line has to read at a glance, the explanation only matters once someone cares.
- Deleted the now-dead `.intg-setup` rule rather than leaving it behind.
- Verified in the browser in **both themes**: cards position correctly, lists and the docs link render, and disabled buttons are visibly dimmed in light and dark.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39.

## 2026-09-08 (Engine's own OAuth client moves into the product)
- **The user was right and my previous answer was wrong.** I sent them to `wrangler secret put` for five values. Two different things were conflated: a *customer's* Google grant, which is self-service and already built, and *Engine's* OAuth client, which identifies the product to Google. Every customer consents to that one client — the way they would with Zapier or HubSpot — so no customer should ever create one. But nor should the operator need Cloudflare access for a one-time registration task.
- **Five Worker secrets become one.** `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` and `GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` move to a Settings → Platform screen; `OAUTH_STATE_SECRET` is generated on first use and stored sealed. `ENCRYPTION_KEY` stays a Worker secret **permanently** — it is the key these rows are sealed with, so storing it beside them is circular.
- **Migration 0019**: `platform_credentials` (one row per vendor, client id in the clear because it is public, secret sealed with the same keyring) and `platform_credential_events`, separate from `integration_events` because rotating the platform client silently disconnects every customer at once — a different question with a different audience.
- `clientFor` was already the single place resolving the OAuth client, which is why this stayed contained: it now reads the database first and falls back to the environment. The fallback is deliberate — an existing deployment keeps working, and local dev needs no admin session.
- **A test caught a real security hole before it shipped**: `/platform/*` was not behind `requireAuth`, so the admin check was reading `user.email` with no authenticated user. It failed closed as a 500 rather than leaking, but the gate was comparing against nothing. Now gated in `index.ts` alongside `/projects/*` and `/accounts/*`.
- Gate is `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS`, **not** `ALLOWED_EMAILS` — reusing the invite list would make every customer a platform admin the moment one is invited. Unset means nobody, not everybody. Non-admins get **404, not 403**: a 403 confirms the screen exists and that this deployment has administrators, to someone who by definition is not one.
- **Fixed the bug from the ship-readiness review that caused the original complaint.** `projectRow` set only the project id, so Integrations reported "Pick a client from the Clients grid first" to a customer who had just picked one from that grid. A project belongs to exactly one account, so both ids are now set together — there is no state where knowing one and not the other is correct.
- Three existing tests changed contract, all deliberately: the 503 messages no longer name `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` or `OAUTH_STATE_SECRET`, because neither is how a deployment is configured any more. The property each protected — refuse to start a flow that cannot complete — is unchanged and still asserted, now by `reason` rather than by matching prose.
- **One ordering regression I introduced and then fixed.** Moving the client lookup into the database put it after `requireOwner`, which throws on an unreachable database, so an unconfigured deployment answered 500 instead of naming its own missing configuration. `clientFor` and `resolveStateSecret` both swallow database failure and report "not configured", so they now run first. The old property — 503 without touching the database at all — is gone and cannot come back, because the client now lives in a table.
- The redirect URI is derived from the request origin and shown with a Copy button. A mistyped redirect URI is the most common OAuth setup failure and the vendor compares it byte for byte, so it should never be typed.
- The client secret is never prefilled, even when one is stored. The API cannot return it, and a masked placeholder that submits blank is how a secret gets silently cleared during an unrelated edit. Verified in the browser across all three states.
- Kept another session's CI `migrate` job, which closes the manual-migration gap I flagged earlier. It needs a `DATABASE_URL` repository secret or `deploy-api` will not run.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39.

## 2026-09-08 (two user types: platform admin and user)
- **"Admin" was one word covering two unrelated questions**, and separating them is most of this change. `account_members.role` (owner/member) is a role *inside one customer account* and already existed. `users.platform_role` (admin/user) is *does this person work on Engine* — new, and it decides who configures Engine's OAuth client and who promotes others. The two do not interact.
- **Migration 0020** adds `users.platform_role` defaulting to `'user'` — a default of `'admin'` would silently promote every existing row and every row `upsertUser` creates on first sign-in, which is every customer — plus append-only `user_role_events` with no foreign keys, so a promotion record survives the deletion of either party.
- Platform role moved out of `PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` into the database. The env list survives as **bootstrap only**, consulted just for a user with no row yet, because the screen that creates users is itself admin-only and someone has to be first. **The stored role wins** — if the list overrode the database, an admin demoted in the product would keep access until someone edited a Cloudflare variable.
- An unreachable database **denies** rather than falling back to the env list. The tempting fallback would hand access back to a demoted admin every time the database hiccupped.
- **Two refusals, both in one transaction with the read that justifies them**: you cannot demote yourself (a misclick that locks a one-admin deployment), and the last admin cannot be demoted by anyone (zero admins means Engine's OAuth client can never be changed through the product again).
- **Found while gating: `/health/integrations` was readable by every signed-in customer.** It names environment variables and says which are missing — operator information a customer gains nothing from. Now admin-only, and the dashboard hides the panel rather than rendering "Could not read integration readiness", which is a worse thing to show than nothing.
- **Access decision worth recording: a platform admin gets no extra access to customer data.** Every project screen still goes through `isAccountMember`. Support access is a different decision needing a reason recorded per access, not a role flag — written into `docs/45-Roles-And-Access.md` and into the migration so it is not quietly assumed later.
- Four existing tests asserted `200` on `/health/integrations` as a stand-in for "auth admitted this". Admin-gating it broke tests about token verification, which had not changed. Retargeted at the actual claim — not `401`, not `503` — so they stay true if that endpoint's own rules change again.
- **`pnpm db:user`** creates the first admin, since the product cannot. Password is prompted, never an argument: an argument lands in shell history, `ps` output and CI logs. Refuses under 12 characters for an account that can configure the platform.
- **A real bug in that CLI, caught by running it rather than reading it.** `terminal: true` on piped stdin makes readline treat a pipe as a TTY: the second prompt never resolved, so the command hung after asking for confirmation, having created nothing. Now a TTY gets the muted prompt and a pipe gets a plain line read, which also makes it scriptable.
- **Verified end to end against a real Postgres 16** in the scratchpad (TCP on 5434 — the scratchpad path exceeds the 103-byte unix socket limit): all 20 migrations apply, `abgaur1996+admin@gmail.com` is created with `platform_role = admin`, the stored hash is `pbkdf2-sha256$100000$…`, the plaintext appears nowhere, the correct password verifies and a wrong one does not, and the short-password, mismatch and bad-role refusals all fire.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39.
- **The account exists only on that throwaway database.** Creating it on Neon is one command, which the user must run — I have no connection string.

## 2026-09-08 (week 1, item 4: the Audit screen)
- Branch `fix/audit-screen`, stacked on `fix/customer-copy` (PR #72).
- Root cause of the broken layout confirmed: the row button reused `.card-act`, which is `width: 100%` for Fix Queue cards. The Audit row now uses its own `.frow-act` (intrinsic width, same look). Measured in the browser: button 89px, row main column 802px, in both themes.
- Findings are grouped by issue type: `groupFindings` in `format.ts` (pure, tested) puts each issue once with a page count, sorts high severity first then by pages affected, and keeps the API's page order inside a group. Severity chip and the fixable pill sit on the group head; each page row shows the path as its title and the full URL under it, with its own impact and "Propose fix".
- Labels added to `ISSUE_LABELS` for the eight content and entity issue types (`not-answer-first`, `weak-eeat`, `weak-entity-coverage`, `sparse-internal-linking`, `missing-wikidata-mapping`, `missing-entity-schema`, `inconsistent-sameas`, `weak-corroboration`). The `unknown` label lost its migration number.

- Not done here, noted for later: "auto-fixable" is still shown for findings whose generator can return no action (review B5, third bullet). That needs the API to report fixability per finding honestly, not a label change.
