## 2026-09-08 (the password prompt was echoing, and a mismatch cost the whole command)
- User hit "Passwords did not match" on `pnpm db:user`. Reproduced under a real pty with `expect` rather than guessing, which found a different and worse bug than the one reported: **the password was echoed in full and left in terminal scrollback.** Patching readline's private `_writeToOutput` had silently stopped working.
- Muting now happens on a `Writable` we own, and the prompt is written straight to stdout so only the *answer* is muted. That does not depend on a Node internal, and it is testable — the test asserts the typed value appears zero times in the output.
- Also collapsed two readline interfaces into one. Closing and reopening on the same stdin can leave a buffered newline that the next interface reads as an empty line, producing exactly the mismatch the user saw without them mistyping. Could not reproduce it deterministically, so it is a probable rather than confirmed cause — but one interface is correct regardless.
- **A mistype now costs a retry, not the command.** Aborting on the first mismatch meant re-running everything, which for a prompt you cannot see is a bad trade. Three attempts, with "Those did not match" and "Too short" messages, then it gives up.
- Verified with `expect` against a pty: happy path (password appears 0 times in output), mismatch-then-retry, too-short-then-retry. Then end to end against a fresh Postgres 16 on 5435 — 20 migrations, piped path and interactive path each write a row with the right `platform_role` and a credential.
- Confirmed on Neon: 0019 and 0020 applied, 20 total, and **no admin exists yet** — all four users are `platform_role = user`. The deployed API is unaffected by the additive migrations (`/health` 200, `/auth/login` 401, `/integrations/providers` 200).
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39.

## 2026-09-08 (PR #71 Cloudflare Pages check)
- Investigated why the "Cloudflare Pages" check on PR #71 stays "in progress" (started 16:37 UTC). It is Cloudflare's git-integration preview build, not CI. Its deployment `bcbb114b.engine-7vv.pages.dev` is live and serves 200; Cloudflare never posted the completed status back to GitHub. No branch protection exists, the PR reports MERGEABLE, so the stale check blocks nothing.
- That preview has no `window.ENGINE_API_BASE` (378-byte `/app/` vs 470 on production), so it cannot be used to test the dashboard. Cloudflare's builds on main also report a Preview URL only; production `engine-7vv.pages.dev/app/` still carries the API base, so CI is the only publisher of production.
- Found the real deploy problem: CI on main fails at `migrate` because the `DATABASE_URL` repository secret is missing (only `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, and the `ENGINE_API_BASE` variable exist). `deploy-api` and `deploy` are skipped on every main push since 349746b (15:27 UTC): merges #68, #70 and #74 are not deployed. Run 34253779706 for #74 will fail the same way.
- No code changed. Fix is a repository secret the user must add (`gh secret set DATABASE_URL`), then re-run the latest main workflow.

## 2026-09-08 (week 1, item 6: where audits run — proposal only)
- Wrote `docs/46-Audit-Runner.md`. Common part under every option: an `audit_requests` queue in Postgres (migration 0021), a customer route `POST /projects/:id/audit-requests` plus a `latest` poll, service-token routes to list, claim (conditional update) and finish, and a `queue-runner` entry point in `packages/crawler` that logs request ids only. Option A: a scheduled GitHub Action every 15 minutes with `workflow_dispatch` from the API for sub-2-minute starts (recommended, about a day, no new accounts; caveat: public-repo Action logs are public). Option B: a Playwright container on Fly.io, Cloud Run Jobs or Cloudflare Containers (two to three days, private logs, a new account).
- Stopped here for the user's decision, as instructed. Nothing for item 6 was built.

## 2026-09-08 (week 1, item 6: the audit runner — built after the decision)
- Decision received: Option A with API dispatch, 50 pages default, repository stays public. Branch `feat/audit-runner`, stacked on `feat/onboarding-main` (PR #75) because it edits the onboarding view that PR adds.
- **Queue.** Migration `0021_audit_requests`: `audit_requests` with status `queued → running → done | failed`, a partial unique index so a project has one live request (the API turns the violation into a 409), and indexes for the dashboard's latest-per-project read and the runner's scan. `repositories/auditRequests.ts`: create, latest, list queued (which also fails any request running longer than 120 minutes so a dead runner cannot hold the slot), claim and finish as conditional updates.
- **Routes.** `POST /projects/:id/audit-requests` (picks the project's oldest entity unless one is named, root URL `https://<domain>`, `maxPages` 1–500 default 50, then best-effort `workflow_dispatch`), `GET …/audit-requests/latest`, and `/internal/audit-requests` list, `:id/claim`, `:id/finish` behind `requireAuth` plus a service-only gate that answers 404 to a signed-in person. New validators `checkAuditRequestBody`, `checkAuditRequestFinishBody`.
- **Dispatch.** `githubDispatch.ts` posts `workflow_dispatch` for `crawl.yml` on `main` when `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` is set (new Worker secret, documented in `wrangler.toml`); any failure is a logged reason and the schedule catches up. Five unit tests with a fake fetch.
- **Runner.** `packages/crawler/src/runQueue.ts` (`runQueue` + `createQueueApi`, seven tests) and `queueRunner.ts` (one browser per pass, exits non-zero if any request failed). Logs request ids and counts only; a crawl error goes to the API's `error` column for the customer, never to the public log.
- **Workflow.** `.github/workflows/crawl.yml`: every 15 minutes plus `workflow_dispatch`, `concurrency: crawl-queue` without cancel, 120-minute cap, fails loudly if `ENGINE_API_BASE` (variable, exists) or `ENGINE_API_TOKEN` (secret, must equal the Worker's `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN`) is unset.
- **Dashboard.** "Run audit" button in the Audit header, disabled while a request is live; a status line (`auditRequestStatusLine`, tested) on `var(--watch)` for queued/running and `var(--risk)` with the recorded error for failed; the view polls every 20 s while live and stops when the container leaves the DOM. Get started queues the first audit after creating the entity. Empty-state copy now says "Run an audit to see what to fix".
- **Verified locally end to end.** The other chat's API on 8787 had not reloaded `.dev.vars`, so a second API ran on 8789 for the test (launch config added then reverted). Migration 0021 applied to the scratch Postgres. In the browser: "Run audit" → `POST` 201, status "Audit queued…", button disabled; a second request → 409 "An audit is already queued or running for this site." Repointed the row at `http://localhost:4321` and ran `node packages/crawler/dist/queueRunner.js`: claimed, 7 pages, `done` with a run id, exit 0; the Audit page then showed no status line and the button enabled. Get started for a new client queued its first audit (201). Test rows were then marked failed so nothing crawls them.
- **Before this works in production:** add repository secret `ENGINE_API_TOKEN` equal to the Worker's `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN` (set that Worker secret if it is not), and the Worker secret `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` (fine-grained, `actions: write` on this repository) for sub-2-minute starts. Migration 0021 lands through CI's `migrate` job on merge.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (api 210, dashboard 41, crawler 39).

## 2026-09-08 (integrations, step 2: API-key providers a customer can connect today)
- Branch `feat/api-key-providers` off `origin/main` (`aebf99b`, #75 and #76 merged).
- Diagnosis behind "the integration buttons don't work": every available provider was Google, and Google needs Engine's own OAuth client registered once in Google Cloud and pasted into Settings → Platform. Nobody has done that, so the API reports no OAuth client and Connect is disabled for user and admin alike, with the reason only in a hover card.
- Bing Webmaster Tools and Cloudflare flipped from `planned` to `available` in `packages/integrations/src/registry.ts`. Both need only a customer-pasted key, both already had a resource lister, and the API-key connect form already rendered from the registry's field descriptors.
- `verifyUrl` was declared on both providers and never called, so a wrong key would have been stored and failed hours later. Added `verifyApiKey` to the library: places the key as the provider declares, calls the verify URL with an 8 s timeout, and maps 400/401/403 to `invalid_credentials` (400 included because Bing answers a bad key with 400 on a GET whose only input is the key) and anything else to `vendor_error`. The connect route calls it before `upsertConnection`; the response now carries `verified`. Five library tests.
- The API-key button disables and reads "Checking the key…" while the vendor is consulted, and restores on failure.
- Tests retargeted for the new catalogue: `registry.test.ts` and `routes.integrations.test.ts` list five available providers; the planned-provider probe uses `ahrefs` instead of `bing-webmaster`.
- Verified in the browser against a fresh local API on 8789: five cards render; the two API-key cards show their forms; a wrong Bing key → `POST …/api-key` 400, toast "Microsoft rejected this key. Check it and paste it again.", button restored, and `GET /accounts/:id/integrations` still lists no connection. Found on the way that the local `.dev.vars` `ENCRYPTION_KEY` placeholder decoded to 34 bytes, not 32, so every credential write answered 503 locally; replaced it with a valid key (local file only).
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (integrations 111, api 210).

## 2026-09-09 (week 2, item 1: a Fix Queue a customer can trust)
- Branch `feat/fix-queue-trust` off `origin/main` (`b12f8bb`). Review item B6 plus the content-rewrite item under "Security and platform notes".
- **Every card shows the change.** `ActionCard` now carries the action's `diff` and type. A card renders one line of what the fix changes in the customer's words ("The page title, as search results and AI answers show it"), then a Now/After preview clipped to 180 characters, then "See the whole change" — a dialog with a line-by-line diff. `diffLines` in `format.ts` is a longest-common-subsequence walk, five tests.
- **Titles and descriptions come from the page, not the brand name.** Migration 0022 adds `crawled_pages.headings`; the crawler already collected them and only the fix-relevant slice was stored, which is why the proposed title of every page was "Acme Dental". `proposeTitle` now leads with the page's heading (or its opening sentence when the heading is only the brand) and adds the brand as a suffix when it fits. `proposeDescription` builds from the page's opening sentences. Both refuse instead of proposing something empty of meaning, and return the reason.
- **Schema type comes from the entity.** `entities.schema_type` (migration 0022, backfilled to `Organization`) is set from a six-option picker in the customer's words at setup and editable in Settings → Your brand. A type already published on the page still wins. `Thing` is refused: `generateSchemaAction` returns a reason, and the API rejects `Thing` as a kind. Generated JSON-LD also carries the page URL and, when known, the Wikidata `sameAs`.
- **Refusals are stated.** `generateActions` returns `{ actions, skipped }`; the propose route returns `skipped` and the Audit screen and Copilot show that sentence instead of "no action could be generated — the fix needs context this crawl did not capture".
- **Content rewrites need a person.** `requiresHumanReview('content')`; `transition` throws on approving an unreviewed rewrite (409). New `POST /projects/:id/actions/:id/review` records who read it and when, and stores the reviewer's edit as the text that deploys; a service token is refused. The card offers "Read the proposed wording" — a dialog with the current copy and an editable draft — and only then shows Approve. The rewrite prompt now fences the crawled page text as untrusted data and strips the fence markers out of it first.
- Verified in the browser against the local API on 8787 and the scratch Postgres (now at 22 migrations): the three pre-existing bad proposals ("Acme Dental" as a title and as a description, `@type: Thing`) are now visible on their cards; a fresh crawl stored headings; proposing a title returned "404 The requested path could not be found — Acme Dental" (that page really 404s on the local static server) and schema returned `@type: Organization` with a `url`; a page with no heading and no body returned the refusal sentence; approving the seeded content rewrite was refused with 409 until it was read, then the edit was saved with `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` and an `edited` audit entry and the card moved to Approved. Changing the brand kind to "A business people visit in person" changed the next proposal to `@type: LocalBusiness`. Checked in light and dark and at 375px.

## 2026-09-09 (Sync now, and the nightly sync that would never have run)
- Branch `fix/sync-token` off `origin/main` (`dbe0597`). Found while the user connected Google Search Console on production for the first time: properties listed, then "Sync now" answered `Sync failed: missing bearer token`.
- Cause: `syncProvider` in `apps/dashboard/src/api.ts` hand-rolls its own `fetch` to get a 120-second budget instead of `request`'s 8, and read the token with `getApiToken()` alone — the Neon Auth path. A password sign-in stores its token in `engine.apiToken` and has no Neon session, so every roster user got a 401 before the route ran. The same omission was fixed for the branded report in #71; this was the copy that was missed.
- Fix: one `authToken()` helper (`getStoredApiToken() ?? await getApiToken()`) used by `request`, `fetchReportUrl` and `syncProvider`, so the next caller that needs a different timeout cannot repeat it.
- `readableError` gained a 401 arm: "Your session has expired. Sign in again." `missing bearer token` is the API's vocabulary for a request it could not authenticate and reached a customer's toast verbatim.
- "Sync now" now disables and reads "Syncing…" while the vendor is consulted, like every other action on that screen.
- **Second bug, found while checking whether the nightly cron would have covered for the button:** `scheduled()` gated on `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` in the environment. Migration 0019 (#68) moved Engine's OAuth client into `platform_credentials` and made the environment a fallback, so the gate skipped the nightly sync on exactly the deployments where an administrator had configured the client through the product — logging "Google integrations are not configured" while the connect flow worked. Production is one of those: the client is in the database and neither variable is set as a Worker secret, so no scheduled sync would ever have run. The gate now requires an encryption key (the real hard requirement, either form) and then asks `getPlatformClientStatus`, environment first so a env-configured deployment needs no query. A database error there propagates rather than being reported as "not configured" — an outage should show as a failed cron run, not a nightly skip.
- New `apps/api/src/scheduled.test.ts`, 4 tests over the gate: no key skips, the `ENCRYPTION_KEYS` rotation form is accepted, the database is consulted for the client, and an env-configured client needs no query. Nothing watched this handler before.
- Verified against the local API on 8787 with a password session: `syncProvider('gsc')` through the built bundle returned `401 {"error":"missing bearer token"}` before the fix and `409 {"error":"no Google Search Console property is assigned to this project"}` after it — same session, same call, now past the auth gate and into the route.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (api 218, dashboard 57).

## 2026-09-09 (week 2, item 2: deploy targets a customer owns)
- Branch `feat/github-app` off `origin/main` (`dbe0597`). Review item B7: PR export ran on one platform-wide `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which cannot reach two customers' repositories and which no customer would hand over in a form.
- **Model, decided before building.** GitHub becomes a customer-owned provider with a third auth kind, `github_app` — not `oauth2` (no authorization/token pair, no refresh token) and not `api_key` (nothing is pasted). Engine's App id and private key live once in `platform_credentials` (vendor `github`, sealed like the Google client secret; App id in `client_id`, PEM in `client_secret_sealed`, setup callback in `redirect_uri`). The per-account value is the installation id, which is not a secret, so it goes in `integration_connections.external_subject` in the clear and **no `integration_credentials` row is written at all**. Access is minted per call: a 9-minute RS256 App JWT exchanged for a 1-hour installation token, never stored.
- **No migration.** The proposal said one, to widen `credential_kind`. Reading the schema showed it is not needed: connections are read with a `left join` onto credentials, so a connection with no credential row is already visible, and `credentialKind` now falls back to the registry's declared kind rather than the column.
- `packages/integrations/src/githubApp.ts`: App JWT signing, `GET /app` for the slug, installation token minting, installation lookup, repository listing, uninstall. `toPkcs8` accepts the PKCS#1 PEM GitHub's download button actually produces and wraps it into the PKCS#8 envelope WebCrypto requires — otherwise the paste fails later with an opaque `DataError`. `iat` is backdated 60 s because GitHub rejects a token issued in the future and a Worker clock a little fast would fail intermittently.
- Routes: `connect-url` branches to an install link (`https://github.com/apps/{slug}/installations/new`), with the slug fetched from GitHub so a renamed App cannot leave a dead link; new `GET /github/setup/callback` claims the same single-use signed state, verifies the installation with GitHub before storing it, and records the connection. `startFlow`'s PKCE verifier became optional — a flow with no code exchange has none, and the row exists for replay protection alone.
- `getAccessToken` gained a `github_app` path; the resources route mints for it too, so the repository picker lists exactly what the customer ticked. Disconnect is a real uninstall (`DELETE /app/installations/{id}`), so revocation is honestly reported as supported.
- Deploy: the `github-pr` branch takes the token from the project's account connection, falling back to `GITHUB_TOKEN` with a logged warning (the user's choice), and its 503 no longer names an env var.
- **`oauthConfigured` became `vendorsConfigured` per vendor.** One boolean for every provider would tell a customer "not available yet" about GitHub whenever Google was unset, and the reverse. The route's own comment had predicted this ("`clientFor` is the one place that changes when a second vendor arrives"). The old field is still returned for a dashboard build that predates the change.
- Settings now renders one panel per identity from a `VendorForm` descriptor rather than a second copy of the Google panel: App ID, a textarea for the PEM, and the right callback path suggested per vendor.
- Tests: 12 in `githubApp.test.ts` (a real generated RSA key, the PKCS#1 wrap verified by an actual WebCrypto import, the JWT verified by an actual signature check), 3 route tests, 2 tile-state cases, and three provider inventories updated.
- Verified locally: the GitHub tile appears and reads "Not available yet" for a customer with the panel saying to ask an administrator; as an admin (promoted in the scratch database, reverted after) Settings shows a "GitHub App" panel with App ID, a private-key textarea and `http://localhost:8787/github/setup/callback` prefilled; `connect-url` answers 503 "Engine's GitHub App has not been configured" before sending anyone to GitHub; deploying a `github-pr` action answers 503 "GitHub is not connected for this client. Connect it on the Integrations screen…" with no env var named. **Not verified:** the install round trip, token minting against real GitHub, and the repository picker — all need a registered GitHub App, which is the user's one-time task.

## 2026-09-09 (week 2, item 4: Search Console and Analytics data in the product)
- Branch `feat/google-data` off `origin/main` (`16f7a5f`, after #82). Review Tier 2 "Google integrations": sync wrote three tables and nothing read them.
- **Probe, and what it could not do.** The production tokens are sealed under the production `ENCRYPTION_KEY`; the local key in `apps/api/.dev.vars` opens neither the platform client nor the refresh tokens ("sealed credential failed to open"), so no Google call could be made from this machine and the live-API probe (dimensions, 16-month limit, page-level cost, sc-domain vs URL-prefix) is not done. Limits taken from Google's documentation instead: 25,000 rows per request (`rowLimit` 1–25,000, default 1,000), `startRow` paging, the API "does not guarantee to return all data rows but rather top ones", `dataState` `final` (default) vs `all`; 1,200 queries per minute per site and per user; GA4 Data API 200,000 core tokens per property per day, 40,000 per hour, 10 concurrent requests, most requests under 10 tokens.
- **What the real rows say (production Neon, read-only).** TartanHQ 28 days: 1,350 date×query rows for 533 queries, 476 clicks, 4,403 impressions; 1,206 of 1,350 rows have zero clicks. Rows per day fall from ~190 (10–17 Aug) to 3–7 (18 Aug on): the request died part-way through a clicks-sorted response, so the days that landed late hold only the rows with clicks. That is Bug 1 measured, and it means the stored totals are biased, not just short. The Test Account project holds 651 rows, all shared with TartanHQ's 1,350 (same property, two projects). GA4: 720 rows, 11 channel groups, and GA4 now reports an **"AI Assistant" channel group** (chatgpt.com 88 sessions and 29 key events, claude.ai 7, perplexity.ai 2, gemini.google.com 2); copilot.com landed in Unassigned. Migration 0015's comment ("no channel isolates them") is out of date; the GA4 connector comment now says so. Revenue is zero in every row; `conversions` still answers as a metric name. One GA4 source value is an XSS payload (`"/><img src=… onerror=…>`), harmless here because the dashboard sets text, never HTML; a reminder that vendor strings are untrusted.
- **Bug 1, the sync.** `googleSync.ts` now shapes rows (`gscDailyRows`, `ga4DailyRows`, deduped on the key because `on conflict do update` rejects a repeated key in one statement) and writes them with postgres.js multi-row inserts, 500 rows per statement (`upsertGscDaily`, `upsertGa4Daily`). Three Search Console requests instead of two: date-only property totals land in new `gsc_site_daily` (migration 0023) because a sum over query rows is lower than Search Console's own figure (anonymised queries are left out of that dimension) and the headline has to match what the customer sees in Google. Outcome is recorded per step: `last_sync_rows` after each step, `last_sync_error` = "<step> failed after N rows were stored: …", `last_synced_at` only on a complete run. `SyncResult` carries `parts` and `backfilled`. A 28-day window fits one request; no chunking needed. **One-time backfill:** `widenForBackfill` reaches back a second 28 days when stored rows do not cover the previous period, so the comparison exists after the first sync and the nightly run stays at 28 days.
- **Not proven end to end:** syncing `sc-domain:tartanhq.com` for real needs the production key or a production session. What is proven: `googleSync.db.test.ts` (skips without `TEST_DATABASE_URL`) writes 5,600 query rows to the local Postgres in 98 ms in 12 statements, re-writes without growing, and writes totals, pages and GA4 rows. After this merges, the nightly cron (03:15 UTC) or "Sync now" on production fills `gsc_site_daily`, `gsc_page_daily`, the 56-day backfill, and sets `last_synced_at` for gsc.
- **Bug 2, the Integrations screen.** The gallery now resolves the client from the selected project (`GET /projects/:id/integrations` returns `account {id, name}`), heals the stored client id when it drifted, and says "Connections for <client>. Every site of this client shares them; another client's connections do not carry over." `GET /accounts` returns `connectedProviders` per client (one query), and a provider not connected here but connected under another client says so in its panel. The assignment line lost "· N rows" (review rule) and reads Synced 2h ago / Not synced yet / Sync did not finish: <step> / Last sync failed. Data is from 1d ago.
- **The product surface.** New `GET /projects/:id/search-traffic` (`repositories/googleMetrics.ts`): 28 days ending on the latest stored day vs the 28 before (deltas only when the previous period is covered); Search: clicks, impressions, impression-weighted position, CTR, daily clicks, top 10 queries by clicks with a brand flag, **queries within reach** (non-brand, position 4–20, ≥10 impressions, by impressions), top pages, `totalsSource` property|queries; Traffic: sessions, engaged sessions, key events, daily sessions, channels folded so every AI-assistant source (GA4's group plus a short host list, each tagged `ga4`|`engine`) is one channel, and the assistant list. `connections` says why a half is null. Pulse renders two panels under the score with a connect/choose/sync button when empty; the score is unchanged.
- **Proposed, not built:** feeding Search Console positions into the organic surface of the score (waits on journey-review decision 1); AI-assistant landing pages (needs a `landingPage × sessionSource` report and table); country/device; 16-month history; revenue; Discover. Hand-off to item 3: `withinReach` and `topQueries` are the keyword seed list — for TartanHQ that is income verification api, payroll data api, hrms integration, fake payslip detector, payslip ocr — so keyword management should offer them instead of a blank field.
- Verified in the browser against the local API on 8787 with the real rows copied into a local "TartanHQ (local copy)" client (scratch Postgres now at 23 migrations): both panels render light and dark and at 375 px with no horizontal overflow; the brand tag sits in a fixed slot so metric columns align; empty states under a client with no connections show "Connect Google Search Console" / "Connect Google Analytics"; Integrations shows the client scope line and "Synced 2h ago" with no row count. Local-only test data: a previous GA4 period at 80% and three page rows, so deltas and the pages list could be seen.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (api 243 passed + 3 skipped without a test database, dashboard 68). Opened as PR #83 (`--base main`).

## 2026-09-09 (why every non-Google screen is empty, and the v1 plan)
- User report: no data in Audit, Entity audit, Fix Queue, SERP, Competitors. Asked how to bring every tab to v1 with the functionality already built, plus two changes: Serper becomes customer-facing (reversing the platform-owned position), and the client becomes a Slack-style workspace rail above the menu with a second left drawer.
- Measured, not inferred. `wrangler secret list` on `engine-api`: only `DATABASE_URL`, `ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN`, `LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET` are set — `SERPER_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `OAUTH_STATE_SECRET` and `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` are not, so `POST /rank/poll` and `POST /ai/poll` answer 503 on production. `wrangler.toml` has one cron (`15 3 * * *`, the Google sync) and nothing else, so six audits and two pollers only ever run from a button. Crawl queue run 34337469246 claimed 1 request and audited **1 page** — the pipeline works, the crawl found one page. Local scratch Postgres: findings 90, actions 10, gsc_query_daily 1350, ga4_channel_daily 720, but serp_positions, citation_events, keyword_configs, competitor_sets, competitor_gaps, citation_opportunities, local_profiles and local_audits all 0.
- Three root causes: two vendor credentials missing in production; nothing scheduled except the Google sync; and the dependency chain unseeded (no UI writes `keyword_configs`, no entity carries prompts, no way to add a competitor by domain, no way to create a local profile).
- Decisions taken with the user: Serper becomes an `api_key` registry row with the platform key as fallback; OpenAI/Gemini stay platform-owned; the workspace rail is always visible, one client is one square; data first, then the shell.
- Wrote `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-v1-data-readiness-plan.md`: the measurements, a screen-by-screen diagnosis table, and eight sequential steps (credentials → rankings → AI answers → audits on a schedule → competitors by domain → local and crawl coverage → workspace rail → findings and verify). Step 7 supersedes the redesign plan's decision 3, which had hidden the client layer. No product code changed.

## 2026-09-09 (v1 step 1: the two vendor keys, Serper as a customer integration, Sarvam as the LLM engine)
- Branch `feat/serp-llm-keys` off `origin/main` (`d1933e1`, after #83). Step 1 of `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-v1-data-readiness-plan.md`. User decisions: Serper becomes customer-facing with the platform key as fallback; the LLM engine is Sarvam (their credits), key in `.env.neon`.
- **Keys verified live before writing code.** Serper: `GET https://google.serper.dev/account` → 200 `{"balance":2499,"rateLimit":5}`, and 403 for a wrong key, so that is the `verifyUrl` — free, and checking against `/search` would spend a search credit. Sarvam: `GET /v1/models` → `sarvam-105b`, `sarvam-105b-conversations`.
- **GLM 5.2 is not reachable on this key.** The user asked for it; every name tried (`glm-5.2`, `sarvam-glm-5.2`, `GLM-5.2`, `zai-glm-5.2`, …) is refused by the API itself: "Input 'glm-5.2' should be one of sarvam-105b, sarvam-105b-conversations". `SARVAM_MODEL` overrides the default, so switching later is a secret change and no code change.
- **Sarvam connector** (`packages/connectors/src/llmSarvam.ts`, 8 tests): OpenAI-shaped chat completions, key in `api-subscription-key`, engine `'sarvam'` added to the `LlmAnswerResult` union and to `citation_events.engine` by migration 0024 (the check constraint from 0005 listed five engines and would have rejected every Sarvam insert). Wired into `createLlmConnectors` from `SARVAM_API_KEY`.
- **The reasoning-budget trap, measured not guessed.** `sarvam-105b` fills `reasoning_content` before `content`, out of the same `max_tokens`. A two-sentence answer took 966 completion tokens; `max_tokens: 400` returned `200 OK` with `content: null` and `finish_reason: 'length'`, which would have been stored as a sample that cited nobody. The first end-to-end poll then failed the guard at 4,000 tokens on a real prompt. `reasoning_effort` exists ('low' | 'medium' | 'high') but does not bound anything: a 'low' run produced 28,793 characters of reasoning and was still truncated at 8,000 tokens, while a 'medium' run finished in 658. So no knob was added — the default budget is 16,000 and the connector throws a named error rather than recording an empty answer.
- **Serper as a customer integration**: registry row (`api_key`, `X-API-KEY`, verify against `/account`), and `apps/api/src/repositories/serpKey.ts` — `resolveSerpKey` prefers the client's connected key, falls back to the platform `SERPER_API_KEY`, and falls back on any connection problem too rather than taking rank tracking away. 4 tests. `/rank/poll` now reads the project once for both its account (whose key) and its domain, and its 503 says "not set up for this client yet" instead of naming a variable.
- **Bug found while verifying: every rank row recorded position 1.** `insertSerpPositions` took the lowest position in the SERP regardless of whose result it was, so it stored whoever ranked first — a competitor's URL — as the tracked entity's own standing, and `latestPositionsByEntity` feeds exactly that into the visibility score's organic surface. Its own doc comment said "the best organic rank ... or null if the domain didn't appear", so the domain filter had simply never been written. Now filtered to the project's domain (subdomains included, lookalikes excluded), null when absent. 5 tests. `entities.urls` is empty on every row in the scratch database and nothing populates it, so the project domain is the only usable target today — noted, not changed.
- **Verified end to end** against a local API on 8789 and the scratch Postgres (now 24 migrations): connecting Serper with a wrong key → 400 "Serper rejected this key. Check it and paste it again." with nothing stored; with the real key → `verified: true`. `/rank/poll` on the platform key, then on the client's connected key, wrote the first rows `serp_positions` has ever held; after the fix, "payslip ocr" → position 5 at `https://www.tartanhq.com/blog/payslip-ocr` and "tartanhq" → 1, where before the fix both said 1 with parseur.com's URL (those two rows were deleted). `/ai/poll` with n=2 wrote the first `citation_events` rows, engine `sarvam`, `cited: false` with no sources — Sarvam does not browse and declined to name companies, so brand-name matching is the only citation signal it can give. Citation *opportunities* (the Backlinks screen) mine `sources_cited` and will stay empty until a browsing engine is added.
- **Production secrets set** (`wrangler secret put`): `SERPER_API_KEY`, `SARVAM_API_KEY`. The Worker now holds DATABASE_URL, ENCRYPTION_KEY, INTERNAL_API_TOKEN, LOCAL_AUTH_SECRET, SARVAM_API_KEY, SERPER_API_KEY. `OAUTH_STATE_SECRET` and `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` are still unset. Rank lookups on production stop answering 503 immediately; Sarvam needs this branch merged, and migration 0024 lands through CI's migrate job.
- Readiness catalogue: new `llm-sarvam` entry marked `requiredForMvp`, `llm-openai` dropped to optional (Sarvam is what this deployment polls), the `SERPER_API_KEY` description now says it is the fallback. `.dev.vars.example` regenerated from the catalogue with `packages/config/scripts/genDevVars.mjs`; the secret list in `wrangler.toml` updated.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (api 252 passed + 3 skipped, connectors 118, integrations 123, config 10).
- Also added the user's behavioural coding guidelines to `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (global, all projects).

## 2026-09-09 (v1 step 2: rank tracking that fills itself)
- Branch `feat/rank-tracking` off `origin/main` (`6a9795b`, after #84). Step 2 of the v1 data-readiness plan.
- **The poller.** New `apps/api/src/repositories/rankPoll.ts`: `listDueKeywords` + `runScheduledRankPoll`, and a second cron `30 4 * * *` alongside the 03:15 Google sync. Daily, not weekly, because a keyword tracked at `daily` cadence cannot be polled daily by a weekly cron; the handler asks what is due, so a weekly keyword is still weekly and `on_demand` is never polled. The due windows are 20 hours and 6.5 days rather than 24 hours and 7 days: a cron fires with drift, so requiring a full 24 hours turns a daily keyword into an every-other-day one. `DEFAULT_RANK_POLL_CAP = 200` because Serper bills per lookup and the platform key is a prepaid balance shared by every client on the fallback. Key resolution is once per account, not once per keyword.
- **Two schedules, one Worker.** `scheduled` now branches on `event.cron`; anything that is not `RANK_POLL_CRON` runs the Google sync, which covers both the 03:15 trigger and a local `--test-scheduled` call that passes no cron. A route test reads `wrangler.toml` and asserts the expression matches, because the two copies drifting would mean the rank poll never runs and the sync runs twice.
- **Bug the runtime caught, not the build.** `export { app, RANK_POLL_CRON }` from the Worker entry module made workerd refuse to start: "Incorrect type for map entry 'RANK_POLL_CRON': the provided value is not of type 'function or ExportedHandler'". Only handlers may be named exports there. `typecheck` and `build` both pass on it, so this would have deployed and failed at boot. The constant moved to `rankPoll.ts`.
- **API.** `GET /projects/:id/keywords` returns every tracked keyword with its current position, the position before it, and the URL holding it — two lateral joins (`limit 1` and `limit 1 offset 1`) rather than a request per row. `DELETE /projects/:id/keywords/:keywordId`, scoped through the project, keeps the `serp_positions` history. `POST …/keywords` now enforces the plan's tracked-keyword cap: `getUsageCounters` has counted `keyword_configs` since G4 and `PLAN_LIMITS` has held the numbers, but nothing ever compared them, so a Starter account could track a thousand keywords — each one a paid lookup on every pass.
- **The screen.** SERP Inspector became Rankings: the tracked table on top (position, change, the page that ranks, Stop tracking) with a summary line, then Search Console suggestions from #83's `withinReach`/`topQueries` with a "Track for <country>" selector, then the live lookup with "Track this keyword" on its result. `rankChange`/`rankLabel` in `format.ts` (7 tests) because a rank improves when the number *falls*, so a naive delta reports every improvement as a decline; "dropped out" and "first poll" are their own cases, not "no change".
- **Two defects in my own first draft, found in the browser.** The suggestion Track button hardcoded `US`, which silently tracked an Indian site's Indian queries in the United States — now a selector, because the stored Search Console rows carry no country and it cannot be inferred. And a failed load of the tracked list rendered as "Nothing tracked yet", which is the one thing an empty state must not say; it now shows the error.
- **Verified end to end** on a local API (8789) and dashboard (4322) against the scratch Postgres: three keywords tracked through the route, then the cron fired locally (`/cdn-cgi/local/scheduled?cron=30+4+*+*+*`) and logged `scheduled rank poll: 2/2 polled` — the two due ones, correctly skipping "payslip ocr" as polled 30 minutes earlier on weekly cadence. Real TartanHQ positions: hrms integration #3, payslip ocr #5, income verification api #6, each with the blog page that holds it. Tracking from a suggestion with IN selected stored `IN · desktop · weekly`; untracking returned 200 and the keyword returned to the suggestion list. Checked in dark and light and at 375 px with no horizontal overflow (the change column drops out under 560 px). Every API call 200/201, no console errors.
- **`turbo.json` now passes `TEST_DATABASE_URL` through.** Strict env mode stripped it, so every `*.db.test.ts` skipped under `turbo run test` and reported as "skipped" whether or not a database existed — including the three googleSync ones added in #83. The API suite goes from 263 passed + 15 skipped to **278 passed, 0 skipped**.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks (api 278, dashboard 73, connectors 118, integrations 123).

## 2026-09-09 (v1 step 3: AI answers, a streamed engine, and a model the user picks)
- Branch `feat/ai-answers` off `origin/main` (`228f49b`, after #85). Step 3 of the v1 data-readiness plan, plus two additions the user asked for mid-build: streaming wherever a person triggers an AI response, and a model picker with a byline per model.
- **The prompt bank.** `entities.prompts` has been a column since migration 0001 and no route ever wrote to it — that is the whole reason `citation_events` was empty, not a scoring or polling defect. New `GET`/`PUT …/entities/:id/prompts` (whole-list PUT, so two tabs cannot merge into a bank neither displayed), trimmed and de-duplicated case-insensitively, capped at 20 prompts of 300 characters. The cap is a cost ceiling: each prompt is 3 model calls per engine per pass. `GET` also returns seeds — `generatePromptSeeds` over the keywords step 2 tracked, so the editor opens with 28 suggestions for TartanHQ rather than a blank box. No migration needed; the schema was already there.
- **The third cron.** New `apps/api/src/repositories/aiPoll.ts`: `listDuePrompts` + `runScheduledAiPoll`, `AI_POLL_CRON = '0 5 * * *'`, cap 40 prompts a run, n=3. Fires daily, each prompt due weekly (6d12h, not 7d — a cron drifts, and a full 7 days turns weekly into every-eighth-day). `scheduled` now branches three ways with the Google sync as the deliberate fallback.
- **A SQL bug the type system cannot see.** The due-check unnests `entities.prompts` and joins it laterally against `citation_events`. Written as `unnest(e.prompts) as prompt` with `where ce.prompt = prompt`, Postgres resolves the unqualified name to `citation_events.prompt` — the innermost scope — making the condition `ce.prompt = ce.prompt`, always true. Every prompt would have looked sampled and **nothing would ever have been due, forever**, with every unit test passing. Fixed to `as pr(prompt)` / `pr.prompt`. Two tests in `aiPoll.db.test.ts` cover it; I reintroduced the bug and confirmed both fail, then restored the fix.
- **Streaming, on one endpoint for both surfaces.** `POST …/ai/stream` (SSE via `hono/streaming`), two modes. Mode `prompt` streams the customer's own prompt and reports afterwards whether the brand was named. Mode `ask` computes the deterministic, cited Copilot answer **first** and sends it before a single model token, then streams a model rewording of that prose — so the answer a person can act on is on screen before the model starts, and a model that stalls or truncates costs tone and nothing else. Citations, drilldown and the Propose-fix button are rendered once from the grounded event and never touched by the stream, which is the guarantee `applyPhrasing` already gave the batch path. `PHRASING_SYSTEM_PROMPT` is now shared by both paths rather than written twice. Nothing streamed is stored: one ad-hoc sample is not a measurement, and letting these clicks land in `citation_events` would move the band by hand.
- **Why streaming needed the thinking phase, measured not assumed.** `sarvam-105b` emits `reasoning_content` before `content`. Live: first thinking token 0.28–0.46s, **first answer token 2.2–21.1s**, finish 7.5–110s. A stream forwarding only `content` shows an empty box for the entire reasoning phase — slower to first paint than a plain request. So `LlmStreamChunk` tags `thinking` vs `text` and the UI reports characters thought. Verified in the browser: at 2.5s the panel read "thought for 1,318 characters" with the answer already arriving.
- **The SSE parser is its own module with its own tests** (`llmStream.ts`, 9 tests). A `data:` line is not guaranteed to arrive whole in one network chunk, so splitting each chunk on newlines drops the tail of every straddling event — which reads as an answer missing random words, not as an error. Partial lines are carried forward; `[DONE]` is a sentinel, not JSON; one malformed event is skipped rather than abandoning the stream.
- **Model control, with bylines measured against the vendor.** New `packages/connectors/src/llmModels.ts` — a closed registry, because the vendor's rejection of an unknown id enumerates every model on the account in a message a customer would see. `GET …/ai/models` serves it; the stream route refuses an id not in it. Two models, two trials each: `sarvam-105b` reasons first (answer starts 2.2–21.1s); `sarvam-105b-conversations` never emits reasoning at all (answer starts **0.44–0.63s**). The honest difference is time to the first word, **not** total time — the conversational model often writes more and finishes no sooner (28–118s) — so neither byline claims to be "faster" and neither claims a quality difference, because none was measured.
- **`sarvam-105b-conversations` rejects `max_tokens` above 8,192** ("For a larger output budget, use sarvam-105b"), so the connector's single hardcoded 16,000 default 400s on it outright. Token budget is now per model in the registry and threaded through `createStreamingLlmConnector`. This is exactly the kind of thing a hardcoded default hides until a customer picks the other option.
- **A stale `dist` served PLACEHOLDER bylines** to the running Worker while the source tests asserting `not.toContain('PLACEHOLDER')` passed — the API resolves `@engine/connectors` from built output. Caught by reading the live route rather than trusting the test.
- **The screen.** Backlinks renamed to AI answers (nav label and heading; the route id stays `offsite` — renaming a screen is not a reason to break kept links). Four panels: cited share by engine as a Wilson band with its range always beside the figure, the prompt editor with keyword-derived suggestions, "Try a prompt now" streaming with the model picker, and citation opportunities. The picker is shared with the Ask Engine bar and the choice is remembered per browser; when it differs from the poll's model the screen says which model the weekly measurement actually used.
- **The opportunities panel reports its own blindness** rather than showing an empty list. It is mined from `sources_cited`, and which engine polls decides whether that is ever populated — a real finding: the reasoning model named sources in 1 of 8 samples, while the conversational model named 11 URLs in a single answer. So the panel distinguishes no samples, no sources at all, and sparse sources, and says which. An empty list must never read as "you have no gaps to close".
- **Verified end to end** against a local API (8787) and the scratch Postgres (24 migrations, no new one): models route serves both bylines; prompts PUT de-duplicates `["best income verification api","  Best Income Verification API  ",""]` to one and refuses 21; streamed prompt mode with the reasoning model produced 124 thinking events then 339 text events, with the conversational model 1,671 text events and no thinking at all; ask mode sent `grounded` first at **18ms** and the rewording preserved every figure (42%, 4, 0%, 0–61, 5) with its two citations intact; `runScheduledAiPoll` polled 2/2 prompts, stored 6 samples, 0 failures in 250s, and re-running immediately found nothing due. The band tightened from 0–66% at 2 samples to **0–32% at 8** — Wilson narrowing on real evidence. In the browser: grounded answer visible at 584ms while the model reworded, no console errors, no horizontal overflow at 375px, dark mode resolving through tokens.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks — api **307 passed, 0 skipped** (was 278), connectors 141 (was 118), copilot 21, dashboard 73.
