- Confirmed **both plans are already tracked in the repo** (committed as `cea9d12`), so nothing needs fetching from another machine: `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-user-journey-review.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-redesign-build-plan.md` (the UI revamp, 8 steps + 6 data screens), `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-v1-data-readiness-plan.md` (8 steps), and the 84-screenshot baseline in `docs/reviews/assets/2026-09-09/`.
- Status read from the code, not the plan text: v1 steps 1–3 are shipped (#84 Serper/Sarvam keys, #85 rank tracking + cron, #86 AI answers + prompt bank + streaming). Steps 4–8 are open. The redesign plan has not started — `apps/dashboard/src/views/` still holds `pulse.ts`, `audit.ts`, `entityGraph.ts`, `offsite.ts` and no `home.ts`, and there is no `accounts.kind` migration.
- Noted the one live conflict between the plans: redesign decision 3 hides the client layer; v1 step 7 makes it a visible Slack-style workspace rail and explicitly supersedes it. V1 step 7 is the standing decision.
- Next up per the v1 order: **step 4 — run the deterministic audits (entity, off-site, competitor, local) on the crawl and the nightly cron instead of a button.**

## 2026-09-10 (v1 step 4: the four deterministic audits stop being buttons)
- Branch `feat/audits-on-schedule` off `origin/main` (`f09eb62`, after #86). Step 4 of `docs/reviews/2026-09-09-v1-data-readiness-plan.md`.
- **The measurement this fixes.** Entity, off-site, competitor and local each read facts the system already holds and call nothing external, yet each was reachable only through a button on its own screen. Production said what that means: `entity_graph_audits` held **one row, from one press, ever**. A customer's first visit is the visit that matters, and on that visit nobody has pressed anything.
- **Two triggers replace the button.** The entity audit runs when a crawl finishes — a crawl is the moment the facts changed. The other three run nightly, folded into the existing 03:15 cron behind the Google sync. **No new cron**: these need no vendor key and no clock of their own. The buttons stay as a way to ask for a fresh answer, not the only way to get one.
- **The crawl hook lives in the API, not the runner.** The plan said `runQueue.ts`. Reading it changed the decision: the runner is a public GitHub Action holding one service token, and giving it a second call to make would be a second way for the pass to be half done. The API already knows the crawl finished (`/internal/audit-requests/:id/finish`), so the API acts. It fires only on the success outcome — a failed crawl re-audits the same stale facts and would stamp a fresh timestamp on an answer nothing refreshed. Verified: a `finish` carrying `error` records no run.
- **Migration 0025, and why `max(updated_at)` was not enough.** All four audits persist only *results*, and every result table carries `updated_at`, so "when did this last run?" looks answerable without a new table. It is not: an audit that runs and finds nothing writes no row at all, so `max(updated_at)` is null and the screen says "never run" about an audit that ran ten minutes ago and came back clean. **A clean result is the answer a customer most wants to trust, so the one case the product must not misreport is exactly the case that breaks.** `deterministic_audit_runs` records the run itself — kind, trigger, findings count — whatever it found, and also drives the nightly due-check, which has the same blind spot.
- **The due-check windows are 20 hours**, the same figure and the same reason as the rank poll: a cron fires with drift, so demanding a full day turns a nightly audit into an every-other-night one. Each kind selects only entities it can speak about — off-site needs a citation sample, competitor needs a set, local needs profile facts — because an audit with no input is not a clean result, it is no result, and recording it would make "last run" a promise the screen cannot keep.
- **The screens.** Each of the four GET routes now returns `lastRun`, and each screen carries one line: "Last checked 3h ago · on the nightly pass · nothing to fix". Every word is load-bearing — before this, the only honest reading of an empty screen was "nobody pressed the button". `auditLastRunLine` in `format.ts` (4 tests). Two empty states rewritten to stop saying no audit has run.
- **Verified on the real runtime**, not just vitest: a scratch Neon branch off production (`scratch-audits-on-schedule`), migration 0025 applied, `wrangler dev` on 8789. All four GETs return `lastRun` (null before any run). `POST entity-audit` → `trigger: manual`, 3 findings; `POST offsite-audit` → manual, 5 findings. Firing `__scheduled?cron=15+3+*+*+*` logged **`scheduled audits: 1/1 run`** and the off-site row came back `trigger: "schedule"` — and it ran even though the Google sync skipped for want of an encryption key, proving the audits are not coupled to the sync's success. Firing it again immediately logged **`scheduled audits: nothing was due`**. Claim + finish through the internal routes with a real `auditRunId` recorded `trigger: "crawl"`; the same with `error` recorded nothing. The four `auditLastRunLine` outputs were read back out of the *built* bundle in the browser, not the source.
- 9 new Postgres tests (`deterministicAudits.db.test.ts`) covering candidate selection per kind, the 20-hour window at 2h/19h/21h, per-kind isolation, the clean-run record, the crawl hook and the no-double-run guarantee.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24. Tests: every package passes except one **pre-existing** failure — `googleSync.db.test.ts`'s 6,000-row test times out at 5s against a Neon branch in Singapore. Confirmed pre-existing by stashing this branch and re-running on a clean tree, where it fails identically; it was written against a local Postgres. Not fixed here (out of scope); raised as its own task.
- Did **not** sign into the dashboard in the browser: that would mean typing the account password into a form. The UI change was verified through the built bundle and the API responses instead.

## 2026-09-10 (v1 step 5: competitors by domain, and the facts that make a gap)
- Branch `feat/competitors-by-domain` off `origin/main` (`404c134`, after #87). Step 5 of the v1 data-readiness plan.
- **The plan's premise was wrong, checked not assumed.** It said to reuse `entities.kind` from #80. #80 added `schemaType` — a schema.org type — and nothing anywhere marks an entity as a rival. So the column is genuinely absent, and migration 0026 adds `entities.role` (`self` | `competitor`). Named `role`, not `kind`, because `@engine/core` already uses "kind" for the schema.org type (`ENTITY_KINDS`, `EntityKind`, `entities.schema_type`) and two meanings of one word in one table is how a future reader picks the wrong one.
- **Why the marker is not optional.** Adding competitors as plain entity rows would have been the small change, and it would have produced a wrong number rather than an error: `assembleSurfaceScores` walks *every* entity in a project and sums their positions and citations into the customer's own visibility score, so a rival would have been folded into it silently. The entity audit would have emitted findings telling the customer to fix a competitor's schema. Every brand picker would have offered rivals as the customer's own. Filtered to `'self'` at four call sites (`pulseRollup`, `entityAudit`, `copilotQuery`, the `/entities` route) and deliberately left at `'all'` in the off-site audit, where the category is the point and a rival belongs in it by definition. Tenancy `known` sets stay unfiltered: they answer "is this in this project", which is true of a competitor too.
- **The screen has been unreachable since it shipped.** Adding a competitor required picking another *tracked entity*, and every entity a customer has is one of their own brands — so there was never a second one to pick, and the view's own empty state said "track at least two entities to compare", a wall no customer could clear. `competitor_sets` is empty in production for that reason, not for want of interest.
- **`addCompetitorByDomain`**: normalizes the input, refuses the site's own address and anything that is not a host, derives a readable name (`acme-corp.co.uk` → "Acme Corp"), and **reuses an existing competitor entity for the same domain** rather than duplicating — two rows for one company would split its facts in half and halve every gap it holds.
- **The plan's done-when does not follow from the domain alone**, and this is the substantive addition. `runCompetitorAudit` compares facts the two entities *hold* — keywords, prompts, citation topics, referring domains, strength. A competitor created from a bare domain holds none, so it can never be ahead on anything and the gap table would stay empty forever. Every rank poll already buys a full SERP and discards every result that is not the customer's own; the rivals are sitting in a response already paid for. `recordCompetitorStandings` reads them: it writes the rival's position into `serp_positions` under the rival's own entity id, and unions the keywords it actually ranks for into its `keywords`. **No extra vendor call, no extra bill.** Writing those rows is only safe *because* of the role filter above — before it, they would have entered the customer's score.
- **A defect found while verifying, in existing code.** `resolveSerpKey`'s own doc says "any connection problem (not connected, revoked, a cleared credential) falls back too", and it only fell back on `ConnectionUnavailableError`. A *sealed* credential that cannot be opened throws something else — and that is the exact state every stored connection is left in by an `ENCRYPTION_KEY` rotation, which `wrangler.toml` warns about in as many words. It threw out of `resolveSerpKey` and 500'd the whole rank poll. So on rotation day, every customer who had connected their own Serper key would lose rank tracking instead of quietly falling back to ours until they reconnect. Three lines, plus a regression test that seals with one key and opens with another. Hit this for real on the scratch branch, which carries production's sealed credential.
- **Verified end to end on a scratch Neon branch** (`scratch-competitors-by-domain`, migration 0026 applied) with `wrangler dev` and a **real Serper lookup**: typing `https://www.Parseur.com/blog` created "Parseur" (`parseur.com`); typing `parseur.com` again returned the same entity id; `tartanhq.com` was refused as the site's own address; `who knows` was refused as not an address. `GET /entities` returned only `TartanHQ:self`; `?role=all` returned both. One live poll for "payslip ocr" returned a real SERP — **Parseur #1, TartanHQ #5** — and the harvest recorded Parseur at position 1 with its URL and remembered the keyword. The competitor audit then produced **1 real gap: `keyword-gap · payslip ocr`**, in one poll rather than "the next day". `GET /pulse` reported `keywordsTracked: 1`, so the rival's position stayed out of the customer's own score.
- 6 new Postgres tests (`competitorByDomain.db.test.ts`) and 1 unit test for the key fallback.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, 67/68 turbo tasks. The one failure is the **same pre-existing** `googleSync.db.test.ts` 5s timeout against remote Neon recorded on 2026-09-10 above; api is 322 passed, 1 failed (was 315 passed).

## 2026-09-10 (a db test that read as broken on a remote database)
- **`googleSync.db.test.ts`'s 6,000-row test timed out at 5,000ms against a remote Neon branch** — reproducible on a clean checkout of main, so not a regression from any recent change. It was written against a local Postgres, where the same work takes ~98ms. Given an explicit `60_000` per-test timeout (not a raised global `testTimeout` — the other suites want the tight 5s default) with a comment saying why: the assertion is about statement *count*, so link latency must not read as a failure. 60s rather than 30s so the in-test `elapsed < 30_000` guard is what fails if the batching ever regresses, instead of racing the timeout.
- **Verified against the remote Neon branch** (Singapore): 3/3 pass, the target test at **8,800ms** — 5,119ms of it the first `upsertGscDaily` alone, which is why the 5s default could never have held. The file's other two tests stay under a second, so the global default still serves them. No local Postgres on this machine (`psql` absent), so the local half of the comparison is unrun.

## 2026-09-10 (v1 step 6: what the crawl could reach, and a location a person can describe)
- Branch `feat/local-and-crawl-coverage` off `origin/main` (`0b541a4`, after #88). Step 6 of the v1 data-readiness plan, both halves.
- **Crawl coverage.** Production's last crawl audited **one page** and nothing on screen said so — the Audit view showed a thin finding list, which reads as "your site is nearly clean" when it means "we only ever saw your home page". `crawlSite` threw away everything about *how* the crawl went; it now returns `{ pages, coverage }`: robots.txt reachable, sitemap URLs found, links followed, pages blocked by robots, whether the budget ran out, and what the budget was. Carried through `reportCrawlToApi` → `POST /audit` (validated, so a malformed field cannot become a sentence nobody can explain) → migration 0027 columns on `audit_runs` → `GET /audit` → one line under the health score.
- **Every coverage column is nullable, deliberately.** Runs recorded before 0027 have no coverage, and "we did not record whether a sitemap was found" is a different statement from "no sitemap was found". `toAuditRun` returns coverage as a whole object or null rather than a half-filled one, so no caller can read a null as a zero. Verified against the existing production run on a scratch branch: `coverage: null`, and the line falls back to the bare page count.
- **`crawlCoverageLine` warns only when the warning is the story**, in the order a reader would act: a spent budget first (not a problem with their site), then the one-page crawl, then a missing sitemap. A warning shown every time trains the reader to skip the one that matters.
- **Two wording bugs found by writing the tests, not after.** A zero-page crawl was about to say "only the home page could be reached" — it reached nothing, and the health score beside it (which the API computes as **100** for zero pages) is over nothing at all, so the line now says that. And `stoppedAtLimit` was strictly `queue.length > 0`, which is false at `maxPages: 1` because the crawler never *looks* for links on its last allowed page — the run would have reported "0 links followed", blaming the site for a budget decision. Now tracked as `discoveryCutShort` and reported as the limit being hit.
- **Local: the UUID field is gone.** Assigning a Google Business Profile listing asked the customer to paste a location entity **uuid**, which nobody has and which fails as an opaque 400 if mistyped. It is now a dropdown of the project's own brands — `GET /entities` returns self only since #88, so a competitor added by domain can never be offered as a location. An account with no brand is told to add one rather than shown an empty select.
- **Local: a profile a person can fill in, and the trap under it.** The plan asked for a form so a customer without GBP can supply the facts. Building it naively would have shipped a wrong number: `reviewHealth([]) === 0` and `blendedScore` gives reviews 25%, so a hand-typed profile with a perfect listing and a consistent address would score **badly for having no review history nobody could type**. Added `reviewsSourced` to the facts; when false, review health is left `null`, the score is renormalized over the surfaces that do have input (the same thing `unifiedVisibilityScore` already does when local's weight is 0), and the review findings are not emitted — "you have unanswered reviews" is not something to say on the strength of an empty list nobody filled. Migration 0028 drops `not null` on `local_audits.review_score` so the null can be stored. The component row renders as "not measured — connect Google Business Profile" rather than a 0%-wide bar, which would say the opposite of what is true.
- **Verified on a scratch Neon branch** (`scratch-local-and-coverage`, 0027 + 0028 applied) with `wrangler dev`: the pre-existing run read back `coverage: null`; a posted run with coverage stored and read back every field; a malformed `coverage.robotsFound` was refused as `"expected a boolean, got a string"` naming the field. The two local cases, **same listing facts**: hand-typed → score **0.68**, `reviewHealth: null`, 3 findings; genuinely GBP-sourced with no reviews → score **0.51**, `reviewHealth: 0`, 4 findings (the extra one is low velocity). The null survived storage and read back through `GET /local-audit`.
- **Fixed a fragile test I wrote in #87.** `deterministicAudits.db.test.ts` asserted `second.attempted === 0` about the *whole database*, but the nightly pass is project-agnostic — any other project with due work fails it for an unrelated reason. It did exactly that on a branch carrying real data. Now asserted against this project's own due list.
- 8 new tests (6 dashboard format, 2 crawler coverage), 2 new local-package tests, 4 crawler call sites updated to the `{ pages, coverage }` shape.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, 67/68 turbo tasks; api 322 passed. The one failure at the time of writing was the pre-existing `googleSync.db.test.ts` 5s timeout against remote Neon; that landed as #89 while this branch was open, and this branch was rebased onto it.

## 2026-09-10 (PR #90 rebase, and a flaky test of mine made deterministic)
- #89 (the googleSync 6,000-row timeout) merged while #90 was open, so #90 conflicted. The conflict was `working_log.md` only — two additive entries — resolved by keeping both, #89's first since it landed first, and correcting #90's stale line that said a task for the timeout "is already running".
- **The rebase surfaced a real defect in a test I wrote in #87**, which my first attempt at a fix had not cured. `deterministicAudits.db.test.ts` asserted on the *global* summary of `runScheduledDeterministicAudits`, which walks every project in the database — so another suite's fixtures, or a row left behind by exercising the API by hand, change what it attempts. It flaked twice. A re-run went green, which is not a fix: the test was wrong by construction. Every assertion is now about this project's own due list and its own run row, and the failure list is filtered to this project. The "nothing is due afterwards" property is already proven by the 20-hour window test, so no second global pass is needed to prove it.
- Ran the API suite **three times in a row against the remote branch: 323 passed each time.**

## 2026-09-10 (v1 step 7: the workspace rail, and a phone that can reach the product)
- Branch `feat/workspace-rail` off `origin/main` (`f860009`, after #90). Step 7 of the v1 data-readiness plan, which supersedes the redesign plan's decision 3 (that had hidden the client layer).
- **What switching a client used to be.** `setAccountId` and a toast. The screen you were on did not change, so the only evidence anything had happened was a message that vanished in two seconds — on a screen still showing the previous client's numbers. Which client was open was stated nowhere else, so on any other screen the answer was "whichever one you picked last, probably".
- **`workspace.ts`**: a permanent 56px column of client squares (initials, active one marked by *shape* as well as colour, `+` to add, the user avatar at the bottom), a rail header naming the open site, and one drawer behind both. Clicking a client with exactly one site switches straight to it; more than one opens the drawer, because guessing which site was meant is the thing that made the old flow unreliable. Selecting a site calls back into the shell to re-render the route **in place** — the fix for the toast.
- **The drawer reuses `dialog.ts`** rather than reimplementing Escape, backdrop-click and focus return. Search appears only above 8 clients. A matched client is narrowed to its matching sites, so searching a domain does not hand back every other site the agency runs for that client — unless the *client name* is what matched, in which case all its sites stay.
- **The phone fix, which is the real one.** `styles.css:101` was `@media (max-width: 860px) { .rail { display: none } }` with nothing put in its place: **every destination in the product was unreachable on a phone.** The rail becomes a fixed bottom bar of icons that scrolls horizontally — all 11 items, not a chosen six, because hiding half of them is the same defect at a smaller scale. `.content` gets 64px of bottom padding so the last row is not under the bar.
- **A gap I introduced and caught in the browser.** The rail header lives inside `.rail .navlabel`, which the mobile rule hides — so on a phone nothing said which site was open and there was no way to switch, which is precisely the defect this rail exists to fix, reintroduced at a smaller size. Added a compact topbar chip shown by CSS exactly when the rail header cannot be: a collapsed rail, and every width under 860px.
- **Verified in a real browser** against a local API (8789) and a scratch Neon branch, with a dev-harness session (a display-name blob only — no password typed, no token; the API ran with `AUTH_MODE=disabled`). Desktop grid `56px 224px 1000px`, four client squares plus add and avatar; the drawer listed four clients with their sites and connected counts; clicking "Test Client Site" set **both** ids, repainted the header to "Test Client Site / Acme Agency · testclient.example", marked the AA square, and re-rendered the route. Collapsed rail → `56px 60px 1164px` and the chip appears. At 375px: `.ws-rail` hidden, chip reads "AA | Test Client Site", the bar holds all 11 routes, `scrollWidth 432 > clientWidth 375` so it scrolls, and **no page-level horizontal overflow**. Dark mode resolves through tokens. Keyboard: opening moves focus into the dialog, Escape closes it, focus returns to the chip that opened it.
- **A static-server trap worth remembering.** ES modules are cached hard per origin: after rebuilding, `127.0.0.1:4322` kept serving the previous `format.js` and threw "does not provide an export named 'clientInitials'" while `curl` of the same URL showed the new file. Re-serving on a fresh port is the reliable way to see a rebuild.
- 16 new format tests (initials, drawer filtering, the search threshold, the open-site label).
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, **test 68/68 tasks — the first fully clean run in this sequence**; api 323, dashboard 96.

## 2026-09-10 (v1 step 8: fix a whole issue at once, and let a machine confirm it landed)
- Branch `feat/findings-and-verify` off `origin/main` (`c43afe5`, after #91). Step 8, the last of the v1 data-readiness plan — both halves in one branch because they share `format.ts` and the Fix Queue card.
- **42 clicks.** A crawl reports one finding per page per issue, so a 7-page site with 6 issues is 42 findings differing only in URL, and the Audit screen offered a Propose button on every row. `POST /findings/propose-batch` proposes for a whole issue group through the *same* `proposeForFinding` the single route uses, extracted to `repositories/propose.ts` so the batch cannot drift from the one-at-a-time path. Verified on real data: three pages missing a title, one call, **three real fixes queued**.
- **Costed rewrites are refused in batch, with a sentence.** A content rewrite is a paid model call per page; "Fix on all 40 pages" must not become forty invoices from one click. `allowContent: false` in batch, and each one comes back as a skip saying why. `PROPOSE_BATCH_CAP = 50` bounds the Worker's own wall clock, and the response carries `findingsInGroup` and `findingsAttempted` so "50 of 214" is sayable rather than silently truncated.
- **`autoFixable` was answering the wrong question.** It was `actionTemplates.length > 0`, which says a fix *exists for this kind of issue*, not that Engine holds what it would take to write one. A brand with no Wikidata entry cannot be given one by editing the site; poor Core Web Vitals are a hosting problem. `MANUAL_ISSUES` names five of those and the reason, the pill reads "Manual", and the reason is printed underneath — a Manual pill with nothing after it is a dead end.
- **`ISSUE_EXPLANATIONS`**: two sentences per issue type on the group heading — what it is, and why it matters. The screen named issues in Engine's own vocabulary ("canonical-conflict") and left the reader to know whether to care.
- **The standing banner is gone.** "Set a deploy target in Settings" was shown on every visit, mostly to someone with no fix to deploy, and on the one visit it mattered it sent them away from what they were doing. `deployTargetForm.ts` extracts the form Settings already had; the first fix proposed with nowhere to land asks for it in a dialog and then proposes the fix. Settings renders the same module, so there is one copy.
- **The Verify button could never have worked.** It asked the *browser* for the deployed page's HTML — which a browser does not have and cannot fetch cross-origin. It posted an empty string, `verifyHtmlDeploy` compared that against the proposed diff, and it failed **every single time**. "Verified" has been a lane since M1.4 with nothing able to enter it. A control that cannot succeed is worse than none: it teaches the customer that deploys do not stick.
- **Verification is a machine step now.** Migration 0029 adds `kind`, `action_id` and `verified` to `audit_requests`, so one runner drains one queue. Deploying an action enqueues its own check; "Check now" enqueues the same one. The runner fetches the URL (robots.txt for a robots fix) and posts the bytes to `/internal/audit-requests/:id/verify-result`; **the matching happens in the API**, because the Action and its diff live in this database and shipping them to a public GitHub Action so it could compare them itself would send a customer's proposed content somewhere it does not need to go.
- **A collision the migration had to fix.** `audit_requests_one_live_per_project` was written when the only request was a crawl. A verify queued during a running crawl would have been swallowed as a duplicate — the fix would deploy and never be checked, which is exactly the state this work ends. Split into one live *crawl* per project and one live *verify* per action.
- **Three outcomes, not two.** `verified` is null while queued, true when the change is on the page, false when it is not — and a fetch failure is recorded as false *with the reason*, because "we could not reach the page" and "the change is not there" are different things and only one is the customer's to fix.
- **Verified end to end on a scratch Neon branch** (`scratch-findings-verify`, 0029 applied) with `wrangler dev`: batch refused with no `issueType`, refused with no deploy target, then queued 3 fixes for 3 pages; an unknown issue type 404s naming it. `verify-request` before deploy → 409 "not been deployed yet". Deploy → a verify request appeared **queued** on its own. The runner's queue listed it as `kind: verify` with its action id; claimed it; reported HTML without the change → `verified false`, "The change is not on the live page yet."; reported HTML carrying the change → `verified true` and **the action moved to `verified`** — the first time that state has ever been reachable. The machine door 404s for a signed-in person, as designed.
- 18 new tests (6 format for explanations and fixability, 5 for the Deployed card's four states, 4 crawler verify-queue, 3 API batch-route). The old `nextAction('deployed') === Verify` assertion was updated rather than left to contradict the new ones.
- Green before push: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, **test 68/68 tasks**; api 326, dashboard 105, crawler 47.


## 2026-09-10 (citation provenance: which model said it, and what "cited" meant)
- Branch `feat/citation-provenance` off `origin/main` (`51bae15`, after #92). Items 1 and 2 of the follow-up agreed after #86: record the model on every sample, and split `cited` into the two claims it was conflating. Not the plan's numbered steps — those are all merged (#84–#88, #90–#92).
- **Sync first.** Main had moved 13 commits while this was in progress. My in-flight migration was numbered 0025 and main had since landed `0025_deterministic_audit_runs` through `0029_verify_requests`, so it was renumbered **0030**. The scratch Postgres had recorded `0025` as applied against *my* SQL, which would have made main's real 0025 be skipped and 0026–0029 apply onto a database missing its tables — unwound the three columns, deleted the `schema_migrations` row, then re-migrated 0025→0030 in order. 30 migrations, no gaps, no duplicates.
- **Why the model column.** `citation_events.engine` is the vendor (`'sarvam'`), not the instrument, and a vendor's models disagree: measured across three category prompts, `sarvam-105b` named a company in one and `sarvam-105b-conversations` named companies in all three. `SARVAM_MODEL` could be changed and nothing recorded that it had been, so a band would have reported a change of instrument as a change in the brand. `LlmAnswerResult` now carries `model`, all three connectors report theirs, and the band groups per (engine, model).
- **Why the split.** `cited` was `citedByName || citedByDomain` — "the model said your name" OR "the model linked your site". Different results of different strength, and on a non-browsing engine the second is structurally unreachable, so one boolean silently changed meaning with the engine behind it. `CitationEvent` now carries both halves; `cited` stays their union so the A3 rollup and the visibility score read the same number as before.
- **Nullable, no backfill, deliberately.** The existing rows were written before anything recorded a model, and the point of the column is that unrecorded provenance must not masquerade as recorded provenance — a default would assert a fact the database never observed. Reads return `null` (not `0`) for the split when no row in a group recorded it, and the screen says "model not recorded" / "how is not recorded" rather than "linked in 0".
- **A bug in my own first draft, found in the browser.** The band names the model each group was measured on, but the model catalogue was fetched *after* the band painted — so the same model read as `sarvam-105b` on the band and "Considered" in the picker, on one screen. The catalogue is now fetched alongside visibility and the band paints once.
- **Verified end to end.** A real poll through the cron path stored 3 samples carrying `model = sarvam-105b` and `cited_by_name/domain = false`, sitting beside the 8 older rows whose model and split are NULL — the distinction visible in one query. `/ai-visibility` returns them as two groups (0–32% over 8 unrecorded, 0–56% over 3 recorded) with `creditSplit.samples: 3`, counting only rows that can be told apart rather than all 11. Screen renders both, no console errors.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, build 24/24, test 39/39 tasks — api **330** (was 326), connectors **149** (was 141), dashboard 105.

## 2026-09-10 (action plan after the v1 build, and the crawl question answered)
- User reviewed the 17 open issues found after #93 and gave a decision on each. Wrote `docs/reviews/2026-09-10-action-plan.md`: decisions table, four waves, and deferred work with a trigger per item. No product code changed.
- **The crawl question is answered, and it is a defect with two causes — not a thin site.** Production crawled tartanhq.com and stored **one** page with `internal_link_count = 0` and **515 characters** of body text. The live site's raw HTML is 809 KB with **zero** visible text, **zero** relative links, and assets on `framerusercontent.com`: it is a Framer site, rendered entirely client-side. Cause 1: `crawlPage.ts:107` navigates with `waitUntil: 'load'`, which fires before Framer hydrates, so the crawler captured a pre-hydration shell — every content finding for this customer is computed over that shell. Cause 2: `crawlSite.ts:137` derives the same-origin test from `rootUrl`, but `https://tartanhq.com` **301s to `https://www.tartanhq.com`**, so `:118` rejects every internal link on the rendered page as off-origin. Cause 2 stays invisible until cause 1 is fixed.
- **Cloudflare can send transactional email.** Cloudflare Email Service (2025) sends from a Worker via a `send_email` binding with no API keys, and `apps/api` is already a Worker. Login codes, invites and resets are transactional, which is what it is for. Two prerequisites found by trying it: the sending domain is not onboarded, and `wrangler email sending list` does not exist in the pinned `wrangler ^3.99.0` — the repo needs wrangler 4.x. Cloudflare Access one-time PIN was considered and rejected: it gates an app for known people, which is not customer sign-up with its own accounts, roles and billing.
- **ClickHouse: no, and it is not free.** No permanent free tier — a 30-day $300 trial, then Basic around $66/month at six hours a day and about $186/month continuous, storage about $25.30/TB/month. Against that, the largest table is `gsc_query_daily` at **1,350 rows**. Migration 0005 says Postgres is wrong *at scale*; at four figures it is right. Recorded a measurable trigger instead of a feeling: ~50M rows in `gsc_query_daily`, or Pulse rollup p95 over 500 ms.
- **Production is fully migrated.** Checked read-only: 30 migrations applied, highest 0030, and `citation_events` carries `model` and `cited_by_name` — #93 was merged by the user and CI deployed it. My first two production queries used column names I had invented (`status_code`, `pages_crawled`); the real schema was fine.
- The coverage columns from 0027 are NULL on every existing `audit_runs` row, because those runs predate #90's crawler. The first real coverage numbers arrive with the Wave 1 re-crawl.
- Ask Engine is renamed **Driver** and becomes a researched, scoped feature: agentic, top-level, widgets/tables/charts/rich text, citations on real marketing data. Recorded the five questions its scoping document has to answer, and kept the existing constraint that figures and citations are retrieval-built and never model-authored.
- Also recorded: recommended default cadences per tier (rank daily on paid, AI weekly, deterministic audits nightly, crawl weekly), the approach for mining brand names instead of cited URLs, and the Bing honesty gap — a customer can connect Bing today and nothing ever appears, with nothing on screen saying why.

## 2026-09-10 (wave 1a: the crawler renders the page, and follows what is on it)
- Branch `fix/crawler-renders-and-follows` off `origin/main` (`a1af6a6`, after #94). Wave 1 of the action plan, issue 18. `entities.urls` (issue 11) is not in this branch — see the two plan corrections below.
- **Baseline re-measured read-only before touching anything.** `crawled_pages` holds exactly one row for the TartanHQ project: `https://tartanhq.com/`, 515 characters, `internal_link_count = 0`. The `audit_runs` coverage columns are NULL on all five rows, as expected for runs that predate #90.
- **Cause 1, the render wait.** `crawlPage` navigated with `waitUntil: 'load'` and read the DOM immediately. It now waits for the page to paint first, racing two signals and taking whichever lands first — `networkidle`, and a poll that stops when the content stops changing. Two signals because each is fast where the other is slow: measured against tartanhq.com, `networkidle` **never fires** and spends the whole 10-second budget, while content-settling gets there in **2.2–3.7 s**; on a conventional page `networkidle` fires in well under a second and the settle poll cannot beat it (it needs three samples). Neither firing is not an error — the page has then had longer than it needed.
- **A page showing nothing is not a settled page.** The settle poll refuses to call `0:0` stable, because a framework that has not started rendering looks exactly like one that never will. Only the network signal or the deadline can separate those.
- **Cause 2, the same-site test.** Discovery compared `u.origin` against the origin of the **seed** URL. It now compares hosts with any leading `www.` stripped, anchored on the URL that actually **answered** the root request. Both halves matter: host-stripping handles apex-to-www, and re-anchoring handles a redirect to a different host entirely.
- **Discovery no longer re-fetches the page.** `discoverLinks` opened a second browser tab and re-navigated with the same `waitUntil: 'load'` — so it carried cause 1 too, and fixing `crawlPage` alone would have left discovery reading pre-hydration shells. `crawlPage` now returns the page's links alongside the record; `crawlSite` filters them. One render per page instead of two, and one copy of the wait.
- **Links come from the whole document, not `main`.** `internalLinkCount` deliberately counts only main content (B2's link-density heuristic), so discovery could not be built on it — a site's nav and footer are where the rest of it is linked from. Capped at 2,000 per page.
- **A duplicate the fix would have created.** `visited` recorded the URL *requested*. With discovery working, the root is reached again as a link to its own final address — a different string, the same page. The final URL is marked visited too.
- **Measured on the live site, before and after.** At `load`: 0 links, 0 headings. After the wait: **22 same-site links, 46 headings**, 38 KB of text. A full dry run of the real `crawlSite` against `https://tartanhq.com` with a 25-page cap: **25 pages crawled, 54 links discovered, stopped at the limit**, in 72 s (~2.9 s/page including the 250 ms politeness delay). Was 1 page.
- **What the working crawl exposes about the site**, all real findings the audit could not previously see: no robots.txt (`robotsFound: false`), no sitemap (`sitemapUrls: 0`), **zero JSON-LD on every one of the 25 pages**, `/home` is linked from the site and returns **404**, and `/trust-center` serves the `/terms-of-use` title.
- Tests: 5 new, all three of the fixable ones confirmed failing with the fixes removed. Fixtures added to `testServer.ts` — a page that renders itself only after a script runs, and a 301 to the same server under a second host (`localhost` → `127.0.0.1`), which is the two-origins-one-site shape of apex-to-www. The `/` fixture's links became relative, which is both more realistic and what makes the redirect fixture meaningful.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks; crawler **52** (was 47).

### Two corrections to the action plan, found while doing this
- **The stated reason for issue 11 is wrong.** The plan says empty `entities.urls` is "why `cited_by_domain` from #93 can never fire". It is not: `citationTargets` already includes `projects.domain`, which is `not null` and is really populated (`tartanhq.com`), so a domain target always exists. `cited_by_domain` does not fire because Sarvam does not browse and emits almost no URLs — which is §7 of the same plan. Filling `urls` with the site's own URL would not change a single citation result.
- **`entities.schema` is empty too, and nothing writes it either.** Confirmed on production: all three entities have `urls = {}` **and** zero schema blocks. The B3 entity audit's whole contract is that it reads "the crawler-resolved on-site JSON-LD" off the row, and no code path ever puts it there — so `missing-entity-schema` is asserted for every entity regardless of the site, and `sameAsConsistency` returns a free 1.0 to everyone because there is nothing to compare. That is a larger defect than issue 11 and sits on the same data path. Raised rather than folded in silently.

## 2026-09-10 (wave 1b: the crawl writes back what it saw of the entity)
- Same branch. User's decision on the correction raised above: fix `entities.schema` first, then `entities.urls`, and correct `sameAsConsistency` so filling `urls` does not make `inconsistent-sameas` fire for everyone.
- **`entities.schema` now comes from the crawl.** `crawlPage` kept only B1.4's *verdict* on each JSON-LD block (`{type, valid, errors}`) and threw the content away. `parseJsonLdNodes` returns the nodes themselves, flattening `@graph` exactly as validation does; `CrawledPage.jsonLd` carries them, `validate.ts` checks them, and `/audit` writes them onto the entity. The B3 entity audit's whole contract is reading the crawler-resolved on-site JSON-LD off the row, and nothing had ever put it there.
- **`schema` is replaced, `urls` is merged.** Schema is a statement about what the site publishes *now* — a block the site removed must be able to disappear, or a fixed problem could never be seen fixed. URLs are merged because the crawl sees the entity's own site and nothing else, while the column also holds profile URLs written by `addCompetitorByDomain` and by people.
- **`sameAs` does not mean "your own site".** schema.org states the site with `url`; `sameAs` lists the entity's *other* homes. Once the crawl put the site origin in `urls`, the unchanged check would have flagged every correctly-marked-up site. `EntityGraphFacts` gains `siteDomain`, supplied from `projects.domain`, and it is excluded from the profiles the check demands.
- **A latent bug my change made bite.** `registrableDomain` never stripped the port, so `acme.com:8443` and `acme.com` counted as two distinct sources — and the new site-domain exclusion missed on any site served on a port. One line, plus a test.
- **Verified end to end against a real `wrangler dev` on a migrated scratch Postgres**, twice, because one site could not prove both halves.
  - **The real customer.** Crawled `https://tartanhq.com` through the actual CLI → `/projects/:id/audit` route: **12 pages, 35 links found, 49 findings, health 24**. `entities.urls` became `['https://www.tartanhq.com']` — the origin that *answered*, not the apex that was asked. `schema` stayed `[]`, correctly: the site publishes no JSON-LD on any of the 25 pages crawled. Its entity audit still reports `missing-entity-schema`, but now as a measurement rather than a constant.
  - **A site that does publish JSON-LD**, served locally with an `Organization` block carrying two `sameAs` profiles. Both blocks landed on the row; the audit's `schema` component went **0 → 1**, `sameAsConsistency` stayed 1 with the site's own domain excluded, `inconsistent-sameas` did not fire, and the entity's strength went **0.42 → 0.62**.
- **A wrong turn, caught by measuring.** The first version raced `networkidle` against content-settling and took whichever landed first. A per-page trace showed `networkidle` is wrong in *both* directions on one site: it never fires on tartanhq.com's home page (spending the whole 10 s budget), and on sub-pages it fires **early** — the contact page has 27,156 characters and **zero links** one second after `load`, and only reaches its 30 links half a second later. Reading then records a site that links nowhere, which is the original defect wearing a disguise. `networkidle` is gone; the settle poll alone decides, with the quiet window raised from 0.5 s to **1 s**. The signature counts text length *and* anchors, because the two arrive separately. Cost: local static fixtures went from ~0.5 s to ~1.25 s per page; the live crawl is ~3 s/page, unchanged, because those pages need the time anyway. Evidence: before the change the same 12 pages stored body text of 5,282–20,000 characters; after, all 12 are complete.
- Tests: 13 new across four packages (4 pure + 4 Postgres for the entity-facts write, 3 for `parseJsonLdNodes`, 3 for sameAs and the own-site exclusion, 1 for the port). The 4 Postgres tests were run for real against a migrated scratch database, not left skipped.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set; crawler 55, entity-audit 18, api 334.

### Recovery and a CI flake that blocked the deploy (2026-09-10)
- **#95 was merged holding only its first commit** (`bb4bba4`), stranding the entity-facts half *and* the `networkidle` correction, so `main` briefly carried the render wait already measured to be wrong. Cherry-picked onto `fix/entity-facts-from-crawl` off the merged `main`, re-ran typecheck 41/41 and test 39/39, opened **#96**. The same thing happened again on #96, this time costing only the log entry above — the lesson is that a PR is frozen the moment it is handed over, so follow-up work needs its own PR rather than a push.
- **#96's merge failed CI, so wave 1 was merged but never deployed.** `migrate`, `deploy-api` and `deploy` were all skipped behind a failing `build`. The failure was one test — `routes.integrations.test.ts > answers 404 rather than 403 to a non-admin` — timing out at 5 s. Not caused by the change: the identical code passed on #96's own PR run.
- **The cause is a test harness that depended on DNS.** Seven suites set `DATABASE_URL` to `postgres://never.connected.invalid/db` to prove a route refuses *before* it queries. That relies on the lookup failing quickly; on CI it does not, and this test makes three role reads against it, so 3 × slow-NXDOMAIN passed 5 seconds. Locally the same test spends 177 ms, which is why it had never been seen.
- **Fixed by removing the name.** All seven now use `postgres://127.0.0.1:1/db` — a dead loopback port, refused by the kernel at once with nothing to resolve. Two assertions in `scheduled.test.ts` matched the old hostname to prove the error came from reaching the database; they now match `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:1`, which proves the same thing.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks; api 285 passing in 416 ms of test time (was 526 ms).

## 2026-09-10 (wave 0, and the first real coverage numbers)
- Confirmed the ground before starting: `origin/main` is `75f3a1f` (after #97) and `51af374` is an ancestor of it, so nothing from wave 1 is stranded.
- **Baseline re-measured read-only on production.** `crawled_pages` holds one row for the TartanHQ project — `https://tartanhq.com/`, 515 chars, `internal_link_count = 0`. All coverage columns (`robots_found`, `sitemap_urls`, `links_discovered`, `stopped_at_limit`, `max_pages`) are NULL on every `audit_runs` row. All three `entities` rows have `urls = []` and `schema = []`.
- **Two corrections to the brief's baseline.** `audit_runs` holds **18** rows, not five; only **one** of them belongs to the TartanHQ project (2026-09-09, 1 page, 5 findings, health 45). And `crawled_pages` has **no `status_code` column** — the columns are `id, project_id, entity_id, url, title, meta_description, body_text, body_html, internal_link_count, updated_at, headings` — so a crawled 404 cannot be read off the page row and has to surface as a finding.
- Branch `feat/wave-0-config-and-honesty` off `origin/main` (`75f3a1f`). All three wave-0 code changes plus the Bing honesty gap in one PR.
- **`OAUTH_STATE_SECRET` was wrongly required, and the plan's reason was right.** Verified before changing it: `resolveStateSecret` (routes/integrations.ts:189) reads the env var only as an override and otherwise calls `ensureStateSecret`, which generates 32 random bytes and stores them sealed in `platform_credentials`. `required: true` put the name in `readiness.missing`, which forced the Google row to `partial`, which held `mvpReady` false — a reported gap that did not exist. Now `required: false`, so it shows up under `optionalPresent` when someone does pin it.
- **Workers observability was genuinely absent** — no `[observability]` block at all, so a cron exception at 03:15 UTC was written nowhere. Added with `head_sampling_rate = 1`: sampling discards whole invocations, and on a Worker doing a handful of sessions plus three cron passes a day the one request that threw is the one most likely to be dropped. Config validated with `wrangler deploy --dry-run`.
- **`ALLOWED_EMAILS` is set on the production Worker.** Value is the four real people in the Neon `users` table; `dev@engine.local` was deliberately left out — it is the local dev seed, not a person, and excluding it cannot lock anyone out. Confirmed with the user before writing. `wrangler secret list` now shows 7 secrets; `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` is still the only missing one.
- **Local dry run of the merged crawler at `maxPages: 50`**, ahead of the production run, so the production numbers have something to be compared against. `crawlSite` called directly, nothing written anywhere: **50 pages in 168 s, 104 links discovered, `stoppedAtLimit: true`**, `robotsFound: false`, `sitemapUrls: 0`, `blockedByRobots: 0`, and **zero JSON-LD on all 50 pages**. Body text is 3,601–20,000 characters per page, the top figure being `BODY_TEXT_MAX_CHARS`, and every page has a real title and 7–79 headings.
- **Where the brief's expectations disagree with what the site actually serves.** The expected "25+ pages, ~54 links" was measured at a 25-page cap; at 50 it is 50 pages and 104 links, and the site is larger still because the run stopped at the limit — so the default `maxPages: 50` under-covers this customer. "`/home` linked but 404" is **wrong**: `/home` returns a full page of 20,000 characters with 20 internal links. What is true is that it serves the *contact* page's title, "Get in Touch | Tartan — Automate Enterprise Workflows with AI Agents", identical to `/contact-us`. "`/trust-center` serving the `/terms-of-use` title" is **confirmed** — both read "Terms of Use | TartanHQ".
- **One unexplained result, recorded rather than dismissed.** The very first dry run of the session returned **1 page** with 20,000 characters of body text but an empty title, zero headings and zero links — a pre-hydration Framer shell, whose inline JSON payload reads as body text. I could not reproduce it: 8 warm-browser and 5 cold-browser trials since all returned 20,000 characters, 49 internal links, 46 headings and the correct title, in 4.6–5.7 s. A mechanism that would explain it: the settle poll's signature is text length *and* anchor count, and wave 1's guard refuses to call `0:0` stable — but a shell showing `20000:0` is a stable signature the guard does not cover. Not a confirmed defect at 1 occurrence in 14 runs; worth watching in the production run, where the shape would be a one-page crawl.

### Job 1 — the production re-crawl, and what it actually found (2026-09-10)
- **The dispatch token works end to end.** The user set `GITHUB_DISPATCH_TOKEN` (a classic PAT with `repo`, which is the scope GitHub documents for `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/workflows/{id}/dispatches`) through the Cloudflare dashboard. Clicking "Run audit" queued request `ecab1aa4` at 08:27:03Z and a `workflow_dispatch` run started at **08:27:04Z**, one second later. `gh workflow run` was never needed. All 8 Worker secrets are now set.
- **The run succeeded and audited one page.** `1 request(s) queued` → `crawling up to 50 page(s)` → `done, 1 page(s) audited`, in 11 seconds. Coverage recorded `robotsFound: false`, `sitemapUrls: 0`, `linksDiscovered: 0`, `stoppedAtLimit: false`, `maxPages: 50`.
- **The reason is not the renderer. The site blocks the crawler.** The one stored page is `https://tartanhq.com/`, 515 characters, title **"ERROR: The request could not be satisfied"**, body `403 ERROR / Request blocked. … Generated by cloudfront (CloudFront)`. A CloudFront WAF block page, stored as the customer's homepage.
- **Controlled comparison: the only variable is the source IP.** The same URL with the same `EngineBot/1.0` user agent, two minutes later from this machine, returns `HTTP/2 301` (`server: AmazonS3`, `via: … cloudfront.net`). Same request from a GitHub Actions runner returns 403 Request blocked. Not the user agent: a browser UA from here gets the identical 301.
- **The site is two providers, and only the apex blocks.** `tartanhq.com` is an **S3 + CloudFront** redirect bucket that 301s to `http://www.tartanhq.com/` — note the plaintext hop, so the chain is 2 redirects. `www.tartanhq.com` is **Framer** (`server: Framer/bea9510`) and answers 200. The crawl seed is `https://${project.domain}`, so every crawl of this customer is forced through the blocked apex.
- **This corrects wave 1's reading of the baseline.** The 515-character production row was never a Framer pre-hydration shell — it was this block page. Evidence that it has been so since at least 2026-09-09: identical 515 characters, identical `findings_count` 5, identical `health_score` 45, and today's run created **no new `findings` rows** at all (the same 5 fingerprints from 09-09 were updated in place). The two code defects wave 1 fixed were real and the fixes work — measured locally at `maxPages: 50`, **50 pages and 104 links** versus 1 page before — but neither could ever have fixed production, because the request is refused before anything renders.
- **The larger defect beside it: a 403 is stored as content.** `crawlPage` captures `response.status()` (crawlPage.ts:220) and puts it on the record; `validate.ts:172` checks it; and then the insert drops it — `crawled_pages` has no `status_code` column, and the column list at `crawledPages.ts:68` does not include one. Nothing anywhere compares the status to 2xx. So an error page becomes the homepage, and B2 computed 5 findings and a health score of 45 over it: `weak-eeat score 0`, `weak-entity-coverage score 0`, `sparse-internal-linking score 0`. Those three read as "this customer's site is bad" when they mean "we were served a 403". Same class as the #86 defect, now in the data layer rather than the screen.
- **What did land, and is real progress.** The `audit_runs` coverage columns from #90 are **populated for the first time** — every one of the 18 prior rows had them NULL. And #96's entity write fired: `entities.urls` went `[]` → `["https://tartanhq.com"]`, with `schema` staying `[]`. The brief expected `["https://www.tartanhq.com"]`; it is the apex precisely because the redirect never happened, so the value is honest — it recorded the origin that answered, which today was CloudFront's block.

## 2026-09-10 (item 1: an error page is not the site)
- Branch `fix/reject-error-pages` off `origin/main` (`ce3f51e`, after #98). The user's decision on the two defects raised at the end of Job 1: do item 1 (reject a non-2xx root), then item 3 (seed the crawl at the host that answers).
- **Three changes, one per place the truth was lost.**
  - **Migration 0031 adds `crawled_pages.status_code`.** The crawler was never at fault: `crawlPage` reads `response.status()` and puts it on the record, `validate.ts:172` checks it, and the insert had no column — so the value was measured, transmitted, validated and dropped. Nullable with no backfill, for the same reason 0030 was: every existing row was written without a status, and a default of 200 would assert that those pages answered 200, which is exactly the false claim the column exists to stop.
  - **`crawlSite` refuses the crawl when the *root* answers non-2xx.** Page one is what every downstream step treats as the homepage — B2 scores it, the health score comes from it, and `entities.urls` records the origin that served it. Thrown rather than returned, because `runQueue:119` already turns an exception into `finish(id, { error })`, so the request lands as `failed` with the sentence attached. No new plumbing.
  - **Root only.** A discovered page that 404s is a real finding about the site's own links, so those are still crawled and stored with their status.
  - **`detectContentIssues` fires nothing for a non-2xx page**, matching the existing silence-not-a-false-pass contract for a page with nothing captured. This is the part that mattered most: an error page is real prose with a real heading, so it scored *badly* rather than not at all, which is how a WAF block became five statements about the customer's writing.
- Tests: 5 new (2 crawler against real Chromium + a 403 fixture shaped like CloudFront's, 1 content, 3 Postgres for the round trip — run for real, not skipped). All three fixes confirmed load-bearing by removing them and watching the tests fail. The content test also asserts the *same body at 200 still fires issues*, so it cannot pass vacuously.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks; crawler **57** (was 55), content **27** (was 26), api **342** (was 339).
- **A self-inflicted mistake worth recording.** I broke the three fixes on purpose to prove the tests catch them, then restored with `git checkout --` on uncommitted work, which reverted the fixes themselves rather than just the breakage. Redone and committed first this time. The test files survived because they were separate paths.

### Two consequences to expect, and a third defect found while checking them
- **Audits of tartanhq.com will now fail rather than lie**, until item 3 lands. That is the intended trade: `failed, the root page answered 403` is recoverable and legible; a health score of 45 computed from a block page is neither.
- **The stale row will not be overwritten by item 3.** The upsert key is `(project_id, url)`, and a successful crawl seeded at `www` writes `https://www.tartanhq.com/` — a different string from the existing `https://tartanhq.com/`. The 403 row will linger with `status_code` null beside the new rows.
- **Findings never expire, which is the third defect.** `upsertFindings` is insert-or-update on `(entity_id, fingerprint)` and **nothing deletes a finding a later crawl stopped reporting**. So the five findings computed from the block page survive any number of clean crawls. Items 1 and 3 stop new lies; they do not retract the ones already recorded. Raised rather than folded in.

## 2026-09-10 (item 3: crawl the host that serves the site)
- Branch `fix/seed-host-that-answers` off `origin/main` (`e96981c`, after #99). Verified #98's merge actually deployed before starting — `build`, `migrate`, `deploy-api` and `deploy` all success on `ce3f51e`, and production `/integrations/providers` now returns `syncsNothingYet: true` for exactly `bing-webmaster` and `cloudflare` and false for the other five. Wave 0 is live.
- **`crawlSite` is now a wrapper; the old body is `crawlSiteFrom`, unchanged.** The configured host is tried first, so a site whose apex works behaves exactly as before and pays nothing. Only a root failure moves to the `www`-toggled sibling.
- **Why a wrapper rather than a restructure.** Each attempt re-fetches robots.txt and the sitemap for the origin it is attempting. Honouring the apex's robots.txt while crawling `www` would be reading the rules of a host we are not visiting — and the apex here is a different provider from `www`, so its rules are genuinely a different document.
- **Only a root failure falls back, which needed a typed error.** `RootUnavailableError` covers both a non-2xx root and a root that cannot be fetched at all (no DNS record, refused connection, navigation timeout) — an apex with no A record is the same situation as one answering 403. Typed so a page forty links deep that times out cannot restart the whole crawl on another host.
- **An explicit `seedUrls` list disables the fallback.** The caller named the URLs it wanted; substituting a host would override a decision rather than recover from a failure.
- **The fallback test does not depend on DNS, by construction.** Seeded at `http://www.localhost:<port>/host-split`, against a fixture that answers 403 when the `Host` header starts with `www.` and 200 otherwise. If the name resolves — macOS does, and systemd-resolved synthesizes `*.localhost` per RFC 6761 — the server refuses it by host. If it does not resolve, the navigation fails. Both are a root that served no site, and both must recover by stripping `www.`. Nothing waits on a lookup to *fail*, which is the dependency #97 had to remove from seven suites.
- **`www.127.0.0.1` was the first fixture and is not a URL at all.** WHATWG parses a host whose last label is numeric as an IPv4 address, and five parts is a parse failure, so `new URL` throws. Found by the test failing with `TypeError: Invalid URL` inside `crawlSiteFrom`. Pinned as a case in the `withWwwToggled` unit test, because it reads like a valid host.
- **Regression-checked against the live site**, which is all that can be checked from here — the apex answers 301 from a residential IP, so the blocked condition that motivated this exists only on a runner. Both directions still work and now record a status: `https://tartanhq.com` → 3 pages, first `https://www.tartanhq.com/` status **200**, 23 links; `https://www.tartanhq.com` → identical. The fallback under the real block can only be proven by the next production crawl.
- Tests: 6 new (3 integration for the fallback, the both-hosts-fail message and the `seedUrls` override; 4 unit cases for `withWwwToggled`). Fallback confirmed load-bearing by disabling it and watching the test fail.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks; crawler **64** (was 57).

## 2026-09-10 (findings can now stop being true)
- Branch `feat/finding-resolution` off `origin/main` (`3bd13be`, after #100). Verified #100's merge first: `0027fdd` is an ancestor of `origin/main`. Note `5483907` is **orphaned** — I amended that commit twice to strip a stray `EOF` line from the message, so the sha I announced earlier is not the one that landed. Nothing was lost; the real tip is `0027fdd`.
- **A correction to what I told the user.** I said stale findings kept the health score at 45. That was wrong: `GET /audit` reads `healthScore` from `latestAuditRun`, and `recordAuditRun` stores the score `runAudit` computed from that run's own pages. Health is per-run and was never polluted. What stale findings polluted is the **inventory** — `listFindingsByProject` and `listFindingsByEntity` — and that is worse than a stale read, because `/actions/generate` will build and deploy a fix for any finding still listed. A problem that no longer exists could be "fixed" against a live site.
- **Migration 0032 adds `findings.resolved_at`**, nullable, null meaning open. Resolved rather than deleted for two reasons: the retraction is the evidence a fix worked, which is the Fix Queue's whole claim; and `actions.finding_id` is an FK to this table, so a delete would cascade away the action history that did the fixing. No backfill — every existing row was written when nothing could resolve anything, so all of them are open by definition, which is what null already says.
- **`resolveFindingsAbsentFrom` is scoped by page and by source, and both scopes are the safety of it.**
  - **By page.** Only findings whose evidence URL is among the URLs the run looked at. A crawl that stops at `maxPages` has said nothing about the pages it never reached — and tartanhq.com stops at 50 today, so a project-wide retraction would clear every finding on the pages the crawl skipped.
  - **By source.** `AUDITED_SOURCES` is `['technical', 'content']`, which is what `POST /audit` actually evaluates. The B3 entity audit and the local audit run from their own routes, so a page audit has no standing to declare `missing-entity-schema` fixed.
  - **Redirect hops count as examined.** A finding recorded against an apex URL that now redirects to `www` was re-checked when the crawler followed that hop; without this its URL would never appear in a crawl result again and it could never close.
  - An empty URL list retracts nothing, deliberately: a caller that posts no pages has looked at nothing, and an empty `seenFingerprints` on its own reads as "the run reported nothing", so the two together would have cleared the whole project.
- **A recurrence reopens the original row** (`resolved_at` back to null) rather than inserting a second one, so `created_at` keeps meaning "first seen" and the actions already attached stay attached.
- **`getFindingInProject` is deliberately not filtered, and the obvious one-liner would have broken verification.** It is read by the verify path (`index.ts:2233`) to get a deployed fix's URL so the page can be re-checked. Filtering resolved findings there would break verification *exactly when a fix worked* — the audit resolves the finding, then nothing can confirm it. Left open, with the consequence recorded below.
- Tests: 6 new Postgres tests, run for real. Each of the three guards confirmed load-bearing by removing it and watching the tests fail (2, 1 and 1 failures respectively).
- **A property of the tests, not the code, found twice.** The suite first shared one entity and then one project across tests, and retraction is project-scoped by design — so a finding one test deliberately left open was a finding the next test's retraction legitimately closed, and the counts depended on test order. Each test now creates its own project.
- Green: typecheck 41/41, test 39/39 tasks; api **348** (was 342).

### What this does not fix
- **The propose path can still reach a resolved finding.** `POST /findings/:id/propose` reads `getFindingInProject`, which cannot filter resolved rows without breaking verification. The hole is narrow — a resolved finding no longer appears in any inventory, so reaching it needs a stale browser tab or a direct API call — but it is real. Closing it properly needs a separate read that excludes resolved findings for the propose path only, rather than one shared accessor.
- **The five findings from the CloudFront block page will not clear themselves.** They are recorded against `https://tartanhq.com/`, and after #100 a successful crawl from CI seeds `www` directly after the apex refuses — so the apex URL appears in no page's `redirectChain` and never becomes "re-examined". They need a one-off retraction, which is a deliberate data decision and not something to hide in a code path. Same for the stale 403 `crawled_pages` row.

## 2026-09-10 (the stale data cleared, and a second CI flake that blocked a deploy)
- **#101 merged and its CI failed on `build`, so `migrate`, `deploy-api` and `deploy` were all skipped** — the exact #96 pattern the plan warns about, and the reason the merge is checked rather than trusted. Caught by looking; nothing surfaced it.
- **The failure was `@engine/auth` > `verifyJwt` > "verifies RS256 as well as EdDSA", timing out at 5031 ms.** Nothing to do with #101, which touches `apps/api` only. RSA-2048 keygen is a probabilistic prime search, so its runtime has a long tail rather than an average: measured here over 12 runs on an idle machine, **16 ms fastest, 55 ms median, 137 ms slowest** — an 8.5x spread with nothing competing for the CPU. On a shared runner the tail stretched past vitest's 5-second default.
- Re-ran the failed jobs to unblock the deploy, which then passed: `build`, `migrate`, `deploy-api`, `deploy` all green. **Neon is now at 0032** and `findings.resolved_at` exists, so #99, #100 and #101 are all live.
- Fixed permanently on `fix/rsa-keygen-timeout`: a 30-second timeout on that one test. A generous timeout rather than a smaller modulus, because the test asserts RS256 verifies *at all*, not that it is fast, and 2048 is what a real JWKS uses. It is the only RSA keygen in the suite — Ed25519 is constant-time, which is why no other test here carries a timeout.
- **Cleared the stale production data**, with the scope confirmed first. 13 findings existed for the TartanHQ project and only **5** came from the CloudFront block page — the ones evidenced against `https://tartanhq.com/` from the 09:57 run. The retraction re-selected them by the same predicates the code uses (project, source in technical/content, evidence url, still open) and **refused to run unless the selection matched the five ids shown**, rather than trusting pasted ids.
  - Resolved: `schema-missing`, `meta-description-missing`, `weak-eeat`, `weak-entity-coverage`, `sparse-internal-linking`. Rows kept with timestamps — 5 resolved rows still present, history intact.
  - Kept open, deliberately: 3 entity findings and 5 `incomplete-third-party-profile` findings. Neither came from a crawl and neither would be regenerated by an audit, since the B3 entity and local audits run from their own routes. `missing-entity-schema` is also still **true** — the site publishes no JSON-LD on any of its 50 pages.
  - Deleted the one `crawled_pages` row holding the 403 page as body text. Nothing has a foreign key to `crawled_pages`, checked before deleting; `getCrawledPage` feeds that body to fix generation, so leaving it would have let a proposed fix be built from CloudFront's error page.
- The project now has **8 open findings, 5 resolved, and 0 crawled pages** — a clean slate for the next crawl to fill.

## 2026-09-10 (the production crawl finally works, and exposes what it stores)
- **The `www` fallback works under the real block.** Request queued 10:32:36Z, `workflow_dispatch` fired 10:32:37Z, and the runner reported **`done, 50 page(s) audited`** in 5m47s — against 1 page in 11 seconds this morning. Every stored row is on `www.tartanhq.com`, so the crawler was refused at the apex by CloudFront and recovered on the sibling host, which is the thing that could only be proven on a runner.
- **Coverage, recorded for real:** 50 pages, **91 links discovered**, `stoppedAtLimit: true`, `robotsFound: false`, `sitemapUrls: 0`, `maxPages: 50`. `status_code` is populated on all 50 rows — 49 at 200 and one 404.
- **The 404 is a real finding and item 1 kept it, as designed.** `/home` is linked from the site, returns **404**, and serves the `/contact-us` title — a soft 404. It was crawled and stored with its status rather than aborting the run, which is exactly the root-only rule from #99.
- **`entities.urls` now holds both hosts** — `["https://tartanhq.com", "https://www.tartanhq.com"]` — because the column merges rather than replaces, so this morning's apex value survives beside the origin that answered today. `schema` stays `[]`: still no JSON-LD anywhere.
- My dry run predicted 104 links; production found **91**. The two crawls select a different 50 pages out of a larger site, so the discovered set differs. Both stopped at the limit, which is the finding that matters: **`maxPages: 50` does not cover this customer.**

### A defect the working crawl exposed: `body_text` is not text
- All 50 rows store **exactly 20,000 characters** — one distinct length across the whole table — and `body_html` exactly 40,000. Both are the caps, on every page.
- The stored text begins `<iframe src="https://www.googletagmanager.com/ns.html?id=GTM-..."` followed by inline JavaScript. It is markup and script source, not prose, and it is byte-identical on every page.
- **Mechanism, confirmed against the live page.** `crawlPage.ts:105` takes `contentEl.textContent`, where `contentEl` is `document.querySelector('main, article') ?? document.body`. Two things compound: this Framer site has **no `<main>` or `<article>`** (`hasMain: false`), so it falls back to `document.body`; and `textContent` includes `<script>` and `<noscript>` contents, which `innerText` does not. Measured on `/products/hypersync`: `textContent` **34,485** chars starting with the GTM iframe, `innerText` **6,960** chars starting with the real page — "Platform / Resources / Sign Up / Contact Us / The Integration Layer For Enterprise Operations." The document carries **1,503,154** characters of inline script, so the 20,000-char cap is spent long before any prose.
- **Which of the 200 findings this invalidates.** Real: `meta-description-missing` ×50 (the tag exists with an **empty** `content` attribute — verified on the live page), `schema-missing` ×50, `not-in-sitemap` ×49, `cwv-poor` ×1. Artifacts of the truncation: `weak-eeat` ×48 and `not-answer-first` ×2, both B2 scores computed over GTM script source.
- **The health score is not affected.** §8 defines it as technical-issue-only, and all four technical findings above are real, so **27** is honest.
- **It also weakens the render-wait heuristic.** `contentSignature` at `crawlPage.ts:136` uses the same `textContent`, so the "text length" half of the settle signature is mostly script bytes that arrive early. The anchor-count half still measures what it claims to. This is likely why the same pages measured 3,601–20,000 characters locally and 20,000 everywhere on CI: it was never counting prose.
- Pre-existing, not caused by this week's work. It was invisible while production held one page, and only a crawl that reaches 50 pages makes "every page has identical body text" visible. Raised rather than fixed — `innerText` is the one-line change, but it moves every B2 score on every page, so it wants its own PR and a decision.

## 2026-09-10 (body_text is prose again)
- Branch `fix/body-text-is-prose` off the #103 merge. The defect raised in the previous entry, fixed in both places it lived.
- **Two fixtures before any fix, both red on the current code.** `/script-heavy` is tartanhq.com's shape: no `<main>`, a GTM `<noscript>` iframe and a 25,000-char inline script ahead of the prose. `body_text` came back beginning `<iframe src="https://www.googletagman…` — the production row, reproduced. `/slow-hydration` is a shell with 5,000 chars of inline script that paints two seconds in; the render wait read it with **no headings and no links**, because `textContent` of the shell was never `0:0`, so it settled on the script bytes one second after `load`. That second failure was predicted by the previous entry and is now demonstrated, not inferred.
- **The fix is `innerText` at both sites** — `extractDomSignals` and `contentSignature` in `packages/crawler/src/crawlPage.ts`. `innerText` is what a reader sees: it excludes `<script>`, `<noscript>` and `<style>` source and `display:none` content. The last point is a semantic change worth knowing about — a hidden accordion body or a responsive duplicate section is no longer captured. On a Framer site the duplicate is the common case, so this removes double-counted sentences rather than losing content.
- Considered and rejected: cloning the element and stripping `script/style/noscript` before `textContent`. It keeps hidden content, which on this customer's site means every section twice.
- Crawler suite 66/66; both new tests green after the fix.
- **What should move on the re-crawl:** `body_text` lengths become distinct per page and well under 20,000; `weak-eeat` ×48 and `not-answer-first` ×2 are re-scored over prose and may fire again for real reasons (the site has no bylines or dates, so `weak-eeat` is likely to stay for most pages — but it will be true this time). `meta-description-missing` ×50, `schema-missing` ×50, `not-in-sitemap` ×49 and health 27 should not change.

## 2026-09-10 (UI: the shell, and the craft it was missing)
- Branch `feat/shell-and-craft`, in a worktree at `.worktrees/ui` so the wave-0/1 session's checkout is untouched. Dashboard only: no migration, no API route, no touched file outside `apps/dashboard`.
- **Step 1 of the redesign plan is smaller than written.** Two of its items already landed: `workspace.ts` is the site switcher, and `styles.css` already turns the rail into a bottom bar under 860px. What was left is the rail's eleven items, the names, the theme, the expired session, and the API-connection panel.

Rankings, Entity Graph, Competitors, AI answers and Local SEO become tabs in a new `views/visibility.ts`, which mounts the five views unchanged and adds only the tab strip; `report`, `get-started` and `clients` stay reachable but leave the rail. `ROUTE_ALIASES` sends every old hash (`#/pulse`, `#/serp`, …) to where it now lives.
- **One name per screen.** `SCREEN_NAMES` in `format.ts` now feeds the rail, the breadcrumb and every page's `h1`, so "Findings" in the rail no longer opens a page titled "Technical audit". Twelve views changed their `h1` to read from it. `breadcrumb()` composes "Site / Screen / Tab".
- **Theme is remembered** (`engine.theme`, next to the rail key); a reload no longer drops back to the system setting. **An expired session signs out**: `request` in `api.ts` reads the API's `code: "expired"` on a 401 and calls `signOut()`, instead of every view rendering a banner the customer cannot act on.
- **The API-connection panel is gone**, with `BASE_KEY` and `setApiBaseUrl`. The origin is the build's job and already was; the field only let a customer break it.
- **Craft foundation.** Motion and type tokens (`--ease-out`, `--press`, `--press-ms`, eight `--t-*` steps). A shared press (`scale(.97)` over 100ms) and `:focus-visible` ring on every control — three controls in 946 lines had a ring before. The block sits last in the stylesheet on purpose: a `transition` shorthand declared later replaces an earlier one, so placed at the top it was silently dropped by `.navitem`, `.iconbtn`, `.card-act` and `.frow-act`. Hover rules on the shell's controls gated behind `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`. The `#16a34a`/`#d97706`/`#dc2626` triad replaced by `--good`/`--watch`/`--risk`, so entity scores and impact pills finally change in dark mode. `.field` moved off mono, which had diluted mono's "this is a readout" signal.
- **Three defects found only at 390px**, all fixed: the active Visibility tab could sit past the right edge with nothing marked on screen (the strip's own `scrollLeft`, not `scrollIntoView`, which walks every scrollable ancestor); the breadcrumb wrapped to a second line beside the site chip that already carried the same context, so it is hidden under 860px; bottom-bar items and tabs were 32–33px tall, now 44.
- **Not done here, deliberately:** the `accounts.kind` migration and `POST /accounts` from step 1 (a migration would collide with the wave-1 session's numbering — the agency case is derived from the account list until it lands); the sign-in screen's own palette; per-screen type sizes and skeletons; Home itself, which is step 2.
- Verified: `npx turbo run test --force` 39/39; the dashboard's test, lint and build; and a browser walk of Home, Findings, Visibility (all five tabs), Settings in both themes at 1440 and 390px, with keyboard focus confirmed as a 2px accent ring on `:focus-visible`.

- **#104 merged** (`8ce41a0`); tip verified in `origin/main` by `merge-base`; the main CI run had `build`, `migrate`, `deploy-api`, `deploy` all green. The crawler itself needs no deploy — `crawl.yml` builds it from `main` on every pass.
- **The site's real size, measured, not assumed.** A local dry run of `www.tartanhq.com` with `maxPages: 500` (crawlSite only, nothing reported to the API) reached **198 pages** from 199 discovered links, `stoppedAtLimit: false`, in 712 s (3.6 s/page from a laptop; the runner measured 6.9 s/page). 191 answered 200 and **7 answered 404** — production's one `/home` soft-404 is one of seven broken internal links. By section: blog 123, case-study 19, products 9, industries 8, product-update 8, and 8 single pages. So `maxPages: 50` covered one quarter of this customer.
- `body_text` on the dry run: 148 distinct lengths across 198 pages, min 30, max 20,000. Distinct per page now; the pages still at the cap are long blog posts, which is the cap doing its job.
- **The plan's cost framing is wrong.** `validate.ts` and the plan say `maxPages` is bounded because "a runner spends real minutes per page", and the decision was called a cost decision. The repository is **public**, so GitHub Actions minutes are not billed at all. The real costs are wall-clock (about 7 s/page on the runner), the `crawl-queue` concurrency group serializing every other customer's audit behind a long crawl, and Neon storage at up to 60 KB per row. Decision on the default pending with the user.
